- 2026-07-14 Lesson: our own research pass reported the SX record sizes as 14/30/31; they are 12/28/29 (upstream's checks are literally nCount*(4+2+4+2)[+16][+1]). Because SX1 resolves the record version BY PACKET SIZE, shipping the wrong table would have made padMule reject every real source-exchange answer. A byte-exact test caught it in minutes. Recorded in [[decisions-and-lessons]]: agent-derived constants are a HYPOTHESIS until a test pins them against real bytes - always assert the byte layout, never just the field list.

- 2026-07-14 Wave 5 research: 4 parallel source-grounded agents over eMule 0.50a (WIRE AUTHORITY) + aMule 3.0.1 -> byte-exact spec docs/raw/wave5-crypto-research-2026-07-14.md. Covers TCP obfuscation (EncryptedStreamSocket: initiator handshake [semi-random marker | u32 randomkey | RC4(SYNC 0x835E6FC4 | methods | padlen | pad)], key = MD5(target_userhash[16] | magic{34 req->resp / 203 resp->req} | randomkey[4]) -> RC4 drop 1024; receiver auto-detects via byte[0] in {0xE3,0xD4,0xC5}=plaintext), UDP obfuscation (8-byte / Kad 16-byte header, MAGICVALUE_UDP_SYNC_CLIENT 0x395F2EC1, no RC4 discard), secure ident (RSA 384-bit PKCS1v15-SHA1, 48-byte sig, cryptkey.dat = base64(DER private key); sign the OTHER peer's pubkey || challenge; OP_PUBLICKEY 0x85 / OP_SIGNATURE 0x86 / OP_SECIDENTSTATE 0x87), and negotiation (MISCOPTIONS2 bits 7/8/9 supported/requested/required; gate: obfuscate outgoing when peer supports+we support AND peer-requests-or-we-request). aMule byte-identical on the wire. Slices: 5a TCP obf, 5b UDP obf, 5c secure ident, 5d wiring + obfuscated differential gate vs amuled (also unblocks the uploader-pull test). Advertise support + accept both + obfuscate on request.
- 2026-07-14 MILESTONE - Wave 5a TCP OBFUSCATION VALIDATED vs real amuled 3.0.1. Implemented RC4 (mule-proto/rc4.rs, pinned to standard vectors) + obfuscation key derivation (obf.rs: MD5(target_userhash | magic{34 requester / 203 responder} | randomkey[4 LE]) -> RC4 drop 1024) + initiator handshake byte layout + FramedStream optional-cipher integration (decrypt reads / encrypt writes in order) + obf_handshake.rs (obf_initiate / obf_accept auto-detect) + connect_peer_obf / accept_peer(auto-detect). padMule completed the full RC4 handshake with amuled and downloaded single-part (600KB) AND multi-part (15MB, hashset over the encrypted stream) byte-for-byte. LANDMINE (differential test caught it): the obfuscation key uses the peer's MARKED userhash (byte[5]=14, byte[14]=111 forced - the hello/source-exchange hash), NOT amuled's raw preferences.dat hash (differs at exactly those 2 bytes); wrong hash = silent SYNC-check failure. Recorded in [[decisions-and-lessons]] + memory. 198 tests. Remaining Wave 5: 5b UDP obf (with Kad/Wave 6), 5c secure ident RSA, 5d server-connect obf + gating + amuled uploader-pull (now unblocked).
- 2026-07-14 Wave 5c secure identification (RSA) DONE + interop-validated vs real amuled. mule-engine/secure_ident.rs: RSA-384 PKCS1v15-SHA1 (48-byte sig), Identity keygen + cryptkey.dat, sign_v1 (signs PEER pubkey || challenge u32 LE) / verify_v1 (mirror: our pubkey || challenge-we-issued, checked with peer key), OP_PUBLICKEY 0x85 / OP_SECIDENTSTATE 0x87 / OP_SIGNATURE 0x86. LANDMINE: aMule cryptkey.dat is base64(PKCS#8 PrivateKeyInfo), NOT raw PKCS#1 - Crypto++ InvertibleRSAFunction::DEREncode emits the PKCS#8 wrapper (AlgorithmIdentifier + rsaEncryption OID). The wire PUBLIC key stays PKCS#1 RSAPublicKey (~58 bytes, < 80 cap). Integration test (tests/secure_ident_interop.rs) loads a REAL amuled cryptkey.dat fixture and uses the key - proves our RSA/DER matches Crypto++. 210 tests. New deps rsa/sha1/base64/rand. Remaining Wave 5: 5b UDP obf (Wave 6/Kad), 5d live secure-ident state machine + credit tie-in + server-connect obf + gating.
- 2026-07-14 MILESTONE - Wave 5d secure-ident live state machine VALIDATED vs real amuled 3.0.1. SecureIdentSession (mule-engine/secure_ident.rs) drives the mutual RSA challenge/response mirroring aMule (BaseClient.cpp handlers + ClientTCPSocket.cpp:1249 dispatch): KEYANDSIGNEEDED sends pubkey then defers signature until peer's key arrives. mule-cli sec-ident. padMule VERIFIED real amuled's signature ("peer PASSED secure identification") and completed its half. LANDMINE #2 (differential caught it, after the cryptkey PKCS#8 one): the ON-WIRE public key (OP_PUBLICKEY 0x85) is SubjectPublicKeyInfo (X.509 SPKI, 76 bytes, AlgorithmIdentifier + rsaEncryption OID), NOT the ~58-byte raw PKCS#1 RSAPublicKey the spec claimed - PKCS#1 makes verification silently fail. So: cryptkey.dat=PKCS#8, wire pubkey=SPKI, PKCS#1 nowhere on the wire. HARNESS GOTCHA: aMule remembers a userhash's pubkey across connections (sends SIGNATURENEEDED not KEYANDSIGNEEDED); a fresh Identity needs a fresh peer clients.met. 212 tests. Both Wave-5 crypto subsystems (obfuscation + secure ident) now fully interoperate with genuine aMule. Remaining 5d: credit tie-in, server-connect obf, gating, transfer-session integration, uploader-pull.
- 2026-07-14 Wave 5d policy layer DONE. (1) Credit tie-in (credits.rs): IdentState enum + resolve_ident_state (verified identity bound to its IP; same userhash from another IP = IdBadGuy, per ClientCredits.cpp:219-231) + score_ratio_ident (denies the credit bonus to a key-bearing peer that is unverified/failed/replaying, only when WE have crypto; keyless + verified peers get the normal ratio). This is what makes secure ident MEAN something - a stolen userhash cannot inherit credits. (2) Obfuscation gating (crypt_policy.rs): should_obfuscate_outbound (need peer hash + both support + peer-requests-or-we-request) + should_reject (hard requirement mismatch), default prefs match amuled (supported+requested, not required). (3) serve-file now auto-detects obfuscation (obf_accept) so padMule serves both plaintext and obfuscated downloaders. 221 tests. UPLOADER-PULL vs amuled: serve side obf-aware + proven P2P + amuled reaches it, but the full amuled-downloads-from-padMule run stays blocked by amuled's unreliable OFFLINE link-source dialer (queues the download, does not reliably dial the link source) - amuled orchestration, not padMule. REMAINING 5d (integration, lower-risk): secure-ident + gating driven during a live transfer, server-connect DH obfuscation.
- 2026-07-14 Wave 5d live-transfer secure-ident wiring DONE. run_secure_ident (mule-engine/secure_ident.rs) drives the mutual RSA exchange as a bounded phase right after the hello, before any file request. Composes cleanly with the transfer: each side sends its signature before any transfer packet and completes on receiving the peer's, so the phase returns before transfer data flows (no interleaving, no dropped packets - traced + tested). Integration test: two engines do hello -> mutual secure ident -> file transfer on ONE connection; both verify each other AND the file transfers byte-for-byte. 222 tests. Closes the secure-ident wiring (the exchange validated standalone vs amuled now sits inline with a real transfer).

- 2026-07-14 Wave 6a STARTED + real-fixture-validated. mule_proto::Kad128 (CUInt128): 4 u32 words MSW-first, XOR distance, MSB-first bit(), 2^120 tolerance; CANONICAL from_hash/to_hash kept STRICTLY separate from the raw wire/disk from_wire/to_wire (each dword byte-reversed: H3 H2 H1 H0 | H7 H6 H5 H4 | ... - reading raw hash as a Kad ID targets the wrong node, the classic Kad bug). mule_files::nodes_dat byte-compat reader/writer (v2 header 0|version|count + 34-byte records: KadID 16 + IP 4 + UDP 2 + TCP 2 + version 1 + UDPkey 8 + verified 1). Proves our CUInt128 wire encoding matches the real network. 240 tests. REMAINING 6a: routing bin-tree + k-buckets.
- 2026-07-15 Wave 6a COMPLETE. New crate mule-kad with the Kad routing table (RoutingTable): binary tree of zones, each leaf a bin of K=10 contacts; descend by XOR-distance bit (bit 0 = closer half); split only if shallow (level<KBASE=4) or near self (index<KK=5) and not past MAXLEVELS=127 (eMule CanSplit); full unsplittable far bin drops the contact (caps far capacity). closest_to(target,n) = the n nearest by XOR (the lookup candidate set). Test loads the real 179-contact nodes.dat -> retains ~142 (rest overflow far bins, correct since saved vs a different self_id), tree splits beyond one bin, closest-lookups XOR-sorted. 240 tests. Next 6b: Kad UDP framing (0xE4/0xE5) + obfuscation (5b, 16-B overhead, no RC4 discard) + bootstrap(empty req)/hello handshake; differential gate = talk to a real Kad bootstrap node.
- 2026-07-15 Wave 6b codecs DONE (source-verified + adversarial-reviewed). mule-kad gains frame.rs / udp_obf.rs / message.rs for the Kad UDP wire. Every layout verified against amule-3.0.1 source (EncryptedDatagramSocket.cpp, KademliaUDPListener.cpp SendMyDetails/AddContact2/Bootstrap, SafeFile.cpp WriteTag, Tag.h CTagVarInt), not the research summary. KEY FACT: aMule ENDIAN_SWAP_32 == wxUINT32_SWAP_ON_BE == no-op on our LE target, so the sentinel (0x395F2EC1) and both 4-byte verify keys are plain little-endian on the wire == our to_le_bytes(). 3 bugs found+fixed: (1) is_protocol_byte used the TCP set {E3,C5,D4,E4,E5,F4,F5}; correct UDP set is {C5,D4,E4,E5,A3,B2} - old set dropped inbound 0xF4-marked Kad packets and could emit 0xB2 that a real aMule drops; (2) deobfuscate must reject a zero-payload Kad packet (eMule `if result<=8`) BEFORE learning verify keys, not after; (3) pack threshold is PAYLOAD>200 (GetPacketSize excludes the opcode/header), not frame>200 - was an off-by-2 over-compressing at payload 199/200 [caught by the adversarial-review workflow]. Refuted (correctly): flate2/miniz_oxide != aMule zlib byte-for-byte, but both inflate identically -> interop-safe, not a wire defect (only matters for a future outbound-byte-exact oracle compare). 264 tests. Next 6c iterative FIND_NODE, then the live UDP gate.

- 2026-07-15 INVESTIGATED eMule 0.70b community fork (C:\Users\ajbuf\Downloads\eMule0.70b-Sources.zip; genuine, copyright->2024, changelog to Aug 2024). CONCLUSION: it is a wire-COMPATIBLE continuation of eMule 0.50a - it makes NO wire-protocol/opcode/tag-format changes over 0.50a (grep of the community-era delta lines 6-1206 for protocol/wire/opcode/handshake/version-byte/backwards-compat = EMPTY; the only "sourceexchange protocol change" is line 2379, PRE-0.50a, already in our base). This confirms padMule's wire fidelity: nothing interop-critical is missing, and the network (mostly 0.50a-derived clients) is exactly what padMule already speaks. The two scary-sounding protocol fixes CONFIRM padMule is already correct: (a) "ED2K hash regression if file length is a multiple of part size" (Aug 2024) = eMule re-fixing a regression THEY introduced post-0.50a; padMule's ed2k_hash already does the canonical empty-MD4-sentinel thing (part_count=size/PARTSIZE+1). (b) "KAD tag count was incorrect" = a 0.51c (2019) regression eMule introduced+fixed; padMule follows 0.50a and never had it. ADOPTABLE VALUE (optional, all non-wire): Kad robustness/anti-abuse hardening (anti-flood ignore+ban, drop UDP from port 0, drop publish w/ unreachable buddy IP, remove routing-zone flooders, allow small ping variation in packet tracking, more unrequested-packet sanitizing), regenerate userhash if zero/lost (padMule already always-generates), and FEATURES: magnet-link parsing (name/len/hash/AICH/sources), ed2k://|search|term|/ link type, "Automatic" search-method selection (server vs Kad by connectivity - padMule now has both), and the "try obfuscation once for a server with no obf data" fallback. Extracted at scratchpad/emule070b. Use as a reference (like amule-master) if/when adding those. Files also present: eMule0.50a-Sources.zip (wire SOT), amule-master.zip.

- 2026-07-15 INTELLIGENT TRANSFER ENGINE (go-beyond-0.70b, all client-side / zero wire change - the safe side of the replicate-wire/improve-internals line). Three pieces: (1) RAREST-FIRST - Download aggregates per-part swarm availability from every peer's OP_FILESTATUS (note_status); PartFile::next_blocks gained a rarity fn + orders wanted parts least-available-first (stable over the (missing,part) tie-break). (2) ENDGAME - next_blocks gained an endgame flag (ignore reservations); take_blocks enters it within ~4 blocks of complete so several peers race the tail, fixing the single-queuing-source last-block stall we hit live. (3) PEER SCORING - run_peer/download_from_peer now RETURN bytes delivered; fetch::download_file keeps a PeerScoreboard and orders each sweep best-first (proven deliverers > untried > proven failures; connect-fails penalized by FAIL_PENALTY=1MB). Same OP_REQUESTPARTS bytes on the wire - only WHOM/WHAT we ask changes. 5 new tests (rarest-first, endgame, scoreboard) + the loopback multi-peer download still completes+verifies. Live search-download: clean login/search/connect (no regression); 0 bytes that run because the one connectable source dropped us (network variance, not code - a prior run pulled 1.78MB). This is exactly the strategic direction: shine via client intelligence, never touch the wire. 327 tests. Design note in the "beyond community" discussion: next candidates are trust/fake-detection + search ranking (client-side) and, with adversarial peer validation, an optional padMule-to-padMule enhancement channel on a provably-ignored tag.

Live Your Life.WAV" (5888780 B, df13c362...), "ESAMI URINE .pdf" (4232 B, 40b3fc7a...). Content uncontrolled -> only name/size/hash recorded. Driver: mule-cli fetch-complete <server.met> <keyword> <out> [max] [min]. LOWID CALLBACK NOW PROVEN LIVE: wav + pdf were both delivered by firewalled LowID sources via OP_CALLBACKREQUEST (we bind a listener during login to earn HighID -> the LowID peer dials us back -> streams the file; the wav's whole 5.9 MB arrived this way). Completion is a source-availability HUNT; the wins were all client-side (zero wire change): (1) queue fast-bail - run_peer returns TransferError::Queued the instant a source sends OP_QUEUERANKING, turning a 25s dead-end into ~2s; (2) diversity-aware sweep - a keyword can be saturated by ONE sharer's collection ("wav" = 200 results ALL from one IP's Age-of-Empires game sounds), so skip files whose only source is an already-stalled IP; (3) min_size to escape a saturated keyword (>=800KB surfaced diverse real audio the game sounds had crowded out); (4) progress-aware callback wait + per-candidate part dirs - wait WHILE bytes flow (patient before first byte, bail after progress stalls), and give each candidate its own dir so a slow callback isn't clobbered by the next candidate (an earlier run delivered a full 5.9MB file then LOST it to a shared 001.part); (5) size-adaptive config (tiny=12s/1 round sweep, larger=40s/5 rounds). Files: catalog.rs (dedup/rank/trust), mule-cli cmd_fetch_complete, multi_source.rs (fast-bail), transfer_session.rs (Queued). 331 tests, fmt+clippy clean, ASCII-only. Recorded in [[build-progress]] + [[decisions-and-lessons]]. Marks tasks: search-intelligence + trust delivered via catalog.rs; LowID callback done. Wire was already correct (Wave 4 differential) - this was purely HOW we pick files and spend time.

- 2026-07-16 ENHANCEMENT CHANNEL Layer 1 (task #31, the highest-compat-risk feature) DONE + adversarially validated. Carrier chosen from SOURCE-GROUNDED recon (Explore agent over aMule 3.0.1 + eMule 0.50a): a HELLO taglist entry with an unknown NAME but a STANDARD type byte is read-and-skipped by both stock clients (aMule hello switch has no default BaseClient.cpp:477-478,635; eMule benign default 585-592); a NONSTANDARD type byte is unsafe (aMule THROWS->disconnect Tag.cpp:179; eMule DESYNCS packets.cpp:565-572); unknown TCP opcode on 0xE3/0xC5 is also ignored but a novel proto byte disconnects; Kad carriers are RISKY (throw->dropped on UDP). Implementation (peer.rs): string-named "padMule" UINT32 marker = <caps:u24><version:u8> in every HELLO/HELLOANSWER (tag count 7->8); ParsedHello::padmule()->Option<PadMuleInfo>; PADMULE_CHANNEL_VERSION=1, caps=0 (advertise nothing we don't honour). 4 unit tests. Two-layer design: Layer 1 detection (passive, must be ignored - it is) + Layer 2 negotiation (custom 0xC5 opcode, only ever sent to a CONFIRMED padMule so stock never sees it) - Layer 2 + NAT-traversal enhancement designed, not built. ADVERSARIAL GATE: amuled differential test passes with the marker in every hello (real aMule serves all 3 files byte-for-byte). That same run caught a REGRESSION in my own queue fast-bail: it was unconditional, so single-source peer-download aborted when amuled rationed slots (2nd/3rd files failed). Fixed by making fast-bail a policy - download_from_peer(.., bail_on_queue): hunt bails, single dedicated source waits. 335 tests, fmt+clippy clean, ASCII-only. New entry [[padmule-enhancement-channel]]; lesson in [[decisions-and-lessons]] (the differential test catches your own optimizations too).
- 2026-07-16 RESEARCH for task #32 (Layer 2 + NAT traversal) - 3 parallel source/web agents, synthesized into design specs. (1) LAYER 2 CARRIER (source, eMule 0.50a + aMule): a 0xC5 extended packet is [0xC5][u32 len LE][opcode][payload], drained by the SIZE field (opcode never consulted during framing: EMSocket.cpp:367-425 / 199-266), so an unknown 0xC5 opcode with arbitrary payload does NOT desync a stock peer - dropped at the switch default, no disconnect (ListenSocket.cpp:2075-2078 / ClientTCPSocket.cpp:1777-1780). Safe opcode = 0xD8 (0xC0-0xDF dead zone, far above eMule's 0xB2 frontier; NOT 0xB3+ where stock grows next). Packable via 0xD4 (opcode survives; keep decompressed < 50000). Only sent after the hello handshake (client object exists). Spec in [[padmule-enhancement-channel]]. (2) EXISTING NAT MACHINERY (source): CONFIRMED no hole punching anywhere - CanDoCallback (amule.cpp:2142-2189) hard-refuses both-firewalled; every callback ends in a TCP dial FROM the firewalled callee. Reusable rendezvous primitives: Kad key-mailbox (firewalled peers CAN publish/search), STUN-equivalent (KADEMLIA2_PING/PONG observed port + FIREWALLED_RES observed IP + UDP firewall tester intern/extern), buddy relay pattern (needs a custom Layer-2 opcode for arbitrary bytes). (3) NAT TRAVERSAL SOTA (web): cone x cone punches (~70-80% direct, libp2p DCUtR 2025 ~70%+-7%), symmetric x symmetric needs relay; borrow DCUtR (RTT/half-RTT synced punch) + BEP-55 (rendezvous via mutual peer); transport = quinn QUIC over the punched socket (pure-Rust QUIC layer, TLS 1.3, keep_alive for NAT upkeep; punch raw UDP first then bring up QUIC); iOS UDP works, foreground-only -> re-punch on resume. Full design + phased plan (Phase 0 discovery -> 1 MVP buddy+punch+QUIC -> 2 Kad rendezvous -> 3 robustness -> 4 relay fallback) + risks (adoption bootstrap, symmetric/CGNAT, iOS lifecycle) in new [[nat-traversal-design]]. Next: implement Layer 2 (opcode 0xD8) + a first message, adversarially re-validate vs amuled, then Phase 0/1 of traversal.
- 2026-07-16 DECISION (Anthony): mass-adoption goal -> DEFER Layer 2 + NAT traversal, keep Layer 1. Rationale: every channel enhancement works only padMule<->padMule, so value is 100% adoption-gated; on today's ~all-stock network Layer 2 never fires. Chicken-and-egg: get peers by shipping a good client, not by pre-building peer-to-peer features (a "no speculative features" call). KEEP Layer 1 (deployed marker accrues recognition for free) + the research (de-risks Layer 2/NAT traversal to a few days of ready work). Near-term priority redirected to ADOPTION DRIVERS, all single-user-valuable day one: Wave 8 iPad app (the whole value prop - no aMule on iPad), on-device HighID (UPnP/NAT-PMP), and the already-landing fetch/search/trust quality. Task #32 parked. Recorded in [[decisions-and-lessons]]; status notes added to [[padmule-enhancement-channel]] + [[nat-traversal-design]].

Minimal HTTP/1.1 + XML tag extraction over tokio sockets (iOS-safe, no new deps), same style as link.rs. mule-cli `upnp <port>`. 10 tests: pure helpers (URL split, xml_tag namespace-insensitive, resolve relative controlURL, parse_wan_service picks WANIP over other services, SOAP body shape, HTTP status/header parse) + a LOOPBACK INTEGRATION test driving http_get+parse_wan_service+soap_add_mapping+external_ip against a mock IGD over a real socket (validates HTTP/SOAP framing end-to-end). Live vs the real gateway: NO SSDP response, but an independent raw-Python M-SEARCH also got nothing -> ENVIRONMENT limit (WSL2 multicast egress and/or Xfinity UPnP off), not our code; should work on a real iPad on a UPnP home net. HighID now has both NAT-PMP + UPnP. Decision context: chosen as an ADOPTION driver (single-user-valuable) after deferring the enhancement channel Layer 2 - see [[decisions-and-lessons]]. Next: mule-ffi (UniFFI) seam.
- 2026-07-16 FFI SEAM DONE (mule-ffi, Wave 8a - toward the iPad app). New crate crates/mule-ffi (lib + cdylib + staticlib) is the UniFFI seam between the Rust engine and the native SwiftUI shell. MuleEngine wraps mule_engine::Engine in an FFI-friendly facade: opaque [u8;16] hashes -> hex Strings, EngineState/EngineEvent -> #[uniffi::Enum], IdentityInfo/DownloadInfo #[uniffi::Record], FfiError. The engine's async &mut self lifecycle runs on an internal tokio runtime (block_on) so exported methods are simple + synchronous: new(config_dir) constructor + start/pause/resume/shutdown/state/identity/kad_contacts/downloads/drain_events (events are polled, not callback-pushed - a deliberate MVP simplification). uniffi 0.28 proc-macro (setup_scaffolding!, no UDL); a uniffi-bindgen bin (uniffi::uniffi_bindgen_main, "cli" feature) generates bindings from the compiled cdylib. VALIDATED on this box: crate builds, a Rust-side test drives the full lifecycle + reads events through the FFI types, and `uniffi-bindgen generate --library target/debug/libmule_ffi.so --language swift` emits mule_ffi.swift (open class MuleEngine + all records/enums/error). Added a small Download::name() accessor for DownloadInfo. On-device (link staticlib + import mule_ffi.swift in the SwiftUI shell) waits for an Apple toolchain. Chosen as an adoption driver after deferring the enhancement-channel Layer 2. 346 tests, fmt+clippy clean, ASCII. - 2026-07-16 ENGINE WIRED LIVE BEHIND THE UI. Engine::start() now brings the real network up (was: load state only). LIVE-VERIFIED from an empty config dir = exactly a fresh install: fetch lists -> Kad 133 contacts -> open port -> **HighID** login to a real server -> Kad bootstrap (21 live contacts) -> State(Running)/Status("Connected"). (1) FRESH-INSTALL GAP FOUND + CLOSED: nothing fetched server.met/nodes.dat (hand-placed on the dev box), so a real install knew no servers and no Kad contacts and could reach NOTHING. New bootstrap.rs fetches both from upd.emule-security.org - fetch not bundle, because a bundled list ROTS (see the 2026-07-13 stale-list failure). Hand-rolled HTTP: byte-safe (these are BINARY; UTF-8 decode corrupts them) and on a RAW SOCKET, which sidesteps iOS ATS entirely (ATS governs URLSession/CFNetwork, not BSD sockets) -> cleartext http:// works on-device with no Info.plist exemption. Best-effort + validated (an HTML error page must never be saved as a server list); failure = come up offline, never crash. Live-checked: server.met 1281 B, nodes.dat 5928 B. (2) HIGHID LANDMINE: order matters - the listener must bind BEFORE login, because the server decides HighID/LowID by connecting back to the advertised port. First live run gave **LowID**; adding start_listener() before connect_server() flipped the identical code to **HighID**. The server's probe is a bare TCP connect+close so merely ACCEPTING passes it. map_port() also tries UPnP so a real device with no router rule can earn HighID. (3) pause() drops the Kad UDP socket + aborts the listener (frees 4662); resume() rebinds the port FIRST, re-runs the handshake, re-bootstraps Kad - correct across an IP change. online_status() is HONEST - never claims Connected when it isn't (the old resume() emitted a hardcoded "Connected"; the test now pins the honesty instead). (4) TESTING: unit tests must never touch the network -> Engine::set_offline(true); engine tests went 2.67s -> 0.05s proving they no longer dial out. Real proof = engine::live::fresh_install_goes_online_and_bootstraps_kad (#[ignore]d). 352 tests, fmt+clippy clean, ASCII. REMAINING: expose search + add-download through the FFI facade (the engine already does both).

I first attributed it to a never-device-activated Apple ID - a REAL documented trigger, but WRONG here: Anthony corrected me that he used his PRIMARY, long-activated ID. Corrected in the entry; recording the miss because I reached for the first plausible cause the web offered and fitted it to the symptom instead of first checking it fit HIM. - 2026-07-16 **padMule RAN ON THE IPAD** (Wave 8 milestone, sideload leg PROVEN). Sideloadly worked where AltServer died on -22411, so the de-risk step is done: State running, Status Connected, Kad 21 -> 158 contacts, identity rows populated - the Rust eD2k/Kad port live on the target device, on the real network, built with no Apple hardware anywhere in the chain. Anthony's screenshots were the entire diagnostic and they earned two findings. (1) **AN EVENT IS NOT STATE**: the HighID/LowID answer was computed at login (connect_server) and emitted as a one-shot `Server("Connected to <addr> (HighID)")`, then `start()` emitted `Status("Connected")` right after - both land in the SAME 1s drainEvents() batch, EngineModel applies them in order, so the honest line was clobbered before a single frame rendered. The most important diagnostic on a device with no debugger was computed and thrown away. FIXED: new `ServerInfo {addr, low_id}` is durable engine state, `online_status()` carries the ID type, `server_info()` is gated on `is_online()` (no server -> no claim), resume() re-records low_id because it can FLIP across an IP change, and the UI polls it as a SNAPSHOT into its own colour-coded row. 2 new tests pin the honesty gate; 354 tests green. LESSON: anything the UI must KEEP showing has to be readable at any time, not announced once. (2) **UPnP IS DEAD ON iOS** - the "find devices on local networks" prompt was `upnp::discover()` firing SSDP M-SEARCH at MULTICAST 239.255.255.250. Blocked twice over: `NSLocalNetworkUsageDescription` was missing from our Info.plist and without it iOS 14+ SILENTLY DROPS every LAN packet (no error at all - developer.apple.com/forums/thread/661606); and multicast on real hardware needs the RESTRICTED `com.apple.developer.networking.multicast` entitlement, which requires Apple approval and is UNREACHABLE for a free-signed sideloaded app. Added the plist key (it gates the unicast paths too), but the entitlement is a hard wall => on-device HighID needs UNICAST port mapping: `portmap.rs` (NAT-PMP, unicast gateway:5351) is ALREADY BUILT but never wired into `map_port()`, which only tries UPnP. Not urgent: this box forwards 4662/4672 to the WINDOWS host not the iPad, so the iPad is LowID regardless, and LowID is survivable (the live wav + pdf both arrived by callback). (3) TWO IP LEAKS ONTO THE SCREEN, caught and closed: map_port() emitted the gateway-reported PUBLIC IP into a UI event, and the login event embedded the client id which ENCODES the public IP on HighID - the exact value scrubbed from the public repo. Both removed; ServerInfo carries low_id ONLY, by design, because this screen gets screenshotted. [[mac-toolchain-setup]] gained a "what the first real device run taught us" section.
- 2026-07-16 **SEARCH + DOWNLOAD REACH THE IPAD** (the app stops being a status screen). The engine could already do both (it fetched the three live files), they were just not exposed through the FFI. What the wiring exposed: (1) **ServerLink owned the socket but could not TALK** - only connect/pause/resume/disconnect; nobody ever read from `conn` after login, and mule-cli bypassed ServerLink entirely to hand-roll the stream. Added `request(pkt, want, wait)` + search/get_sources/request_callback. Unsolicited server chatter seen while waiting for a reply is FORWARDED, not dropped - extracted `classify_server_packet` out of login_handshake so the same mapping serves both (a server interleaves MOTD/status/serverlist with replies for the whole connection, not just during login). (2) **VERIFICATION GAP FOUND**: `download_file` NEVER calls `verify_ready_parts`, so `is_complete()` means "all bytes arrived", NOT "verified" - and a SINGLE-part file (<PARTSIZE) has no part hashes at all, so nothing checks it (the documented multi_source.rs:333 landmine). => finish_download now re-derives the WHOLE-FILE ed2k hash and refuses to save on mismatch. This is the only thing between corrupt bytes and the user's Files app. (3) **New `ed2k_hash_parts`** in mule-proto: mule-cli verifies by `parts.concat()` - the whole file in RAM, fine on a 32GB dev box, not for a big file on an iPad. The hash is structurally part-wise, so it streams. Pinned against `ed2k_hash` at every boundary (0, 1, PARTSIZE-1, PARTSIZE, +1, 2*PARTSIZE, +1) and asserted never to read the trailing sentinel part - the part_count(floor+1)-vs-data_part_count(ceil) landmine, now handled in ONE place. (4) **Attacker-controlled filenames**: P2P names are hostile input; `safe_filename` collapses them to one inert path component (tested: "../../Library/Preferences/evil" -> "_.._Library_Preferences_evil", asserted to join INSIDE the parent) and `unique_dest` never overwrites an existing file. (5) **LowID cannot ask for callbacks** - a LowID asking a LowID to call back is the one case eD2k cannot route, so callbacks are only requested when WE are HighID (uses the new ServerInfo). (6) The **listener was dropping every inbound peer** after the handshake - it now routes them into an unfinished download (the shared Arc<Mutex<Vec<Arc<Download>>>>), which is what makes a callback deliver. (7) **Downloads land in Documents** + UIFileSharingEnabled + LSSupportsOpeningDocumentsInPlace, so they show in the Files app: Application Support is invisible, and a file the user cannot open is not a finished download. (8) **THE SAME EVENT-CLOBBERS-STATE BUG AGAIN**: `.server` events were writing to `status`, which would have wiped the polled "Connected to <server> (HighID|LowID)" line the moment anything said "Saved 'x'". Server events now feed the transient `notice`; `status` stays the connection state. Watch for this shape - it has now bitten twice. Also fixed: map_port()'s doc claimed it "tries NAT-PMP and UPnP" while the body only ever tried UPnP. 363 tests, clippy clean, ASCII.
- 2026-07-17 **THE FULL DOWNLOAD LOOP RAN ON THE IPAD** (Wave 8 - the app is now a real client, not a demo). From the SwiftUI app on the device: Anthony typed `test`, the connected server (45.87.41.166, LowID, 162 Kad) returned a full page of results with sizes + source counts, he tapped Get, and the engine downloaded a 168KB .doc from a SINGLE source, verified the whole-file ed2k hash, saved it to Documents, and it opened in the Files app under "On My iPad > padMule". Banner trail across his screenshots: "Downloading '...'" -> "Saved '...'" -> the file visible + readable in Files. FIRST end-to-end search->download->verify->save->open done entirely on-device. Finding: **LowID did NOT block it** - the sole source was HighID and reachable, so a LowID client is only walled off from files whose ONLY sources are themselves LowID, not from the network at large; downloads work today, and HighID is a throughput/reach improvement, not a prerequisite. INSTALL LESSON (cost a cycle): the previous re-sideload looked like "the exact same thing as before" because an older/cached .ipa got installed - the search feature was verifiably in the CI Mach-O the whole time. New standing workflow at Anthony's request ("just download it for me from GitHub from now on"): fetch with `gh run download <id> -R ajbufort/padMule -n padMule-ipa`, PROVE the feature is in the binary (`strings padMule | grep "Search the eD2k network"`), stage into /mnt/c/Users/ajbuf/Downloads/ with the run id IN the filename, and delete the old app off the iPad before reinstalling - never point him at the Actions page (many identically-named artifacts). Updated [[padmule-ios-app-path]] + [[padmule-live-downloads]] memory; content policy held (name/size only, no contents). Next candidates, Anthony to pick: on-device HighID via unicast SSDP-at-the-gateway, Kad keyword search, upload/serve (stop being a pure leech), or a cancel/delete control.
- 2026-07-17 **UPLOAD / SERVE + LEECH MODE** (padMule stops being a pure leech; feature 1 of 4 Anthony greenlit). New `share.rs`: a `SharedFile` is a COMPLETE file on disk we will serve; `serve_shared` answers the eD2k upload sequence (filename -> status -> hashset -> slot -> parts), reading each requested block off disk so a large file is never held in RAM. The inbound listener now tells a LEECHER (sends OP_REQUESTFILENAME first) from a called-back LowID SOURCE (stays silent, waiting for us to drive one of OUR downloads) by PEEKING the first packet with a 3s timeout - cancel-safe because `framed::read_packet`'s only await is the socket read and bytes are appended to its buffer synchronously after, so a stalled first read buffers nothing and loses nothing. `finish_download` seeds each verified file into the shared library (capturing the hashset before `into_store` consumes it); `serve_inbound` gates on the sharing switch + an 8-slot concurrency cap - a peer that finds us leeching or full gets OP_FILEREQANSNOFIL and moves on, not a hang. Engine `set_sharing`/`is_sharing` is an `AtomicBool` the listener reads lock-free -> FFI `setSharing`/`isSharing` -> a SwiftUI "Share uploads" toggle polled as a SNAPSHOT (same "an event is not state" discipline as the server login). Flipping it OFF is the private-use "Leech Mode": a NEUTRAL pause-uploads control (the mechanism Anthony chose), not a separate untracked feature - because a CI-built .ipa can only carry committed code, a literally-untracked button could never reach the device. Only COMPLETE files are shared for now; serving in-progress parts (range reads out of a live `.part` + a read-side availability bitfield) is the next step, noted in the module docs. 6 new tests (serve a held file single- and multi-part-with-hashset, refuse an unheld hash, Leech Mode refuses a file we DO hold, sharing-on serves through `serve_inbound`). 368 workspace tests, clippy + fmt + ASCII clean. Updated [[padmule-ios-app-path]].
- 2026-07-17 **LICENSE + NOTICE at the repo root** (GPL compliance for the public derivative). Anthony asked whether vendoring the aMule codebase is inappropriate. It is NOT: aMule 3.0.1 is GPL-2.0-or-later, and the GPL PERMITS redistributing the source in a fork/port as long as the license + notices travel with it and the derivative is GPL too - `amule-3.0.1/LICENSE.md` + `docs/COPYING` + `docs/AUTHORS.md` are preserved intact, and the tree is our documented reference oracle. The REAL gap he surfaced: padMule had NO top-level license of its own. Added `./LICENSE` (GPL v2 text, copied verbatim from `amule-3.0.1/docs/COPYING`, pure ASCII, 338 lines) + `./NOTICE` (padMule (C) 2026 Anthony Bufort, GPL-2.0-or-later, states the derivation from aMule/eMule and that the vendored tree is the reference oracle). Consistency wrinkle recorded, not acted on: `refs/` (eMule/aMule oracles) is gitignored while `amule-3.0.1/` is tracked - keeping it tracked preserves the in-repo divergence record, so it stays. See [[padmule-licensing]].
- 2026-07-17 **CANCEL / DELETE A DOWNLOAD** (feature 2 of 4). A download can now be removed mid-flight. The wrinkle: `fetch::download_file` spawns DETACHED worker tasks (dropping the outer future does NOT abort them - a dropped `tokio::spawn` handle detaches, it does not cancel), so aborting the driver would leave workers writing to a `.part` we are about to delete. Fix = COOPERATIVE cancellation: `Download` gains a lock-free `cancelled: AtomicBool`; `take_blocks` returns empty when set (the peer session ends, the worker loop falls through), and both the round loop and worker loop in fetch.rs check `is_cancelled()`. Engine `cancel_download(hash)`: finds the download, `cancel()`s it, removes it from the registry, and deletes the `.part` + `.part.met` (`PartStore::remove_backing_files` - an open handle a straggler holds keeps the bytes readable via the unlinked inode, but the paths leave disk at once so a restart will not resume it). The outer add_download task bails without saving when it sees `is_cancelled()`. FFI `cancelDownload(hash) -> bool` -> SwiftUI swipe-to-delete (destructive "Remove") on each transfer row. 2 new tests (cancel removes it + deletes both files + unknown hash is a no-op false; a cancelled download hands out no more blocks). 370 workspace tests, clippy + fmt + ASCII clean.
- 2026-07-17 **KAD SEARCH MERGED INTO THE SEARCH BOX** (feature 3 of 4; the box stops depending on a live server). `engine.search()` now queries the connected server AND Kad CONCURRENTLY (`tokio::join!` over the two DISJOINT fields - server link + Kad node - so the wait is the slower of the two, not the sum), then folds both into ONE `catalog` pass. Kad's already-distilled `FileResult {hash,name,size,sources}` is converted to a synthetic tagged `SearchResultFile` (id tags 0x01 name / 0x02 size / 0x15 sources) so dedup/rank/trust apply uniformly and a file found on both merges by hash with the better availability winning. Either half may be absent: a SERVERLESS client still gets Kad hits (Kad needs no server), a client with no Kad contacts still gets server hits. Kad bounded by KAD_SEARCH_WAIT=15s (concurrent, so usually free) + KAD_PER_QUERY=750ms; a lookup that misses the budget contributes nothing. Reuses `KadNode::resolve_keyword` (already proven by `mule-cli kad-keyword`). NO FFI/Swift change - the merge is transparent at the engine seam, so the existing search box just returns more. Added tokio `macros` to the main dep (was dev-only) for `join!`. 1 new test (a Kad hit + a server hit for one hash collapse to one ranked file, best availability wins). 371 workspace tests, clippy + fmt + ASCII clean. Closes the caveat from the search-wiring milestone ("search only queries your connected server, not Kad").
- 2026-07-17 **ON-DEVICE HighID via UNICAST SSDP** (feature 4 of 4; the biggest and least verifiable). iOS SILENTLY DROPS multicast SSDP without the restricted multicast entitlement (unreachable for a free-signed app), so UPnP discovery could never earn HighID on the iPad. Fix: UNICAST M-SEARCH aimed straight at the gateway - same UPnP protocol, no multicast, no entitlement. `upnp.rs`: `msearch_message()` (MX only on the multicast form), `gateway_candidates()` (infer .1/.254 of our /24, since iOS exposes no default-gateway API), `local_lan_ip()` (the connect-a-UDP-socket-to-a-public-host trick; no packet sent, just reads the chosen source addr), `discover_unicast()` (M-SEARCH per candidate, trust ONLY replies from the queried addr, then reuse the existing `http_get` + `parse_wan_service` + SOAP AddPortMapping). `map_port()` now tries multicast then falls back to unicast, so the SAME binary earns HighID on desktop AND iPad. `map_port_unicast()` + `mule-cli upnp-unicast <port>` expose the unicast-only path for live verification. 3 new tests (gateway_candidates .1/.254 and never self; unicast M-SEARCH omits MX + multicast keeps it; `discover_unicast` end-to-end against a mock UDP gateway + mock description server over loopback). 374 workspace tests, clippy + fmt + ASCII clean. **VERIFICATION STATUS: unit + mock PROVEN; LIVE against a real gateway UNVERIFIED from here.** WSL2 returns NO SSDP response by any method (multicast, unicast, or raw `nc`), and the Xfinity gateway at 10.0.0.1 is reachable on :80 but SILENT on SSDP:1900 - so its UPnP is apparently OFF (likely why manual forwarding was needed originally, not UPnP). Gateway INFERENCE is correct: `local_lan_ip`=10.0.0.33, `candidates[0]`=10.0.0.1=the real gateway. The real verdict is the on-device test: iPad flips to HighID => unicast UPnP works; iPad stays LowID => the gateway's UPnP is off and the fallback is a manual forward to the iPad's OWN LAN IP (the dev-box forward points at the Windows host 10.0.0.33, which does nothing for the iPad). See [[padmule-dev-box-networking]].

Comcast keeps it off at the firmware level. RESOLUTION (Anthony's call): bought a **TP-Link Archer BE9700** (tri-band Wi-Fi 7, 1x 10G + 4x 2.5G), put the XB8 in **bridge mode** (it hands off 2 Gig only out of its red 2.5G Port 4; confirmed via Ookla CLI = 2099 Mbps down / 360 up / 12 ms on the PC's 2.5G "Ethernet 4" NIC), and ran the BE9700 as edge router (default MAC, UPnP on). Then `mule-cli upnp-unicast 4662` from the dev box (now 192.168.0.32, gw 192.168.0.1) **SUCCEEDED**: the BE9700's IGD mapped the port and GetExternalIPAddress returned the REAL PUBLIC IP (matches api.ipify.org => no double-NAT). This LIVE-VALIDATES the exact iOS unicast-SSDP path (feature 4) against a real gateway - the last unverified item of the whole build. iPad still LowID at report time = iPad-side (iOS Local Network permission, or a stale session needing a force-restart; also a lingering test mapping 4662->dev-box). - 2026-07-17 **ROOT CAUSE of the iPad LowID = my own leftover mapping + a query bug** (the Port-mapping row paid off immediately). The row read "UPnP: could not map port 4662 (gateway refused: ConflictInMappingEntry)" - so the iPad REACHED the BE9700's IGD (permission/reachability fine) but got 718: 4662 was already mapped to **192.168.0.32 (the DEV BOX)** - a PERMANENT (lease 0) mapping left by my own `upnp-unicast 4662` validation run, squatting the port the whole time. Worse, `query_specific_mapping` treated ANY non-200 as "free", so my earlier "is it mapped?" check falsely said 4662 was unmapped and I dismissed the very conflict I had flagged. TWO FIXES: (1) `query_specific_mapping` now distinguishes 714 (NoSuchEntryInArray = genuinely free) from real faults (returned as errors) - no more false "free". (2) `map_port` + `map_port_unicast` now **DELETE-THEN-ADD**: clear any stale mapping (our own old DHCP IP after a lease change, or a leftover permanent one) before AddPortMapping, so a strict IGD's ConflictInMappingEntry can never wedge us again. Verified LIVE: mapping 4663 twice (used to conflict on the 2nd) now succeeds both times; 4662 cleared and confirmed STABLY free (fixed query, no rogue process). 375 tests, clippy/fmt/ASCII clean. LESSON: a validation side effect (a permanent port map) + a lenient error path that read as "no problem" cost hours - make diagnostics fail LOUD, and always clean up what a test mutates.

This closes the ENTIRE build: padMule is now a first-class eD2k/Kad client on the iPad - search (server + Kad), hash-verified downloads to Files, upload/sharing with the Leech-Mode toggle, swipe-to-cancel, AND HighID reachability - all built with NO Apple hardware (CI + Sideloadly). Durability note: reserve the iPad's IP (192.168.0.182) in the BE9700 so the permanent mapping never goes stale, and the delete-then-add fix self-heals if it ever does. (4) real upload queueing: `share.rs::UploadGate` sends OP_QUEUERANKING at capacity (0x60, 12-byte payload per eMule 0.50a) and grants a freed slot in place, instead of lying OP_FILEREQANSNOFIL; scoped to the held connection (no persistent queue/dial-out/UDP-reask - foreground-only platform fit; see [[decisions-and-lessons]]). Fixed: (a) upload-cap BYPASS - OP_REQUESTPARTS streamed data without a granted slot, so a peer skipping OP_STARTUPLOADREQ ignored the cap+queue (now requires a held permit); (b) queue-counter LEAK on disconnect-while-queued (RAII WaitTicket guard); (c) unbounded idle serve sessions after the permit moved into the upload arm (60s idle-read timeout + 120s queued-wait bound); (d) known.met race + torn write on concurrent finishers (serialize with a lock + atomic temp+rename); (e) stale re-share of a file replaced at the same path (load verifies on-disk size == persisted size); (f) startup stall - resume_fetches ran serially inline holding the FFI engine lock, risking a skipped checkpoint if backgrounded (report Running first, bound the pass 8s total/4s each); (g) best-effort rank-vs-grant order (documented); (h) fetch-complete max_size over 4 GiB (omit the 32-bit wire filter, rely on the client-side u64 filter). 385 workspace tests, clippy/fmt clean, and the amuled differential test still transfers all 3 files byte-for-byte (it exercises the DOWNLOAD direction - it did NOT catch the serve-side cap/queue bugs, the review did). 3 new tests. LESSON in [[decisions-and-lessons]]/[[build-progress]]: re-review any change that reshapes a hot path; "moved the check" == "removed the check" until proven otherwise. Commit c3026ea.

- 2026-07-18 **0.70b SECOND FEATURE SLICE (ratings-read channel A + per-file unshare).** (1) SERVER RATINGS: catalog now parses FT_FILERATING (0xF7) from the OP_SEARCHRESULT tags padMule already receives. The tag is a MASKED field, decoded aMule's way `(value & 0xF) / 3` (SearchFile.cpp:77): 0 none / 1 Fake / 2 Poor / 3 Fair / 4 Good / 5 Excellent, best-of across a hash's rows; rating 1 (Fake) also flags Trust::Suspect so it sinks + shows a warning. FFI SearchHit.rating; colored pill on search rows + a Rating row in the detail sheet. Research (agent, cited): rating and comment travel on DIFFERENT mechanisms - rating is a tag (server search result OR the FT_FILERATING masked tag), but the COMMENT is NOT a tag; it rides OP_FILEDESC (0x61) POST-CONNECT from a source (needs AcceptCommentVer=1 in the hello + an inbound handler), so comments + per-source ratings would badge DOWNLOAD rows - deferred as a bigger item, with Kad notes (#22). Landmine noted: aMule's `(x&0xF)/3` and eMule's `LOBYTE/51` decode the same tag DIFFERENTLY; replicated aMule's. (2) PER-FILE UNSHARE: engine unshare_file(hash) drops a file from the live shared list AND known.met (new forget_shared_file + a shared write_known_met_atomic, under the known_met_lock) so it does not re-share on restart; the file stays in Files. FFI unshare_file -> swipe-to-Unshare on the Shared screen (parallels swipe-to-cancel on transfers). The finding CONFIRMS the disclosed verified-badge deferral, and sharpened it: note_source_verified has NO live caller, and run_secure_ident is called only in tests - so padMule never runs identity verification on any live path (the dive's "padMule already runs secure-ident" premise was inaccurate; padMule has the code but never runs it live). So the checkmark can never render in production today. Not a hidden bug - the seal is guarded by `if s.verified` (always false), so no dead UI shows; the encryption LOCK (obfuscated) is real and does render. DECISION: hold the identity checkmark as PARTIAL rather than rush secure-ident into a live path - it needs (a) a placement decision (pre-transfer = a timeout cost on every source connect; post-successful-transfer = bounded but hard to integration-test) and (b) a real integration test (the differential harness drives download_from_peer, NOT fetch_one, and there is no secure-ident-capable serve side in tests), so shipping it now would be inspection-only-validated. Recorded as the clear next pass. No code change; [[emule-070b-features]] #4 already marked PARTIAL with this rationale.
- 2026-07-19 **VERIFIED-IDENTITY BADGE NOW LIVE** (the deferred item, delivered). fetch_one runs mutual secure-ident (run_secure_ident) with a source AFTER it delivers bytes, on the warm connection, and marks it verified on success (note_source_verified -> the per-source sheet's blue seal). Bounded + safe: gated on the peer ADVERTISING sec_ident (Capabilities.sec_ident != 0, so a non-supporting source never costs the completion sweep a timeout), gated on bytes>0 (only real deliverers), best-effort with an 8s cap (a silent/timing-out peer just stays unverified; the file already arrived). Identity is now Clone (Arc'd into the parallel download workers); download_file gains Option<Arc<Identity>> (engine supplies it, CLI/sequential paths pass None). Does NOT touch the differential path (peer-download uses download_from_peer, not fetch_one) - differential still byte-for-byte. PROVEN by a unit test with a mock secure-ident peer (a silent-peer test confirms no hang/failure). Note: the dive's premise "padMule already runs secure-ident" was inaccurate - it only ran in tests until this change. 402 tests, clippy/fmt clean, CI iOS build green. Marked #4 DONE in [[emule-070b-features]]. Adversarial review workflow over the change. Commit d401ec6.
- 2026-07-19 **VERIFIED-IDENTITY BADGE REVERTED (secure-ident-in-fetch was broken).** The adversarial review of d401ec6 CONFIRMED a HIGH deadlock: padMule's fetch HELLO advertises MISCOPTIONS1 sec_ident=0 (HelloInfo::baseline -> baseline_misc_options1(0)), so a real aMule/eMule uploader's m_bySupportSecIdent reads 0 and it never issues its own OP_SECIDENTSTATE; padMule-as-initiator-post-transfer then waits forever for that packet -> the exchange DEADLOCKS and burns the full 8s timeout on EVERY real delivering source (delaying the download's "Saved" event by ~8s), and verified never fires against a real peer. The unit test was a FALSE POSITIVE - it had BOTH ends call run_secure_ident as initiators (synthetic; no real serve peer behaves that way). Reverted d401ec6 -> commit dbfecad (fetch_one back to no secure-ident, download_file back to no Identity param, Identity un-Clone'd). Reverted tree: 237 mule-engine tests + full workspace pass, clippy/fmt clean. LESSON (reinforced): a synthetic unit test that does not match how the real peer behaves is worse than no test - it manufactures false confidence; do not ship an interop feature validated only by a mock that both-sides-initiate. The original deferral instinct (cannot integration-test secure-ident on a live path) was correct and should not have been overridden. CORRECT future approach: advertise sec_ident in the fetch HELLO so the uploader initiates, RESPOND to its OP_SECIDENTSTATE right after the hello (not post-transfer as initiator), and build a real integration test with a mock uploader that initiates secure-ident (the differential harness drives download_from_peer, not fetch_one). #4 back to PARTIAL in [[emule-070b-features]]; the encryption LOCK stays live, the identity CHECKMARK stays off (guarded by `if s.verified`, always false - no dead UI).

AUTO deferred - it needs a periodic source-count recompute loop that does not exist (documented). Persistence is byte-faithful part.met: pinned against the aMule source (NOT an agent summary) - PartFile.cpp:928-933 writes BOTH FT_DLPRIORITY(0x18) + FT_OLDDLPRIORITY(0x13), same value, and CFileDataIO::WriteTag (SafeFile.cpp:513) emits the tag's own type = CTagInt32's UINT32, so padMule writes TagValue::U32(priority) for both; open() reads either (any int width) and clamps unknown/PR_AUTO(5) -> Normal like aMule (PartFile.cpp:503-515). A byte-LAYOUT test asserts the exact on-disk tag bytes (03 01 00 18 <v LE>), per the "assert offsets not just round-trip" lesson. ManagerConfig dropped its fixed `parallel` (now priority-derived + live); Download gained a lock-free priority atomic (init from store); engine set_download_priority + FFI set_download_priority + DownloadInfo.priority. - 2026-07-19 #7 priority adversarial review (4 dimensions, 12 agents) found 7 CONFIRMED issues - all fixed (commit 9797d82), which is exactly why a hot-path change earns a review even when 406 tests + the differential are green. (1) MEDIUM: dropping ManagerConfig's `parallel` field silently regressed the CLI fetch-complete harness from 6/8 -> 4 concurrent peers. (2)+(5) LOW: I made the concurrent-peer count live per round but left the round budget frozen at spawn, so the "sweeps 6/8/12 rounds live" claim was false for a mid-session bump. FIX for both: ManagerConfig is now an enum - Fixed{parallel,per_peer,rounds} for the CLI/test harnesses (restores 6/8), ByPriority{per_peer} for the engine, and download_file's round loop became a `while` that reads BOTH the peer count AND the round budget live from dl.priority() each round. (3)+(6) MEDIUM/LOW: I clamped PR_AUTO(5) -> Normal, but aMule maps AUTO -> HIGH (PartFile.cpp:506-509) - importing a desktop Auto download silently downgraded it; FIX maps AUTO -> HIGH (genuinely-unknown 3/4/6 still clamp to Normal), comment + test corrected. (4) MEDIUM: a priority change on an already-IDLE download (fetch task ended) is inert until the next fetch/restart - the engine has no periodic re-drive; documented honestly (running downloads ARE fully live). (7) LOW: set_download_priority's blocking save_met could race finish_download's Arc::try_unwrap and strand a just-finished file; FIX skips a complete download. Re-verified: 406 tests, clippy/fmt/ASCII clean, differential still byte-for-byte; a focused re-review of the fix commit (9797d82) confirmed NO regressions (while-loop iteration count identical to the old for-loop, enum dispatch + all caller variants correct with 6/8/4/4/3 restored, AUTO->HIGH byte-exact vs the oracle). It also surfaced a PRE-EXISTING latent issue (not introduced by #7): finish_download does Arc::try_unwrap to reclaim the PartStore, and any concurrent Arc<Download> holder (downloads() poll, cancel, set_download_priority) at that instant makes it fail -> the byte-complete .part is left unfinished, and resume_fetches skips it on restart (it filters complete downloads), so it can strand. Razor-thin (microsecond TOCTOU) + LOW; filed as a future task, NOT expanded into #7. LESSON reinforced: the differential gate drives download_from_peer, NOT download_file, so fetch-manager changes MUST be adversarially reviewed - the tests encode the paths you thought of.

- 2026-07-19 **#35 fixed: finish a completed download through the lock (no Arc::try_unwrap).** finish_download reclaimed the PartStore via Arc::try_unwrap (into_store), which fails if ANY concurrent Arc<Download> clone exists (the 1s downloads() poll, cancel, set_download_priority) - stranding a byte-complete .part (removed from the registry, never moved to Files, and resume_fetches skips complete downloads on restart). FIX: PartStore::finish_in_place(&mut self) renames the .part without consuming the store (Unix/iPadOS renames an open file fine; the handle points at the moved inode until drop), and Download::finish_to(&self) drives it through the inner lock, so a live clone can no longer block the finish. Regression test: finish_to moves the file + clears .part/.met while a second Arc clone is held. into_store stays for test helpers. 407 tests, clippy/fmt clean, differential byte-for-byte (it exercises this completion path). Commit 3ad90dd. Task #35 done.

NodeIdentity.rsa is now Arc<Identity> (cheap-shared into the parallel fetch workers). peer-download also advertises sec_ident + carries a fresh Identity so the amuled differential exercises the REAL exchange. VALIDATED against a FAITHFUL other-side (the whole point, [[interop-test-fidelity]]): (1) a unit test with a mock uploader that INITIATES secure-ident + serves -> padMule downloads AND verifies (the RIGHT mock, not both-initiate); (2) the differential test: "source identity verified (secure-ident): true" against REAL aMule 3.0.1, byte-for-byte (repeat same-IP connections read false because amuled caches trust per IP - expected). Both refuted findings are non-defects: (1) padMule omits eMule's per-IP signature-flood cap - refuted because the per-peer session is timeout-bounded, the download completes on other parallel workers, and the CPU-waste "flood" is strictly a worse deal for an attacker than an already-possible free worker-stall (so it grants no new capability); per CLAUDE.md "no speculative hardening for impossible cases," left as-is. - 2026-07-19 **Local eD2k SERVER oracle: real Lugdunum eserver 17.15, sandboxed.** Anthony asked whether we could OBTAIN and RUN a local server oracle (to make #9 global-UDP-search / #10 related-search testable against a REAL server, not a round-trip mock). Yes: obtained Lugdunum eserver 17.15 from emule-security.org/downloads/100 (the site padMule already trusts for server.met). SECURITY: it is a legacy (2007) closed-source binary, so it runs ONLY inside `unshare -rn` (isolated net namespace, loopback-only, NO egress - verified an egress probe fails "Network is unreachable"); gitignored under build-oracle/eserver/, sha256-recorded (i686 fe38ecdf..., x86_64 82d19017..., zip e518451a...). GOTCHA: the x86_64 build SIGSEGVs at startup - `segfault at ffffffffff600400` = the legacy VSYSCALL page (time() via vsyscall), which modern kernels block (WSL2 6.6, no vsyscall=emulate); the i686 build does not use it and runs under WSL2 IA32 emulation, so the oracle uses eserver-i686. PROVEN: padMule logs into the real eserver -> `Connected { id: 2, low_id: true }` + eserver's own "your ip 0.0.0.0 -> LOWID" message (LowID is correct - no routable IP in the isolated namespace); pause/resume reconnect works too. eserver listens UDP on 4661/4665/4669 (confirmed via ss), so #9's OP_GLOBSEARCHREQ/RES is now testable. New scripts/eserver-oracle.sh (obtain+verify+config+isolated run, one command) + docs/wiki/[[ed2k-server-oracle]]. This completes the oracle set: amuled (peer, differential), real eMule (peer, [[emule-peer-oracle]]), real eserver (server). Task #37.

- 2026-07-19 OP_OFFERFILES adversarial review -> 4 REAL defects, all fixed (commit b236ed0), all in paths the LowID small-file eserver test could not reach. (1) CRITICAL: offer_shared_to (awaited inline by connect_server -> start()/resume()) had NO timeout on the offer write and held self.shared across it -> a stalled TCP write (a mobile reconnect after a network change) would hang connect/resume forever AND block a concurrent share change (finish_download's shared.push), violating the lifecycle bound. FIX: snapshot the library into an owned Vec + release the lock before the .await, and wrap link.offer_files in timeout(10s) best-effort. (2) CRITICAL: a >4-GiB offer to a SERVER needs FT_FILESIZE(low32) + FT_FILESIZE_HI(0x3A, high32) as TWO 32-bit tags (count 3), not one u64 FT_FILESIZE (peer/VBT form) - aMule branches pServer vs pClient (KnownFile.cpp:1209-1234). FIX + a large-file test. (3) no per-packet cap: aMule caps each OFFERFILES burst at min(server soft-files, 200) + republishes the rest; padMule sent the whole library unbounded -> cap at 200 (v1 does not republish the remainder). (4) the HighID branch published the real id+port (== public IP) into the server search index; aMule uses FILE_COMPLETE markers for a complete file whenever the server advertises compression (KnownFile.cpp:1172 - every live server) -> ALWAYS use FILE_COMPLETE markers (also removes the id from the connect path + the privacy caveat). Re-verified vs real eserver: still indexes (1 files, 1 complete, 2 keyw). 414 tests. LESSON: a small-LowID validation misses the large-file + HighID + oversized-library paths - the review, cross-checked vs the aMule oracle, caught all three. - 2026-07-19 OP_OFFERFILES fix re-review (commit b236ed0) came back CLEAN: all 4 fixes correct, NO functional regressions (lock genuinely released before the await, timeout bounds the write, large-file tag count matches the two 32-bit halves, 200-cap correct, no id/port leak at any call site). Only a cosmetic nit - a stale HighID comment left in cmd_offer_search - which was deleted (commit e615b33, trivial doc-only). OP_OFFERFILES (#39) pushed and done.
- 2026-07-19 **OP_OFFERFILES follow-up: re-offer on a mid-session share change (task #40).** v1 only announced shares on connect, so a download finishing mid-session was unfindable until a reconnect. finish_download now raises a shared_dirty AtomicBool (cloned into FinishCtx); the 1s FFI downloads() poll (the app's global heartbeat, a Swift Timer that survives pause/resume) drains it via the new Engine::maintain_shares, which re-announces the library while a server is connected. offer_shared_to became an associated fn (shared, link) so maintain_shares gets disjoint-field borrows of self.server (mut) + self.shared. Adversarial 2-lens review (concurrency + protocol, each finding independently verified; 2 confirmed, 2 dismissed) caught TWO REAL defects, both fixed BEFORE push: (1) MEDIUM - maintain_shares swap(false)'d the flag UNCONDITIONALLY, before the connected check; a poll firing while the link is PausedForBackground would consume the flag with no offer, and resume()'s success fast-path does NOT re-offer (only a full connect_server does), so the file could go unannounced for the whole session. FIX: load-peek without clearing, and store(false) only AFTER confirming a connected server (no await between, so no lost update; a completion during the offer re-arms). (2) LOW - offer_shared_to took `.iter().take(200)` = the OLDEST 200 by insertion order, but finish_download appends new completions to the TAIL, so once the library hits 200 the just-finished file (the whole reason a re-offer fires) is exactly the one dropped - and connect_server shares the same code so a reconnect omitted it too. FIX: `.iter().rev().take(200)` = the NEWEST 200. Dismissed (correctly): the 10s write under the Engine mutex (mirrors connect_server's existing best-effort risk; offer_files is a single fire-and-forget packet) and the cleared-flag-on-failed-offer drop (intentional best-effort, consistent with connect). Wire packet (build_offer_files) unchanged -> [[ed2k-server-oracle]] OP_OFFERFILES validation still holds; CLI offer-hold does not route through offer_shared_to. 415 tests (1 new: maintain_shares_keeps_the_flag_until_it_can_offer), clippy/fmt/ASCII clean. Code commit 8dcf7cb.
- 2026-07-19 **#10 related-files search (eMule `related::` protocol) - DONE + LIVE-VALIDATED (task #41).** Research (refs/emule-0.50a): "Search related" is NOT a new opcode - CSearchResultsWnd::SearchRelatedFiles builds a normal search whose expression is `related` + `::` + md4str(hash) per file (SearchListCtrl.cpp:941 "just a shortcut for the user typing related::[filehash] into the searchfield"), sent as an ordinary OP_SEARCHREQUEST; the Lugdunum server special-cases the `related::` prefix. Gated by CanSearchRelatedFiles = server advertises SRV_TCPFLG_RELATEDSEARCH (server.h:39 = 0x40) in the OP_IDCHANGE flags word. md4str = UPPERCASE hex (otherfunctions.cpp:1231). Implemented across the stack: SRV_TCPFLG_RELATEDSEARCH const (server_messages.rs); ServerState::Connected gains related_search: bool from ic.tcp_flags & 0x40 (connection.rs, threaded through EVERY construction/destructure incl. the resume() success path so ServerInfo stays accurate); ServerLink::related_search_supported; search::related_keyword(hash) -> "related::<UPPERHEX>" as ONE keyword leaf (write_keyword does not split on ':'); Engine::related_search - SERVER-ONLY (Kad has no related index), gated, ranked via the same catalog() as normal search; FFI related_search(hash) + ServerInfoFfi.related_search + a shared ranked_to_hits() factored out of search(); the app's "Search related" button (SearchDetailView) now runs the TRUE protocol when server?.relatedSearch, else falls back to a filename keyword search (was a filename stub); CLI related-search harness. **LIVE-VALIDATED vs real Lugdunum eserver 17.15 ([[ed2k-server-oracle]]): it ADVERTISES 0x40 (padMule read `Connected { related_search: true }` off the live server) AND accepted the `related::` query without error.** No hits returned - an empty single-client server has no co-occurrence index, and a returned related hit needs the searcher on a different IP than the offerer (same self-filter caveat as offer-search) - so the flag read + query acceptance are proven live, a populated result is not reproducible in the isolated single-IP oracle. The user expected this NOT to be testable; it turned out to be. 3-lens adversarial review (protocol / Rust / Swift-binding, each finding independently verified) returned CLEAN (0 findings, all 3 finders deliberately empty; journal-confirmed). 417 tests (2 new: related_keyword_is_the_related_prefix_plus_uppercase_hex, handshake_reads_related_search_support_from_the_flags_word), clippy/fmt/ASCII clean. Commit 61166d8.
- 2026-07-19 **Tier-2 #14 statistics tab - DONE (task #42, commit 81a58c6).** A live down/up transfer-rate chart (last ~60s) + session byte totals + up:down ratio, as a 5th toolbar Screen (chart.xyaxis.line). Design split: the ENGINE only counts bytes (new `crate::stats`: two process-global monotonic AtomicU64 counters, add_downloaded at multi_source.rs where received blocks are committed, add_uploaded at share.rs where a served block is written - FILE DATA only, matching eMule "session down/up"); the UI does ALL the statistics (EngineModel.sampleStats folds the totals from the existing 1s refresh() poll into a rolling rateHistory: [RatePoint]; StatsView renders with Swift Charts). Global-not-per-Engine is deliberate: one Engine per process, and threading two counters through the whole transfer call chain (download_file -> fetch_from_sources -> download_from_peer + serve_shared) would touch a dozen signatures for no fidelity. FFI: TransferStats{total_down,total_up} + MuleEngine::transfer_stats. 3-lens adversarial review (Swift/Rust/integration) caught ONE real bug, unanimously (all 3 lenses, 0 dismissed): sampleStats assumed a strict 1s cadence, but refresh() ALSO fires off-cadence (download/cancel/priority completions, pause/resume, boot), so a sub-second byte delta was charted as a per-second rate -> false dips + under-reporting + a sub-60s window. FIX: rate = delta / ACTUAL elapsed seconds (Date-based), and a rate point is folded only once >= ~0.9s of real time has passed; off-cadence refreshes still update the totals but never inject a dip or rebase the baseline. LESSON: a poll assumed to be periodic often is not - a UI model's refresh() is a shared chokepoint called from many actions; rate math must divide by measured elapsed time, not an assumed tick. 418 tests (1 new: stats counters_are_monotonic_and_additive, a race-safe delta assertion since the counter is a shared global), clippy/fmt/ASCII clean. Global-static test isolation is safe because no existing test asserts an absolute total.
- 2026-07-19 **"Finish the search engine" (task #43): boolean OR/NOT tree (#30) + AICH byte-validation - BOTH DONE.** (1) BOOLEAN SEARCH (commit 92a67e0): the keyword field, previously sent as ONE string leaf, is now parsed as a full boolean expression - implicit-AND between adjacent words, explicit AND/OR/NOT (uppercase, as eMule requires), parentheses, "quoted phrases" - into a binary QueryExpr tree, serialized in PREFIX order with eMule's EXACT operator bytes (AND=00 00, OR=00 01, NOT=00 02; NOT is the BINARY "left AND-NOT right", from CSearchExprTarget WriteBooleanAND/OR/NOT, SearchResultsWnd.cpp:888-908). Also fixes multi-word search to split into ANDed leaves like eMule (was one leaf). The keyword subtree is ANDed with the size/type/source filters. PRECEDENCE LANDMINE: eMule parser.y:52-54 declares `%left TOK_AND` FIRST, making AND the LOWEST precedence (then OR, then NOT highest), all left-assoc - with an in-grammar comment that AND must be lowest so `a b OR c d` == `a AND b OR c AND d`. My first cut had it INVERTED (OR-lowest); the adversarial review's "dismissed" precedence finding was factually right and I fixed it (replicate-then-improve: match eMule). The SAME review CONFIRMED a real crash: unbounded parse_or/and/primary recursion aborted the process on deeply-nested input (a Rust stack overflow is an uncatchable SIGABRT; the reviewer measured ~8000 nested `(` on a 2MB stack, and the iOS ~512KB thread stack is worse at ~2000) -> FIX: a nesting depth guard (bail to the fallback leaf past MAX_DEPTH=64) + an ITERATIVE stray-token skip in parse_primary (the operator-skip used to self-recurse). Parser is now parse_and(lowest)->parse_or->parse_not(highest)->parse_primary. VALIDATED: 9 byte-layout tests (incl. and_binds_looser_than_or_like_emule + a_pathologically_nested_query_does_not_crash) + the real Lugdunum eserver 17.15 ACCEPTED "linux OR (ubuntu AND iso) NOT windows" and "movie 2024 OR 2023 NOT cam" in clean login->search->response exchanges (new mule-cli server-search harness; global-search reuses the encoder; the dead public server.met I tried returned 0 for even a single keyword, so live-public validation was not reproducible - the isolated eserver is the faithful other-side). (2) AICH BYTE-VALIDATION (commit 61f206b): aich_master_hash was only structurally cross-checked vs SHA1 ([[interop-test-fidelity]]: a same-way-wrong reference gives false confidence). Now byte-validated against real amuled 3.0.1: a DETERMINISTIC 10 MB file (LCG byte[i]=((i*1103515245+12345)>>16)&0xFF; > PARTSIZE 9,728,000 so the multi-part tree branch runs; 55 AICH blocks = ceil(10M/184320)) hashed by amuled yields master root bc301c26ff3cc6d98e804901603a0a3288351100 - and padMule produces the IDENTICAL root. GOTCHA: aMule stores the AICH NOT in known.met (which stays a 5-byte empty header) but in known2_64.met, the AICH hashset backup, laid out `version(1) | [root(20) | count(u32) | count*hash(20)] per file`. scripts/aich-golden.sh reproduces the golden end-to-end. 426 tests, clippy/fmt/ASCII clean. Tier-1 + these two Tier-2 items done; next per Anthony: on-device verification pass, then #17 preview incomplete media.
- 2026-07-19 **Tier-2 #17 preview/open incomplete media (AVPlayer) - DONE (task #44, commit 39c59fe).** Play a still-downloading media file. Design: a per-Download `preview` AtomicBool (read lock-free by take_blocks) flips PartFile::next_blocks from rarest-first to forward-SEQUENTIAL, so the raw sparse `.part` (byte-addressed: write_block seeks to the offset) grows CONTIGUOUSLY from offset 0. PartFile::contiguous_prefix() = first-gap start = the leading run a player can read (guaranteed real on-disk data since write_block sync_data's BEFORE fill_gap). The UI snapshots `[0, prefix)` to a temp file (copy_file_prefix) and plays it with AVKit VideoPlayer; "Preview" is a context action on incomplete AVPlayer-friendly media (mp4/m4v/mov/m4a/mp3/aac/wav/caf/aif/aiff). FFI: set_preview, preview_snapshot(hash,dest)->bytes, DownloadInfo.preview+contiguous_prefix. 3-LENS ADVERSARIAL REVIEW found 5 confirmed issues (3 distinct), ALL fixed before commit: (1) LOW->real: snapshot_prefix held the Download inner lock AND the FFI Mutex<Engine> across a synchronous fsync'd copy of the WHOLE prefix (hundreds of MB for a big video), stalling the download's block commits + the 1s heartbeat for seconds. FIX: split into preview_target (returns (part_path,len) under a BRIEF lock) + copy_file_prefix (a free fn that opens its OWN read handle); the FFI does the copy in spawn_blocking with NO lock held. SAFE because `[0,len)` is completed contiguous data the block writer never rewrites (writes only fill gaps at/past len = the first gap start), so a second read handle on a disjoint region races nothing. (2) LOW: preview latched ON - startPreview called setPreview(on:true) and nothing ever called setPreview(on:false), so one tap disabled rarest-first (which pulls rare parts before they vanish) for the whole session. FIX: PreviewItem carries the hash; PreviewPlayerView.onDisappear -> model.stopPreview(hash) reverts it. (3) MEDIUM: the ORIGINAL ordering fetched first+last parts early "so the container trailer/moov arrives early", but the snapshot only copies the CONTIGUOUS HEAD - the early last-part is a disconnected island NOT in `[0,prefix)`, so it never reached the player and just SLOWED prefix growth. FIX: pure SEQUENTIAL ordering (parts.sort_by_key(|p| p)); a non-faststart/moov-at-end file now shows an honest "not enough downloaded yet" overlay (poll AVPlayerItem.status==.failed) instead of a black screen. LESSON: a download-prioritization idea borrowed from eMule (first+last for external players / range access) does NOT transfer to a snapshot-a-contiguous-prefix player; the real moov-at-end fix is a future AVAssetResourceLoader serving downloaded ranges. 429 tests (3 new: contiguous_prefix_stops_at_the_first_gap, preview_is_sequential_ignoring_rarity, copy_prefix_snapshots_the_contiguous_leading_bytes), clippy/fmt/ASCII clean. Next per Anthony: #1 on-device verification WITH a hands-on FFI-driven simulation.
- 2026-07-19 **#1 hands-on FFI app-simulation + a Kad-only download fix it found (task #45, commit 64d4bf4).** Anthony asked for on-device verification WITH a "hands-on review via simulation." Since the SwiftUI shell only compiles in CI, the highest-fidelity local hands-on is to drive the REAL MuleEngine FFI OBJECT (the exact seam the app uses) through EngineModel's call sequence and render each "screen" as text: crates/mule-ffi/examples/simulate.rs (a mule-ffi example: MuleEngine::new -> identity -> start -> poll state/server_info/kad_contacts -> search -> add_download -> downloads -> transfer_stats -> set_preview/preview_snapshot -> related_search -> set_sharing -> pause/resume -> shutdown). scripts/simulate.sh runs it INSIDE the isolated eserver namespace (writes a minimal 15-byte server.met pointing at 127.0.0.1:4661) for a deterministic run, and it is also runnable against a live server.met+nodes.dat config. RESULTS: (a) vs eserver - proved the seam + lifecycle: BOOT identity, START->Connected 127.0.0.1:4661 (LowID) [related-search supported], status polling, Leech toggle, pause->Paused->resume->Reconnecting->Connected(id:4), shutdown->Stopped. (b) vs a live config where every server was DOWN but Kad was UP - Kad bootstrapped ~141 contacts and search returned 59 REAL Linux-ebook hits VIA KAD (real names/sizes/sources/types/trust), all through the FFI. **THE SIM FOUND A REAL BUG**: add_download hard-gated on `self.server.is_none()` and returned NoServer BEFORE calling find_sources - but find_sources is explicitly serverless-capable ("a serverless client still gets Kad sources"). So padMule could SEARCH via Kad but never DOWNLOAD when servers were down. FIX (engine.rs): drop the server gate; usability = HighID sources present, OR (server present AND LowID sources present) - so a Kad-only client downloads from HighID Kad sources directly, and a LowID-only source set without a server is honestly NoSources (a LowID peer needs a SERVER callback, request_callbacks already no-ops without one) rather than a stuck 0% download. RE-RUN CONFIRMED: add_download Started and the file progressed 0.00->0.20 MiB (1.0%) via Kad with NO server connected. LESSON: driving the real FFI object end-to-end catches integration gates that per-layer unit tests miss - the simulation is a reusable pre-device confidence check. Still TODO for #1: stage the CI .ipa + a concrete on-device test script for Anthony to run via Sideloadly. 430 tests (1 new: add_download_without_a_server_still_tries_kad), clippy/fmt/ASCII clean.

Boolean NOT + UDP global return 0 from eserver = server-side quirks (padMule's boolean encoding is byte-identical to eMule 0.50a). Gate: 433 tests, clippy/fmt/ASCII clean. build-progress row 8x. - 2026-07-20 **CORRECTION to yesterday's "boolean NOT server-side quirk" claim (it was wrong).** Added a boolean OPERATOR MATRIX to simulate.rs and ran it spaced (MATRIX_GAP=20) against the seeded eserver: padMule's boolean search is FULLY CORRECT - implicit-AND=1, explicit-AND=1, OR=2, OR-one-side=1, NOT-excludes-1=2, NOT-excludes-nothing=3, phrase=1, every count exact. The 0-results I mislabeled as "operator quirks" were the eserver's per-client SEARCH RATE-LIMIT: it answers only the FIRST search in a rapid burst and silently drops the rest (the sim fired search #2 immediately after #1). The GLOBAL-search 0 is separately explained by Engine::global_udp_search deliberately SKIPPING the connected server (the sim's server.met lists only that one, so the fan-out set is empty) - also correct behavior, not a quirk. Real takeaway = a padMule UX gap: unlike eMule, padMule has NO client-side search throttle or "you must wait N seconds" feedback, so a user searching too fast gets mysterious empty results. Candidate v1 polish. The matrix is OPT-IN (MATRIX_GAP>0) so routine sims stay fast + honest (a gap=0 burst would just show a misleading wall of zeros). Fixed [[ed2k-server-oracle]] + build-progress row 8x. Ties to [[verify-before-reporting]]: I reported a diagnosis before spacing the probes to actually test it.

Peer: (6) localize_corruption - on a whole-file hash failure, blame the individual bad part per-MD4 (off-lock) and re-open only it, closing the "one bad source -> full re-download" loop; (7) per-IP inbound connection cap (16/IP, IpConnSlot) so one address cannot starve the 200-permit pool; (8) inbound TCP obfuscation wired (obf_accept auto-detect) + the listener advertises crypt-SUPPORTED (never REQUIRED) so crypt-required peers can reach us - plaintext stays byte-identical, the amuled differential still passes.
