- 2026-08-01 **Serve-side secure-ident: investigated -> DoS-cap kept, feature deferred (build-progress 8ac).** Took on the top remaining Band-A security item. A 4-agent study workflow blueprinted a non-blocking, reweight-only serve-side secure-ident mirroring the proven download path; implemented test-first (a faithful real-role mutual-verify test + a non-block regression, amuled differential still byte-for-byte). A 10-agent adversarial-review workflow then found a HIGH regression the direct-serve tests structurally could NOT catch: advertising sec-ident on the LISTENER hello makes a sec-ident-capable peer LEAD with OP_SECIDENTSTATE, which the leecher-vs-source peek (is_upload_request) does not classify -> it drops both a leecher's upload AND a called-back LowID source's download. A first-principles protocol trace then showed NOT advertising is no escape either: verifying a leecher requires the leecher to CHALLENGE us, which requires us to advertise (the exchange is inherently mutual - signing needs the peer's pubkey, only sent in response to a challenge). So the feature needs advertise + a listener-peek refactor (drain sec-ident before classifying) + the credit store, as ONE unit, validated against a real eMule DOWNLOADING from padMule ([[emule-peer-oracle]], the reverse of scripts/differential-test.sh). Per [[interop-test-fidelity]] + the row-8j revert lesson (do not ship an interop feature you cannot validate against a faithful other-side, especially on the LowID-callback hot path), the wiring was REVERTED. KEPT the one genuinely-valuable thing the review surfaced: the SecureIdentSession now caps RSA signature verification at ONE attempt per connection (verify_attempted), bounding a peer that streams OP_SIGNATURE to force repeated public-key verifies - this also hardens the EXISTING download-side handle_aux_packet path (eMule self-throttles identically, m_dwLastSignatureIP). Separately VERIFIED and CORRECTED an earlier recommendation of mine (to clear the AICH advertised bit for honesty): the AICH bit STAYS advertised - 0x34103212 is byte-verified against real aMule which also advertises it, and an unanswered AICH request is NON-breaking (eMule ClientAICHRequestFailed just re-downloads the part, DownloadClient.cpp:2295 - no disconnect/ban); clearing it would diverge from every real client for zero benefit. Scorecard unchanged (18/5/3 - serve-side reverted). 464 tests (1 new secure-ident DoS test), clippy/fmt/ASCII clean, differential byte-for-byte. META-LESSON: a study-workflow blueprint is a hypothesis, not a verified plan; the adversarial review + a hand-traced protocol walk are what caught that "advertise + handle inline" silently breaks the leecher/source connection classifier. Check and re-check earned its keep.
- 2026-08-01 **LIVE KAD REVALIDATION of the 2026-08-01 security batch (build-progress 8ab) - PASS.** Ran the real-network Kad path after the receiver-key/verified-bit + ipfilter-into-Kad + sybil-cap(2->1) + find_node answer-validation (>11 count cap, self/dup-IP filter) + port-53 changes. `mule-cli kad-bootstrap` against a fresh upd.emule-security.org nodes.dat (194 contacts): sent the obfuscated BOOTSTRAP_REQ, decoded BOOTSTRAP_RES from a v8 node (20 contacts, ReceiverKey path - so the new bValidReceiverKey computation runs), seeded routing, completed HELLO_RES. `mule-cli kad-keyword linux`: the iterative FIND_NODE lookup + SEARCH_KEY_REQ resolved a large set of real files (real hashes/sizes/sources) - so the new answer-validation does NOT reject legitimate real-node answers. Confirms the Kad hardening is live-clean (not just unit-clean); closes the "revalidate on the next live Kad run" caveat. STILL manual/pending: the reverse-direction eMule peer oracle (a real eMule DOWNLOADING from padMule) for the inbound-obf + would-be serve-side-secient wire fidelity - that needs the Windows eMule GUI (Anthony), not automatable from WSL.
- 2026-08-01 **REVERSE peer oracle built + it caught a real serve-side bug (build-progress 8ad, commit 04d9116).** Anthony asked whether padMule's SERVE side was the flaky party or amuled was; the honest answer was that no test had ever driven padMule's serve wire bytes with a real client - the amuled differential + the eMule oracle both have padMule DOWNLOAD. Built scripts/amuled-reverse-oracle.sh: a real amuled 3.0.1 DOWNLOADS a file FROM padMule, relayed through the real Lugdunum eserver, in one isolated `unshare -rn` namespace. Technique: amuled will NOT dial an offline link-source but WILL dial a server-provided source, so padMule serves + OFFERS the file to the eserver (serve-file gained optional `<eserver-host> <eserver-port>` = login + OP_OFFERFILES) and amuled discovers padMule as a source. Namespace landmines each hit before it passed: amuled filters PRIVATE server IPs + a source must differ from the server IP and amuled's own IP (-> public-looking IPs on distinct /24s), padMule's serve IP must be the interface PRIMARY (its HighID address), server.met stores dotted-quad order (inet_aton, not reversed). It IMMEDIATELY caught THE bug: padMule's HELLO advertised ext_multipacket, so amuled sent the bundled OP_MULTIPACKET_EXT (0xa4), which padMule's serve loop drops (it answers only the individual opcodes) -> download died at 0 bytes. Same class as Wave-4d (advertise no capability you do not honour); every symmetric test missed it because padMule-as-downloader sends the INDIVIDUAL opcodes, so no padMule<->padMule or forward-differential path delivers a multipacket to padMule's serve side. FIX: clear the multipacket + ext_multipacket bits (baseline misc1 0x34103212->0x34103210, misc2 0x438->0x418) so real clients fall back to the individual OP_REQUESTFILENAME sequence padMule serves; honouring multipacket serve-side + re-advertising is a tracked FOLLOW-UP (Option B). With the fix the reverse oracle PASSES - real amuled pulls the file byte-for-byte, the FIRST validation of padMule's serve bytes against a genuine client. transfer_session::serve gained a SERVE_DEBUG opcode trace (revealed the 0xa4). Gate: 464 tests, clippy/fmt/ASCII clean, amuled differential still byte-for-byte (download direction unaffected). LESSON: a symmetric interop test suite has a BLIND SPOT exactly where the two roles send different bytes; the serve side needed its own oracle, and it found a real bug the moment it existed. [[emule-peer-oracle]] [[ed2k-server-oracle]] [[interop-test-fidelity]].
- 2026-08-02 **Serve-side OP_MULTIPACKET honoured (build-progress 8ae, commit deab2ab) - Option B.** Follow-up to 8ad: instead of just NOT advertising multipacket, padMule now ANSWERS the bundled OP_MULTIPACKET(_EXT) request and re-advertises the capability byte-equal to a real aMule/eMule (MISCOPTIONS1 0x34103212 / MISCOPTIONS2 0x438). TDD: build_multipacket_answer(hash,name,available) emits <hash><REQFILENAMEANSWER+name><FILESTATUS+status> and deliberately does NOT parse the bundled sub-requests - the downloader's answer reader processes each sub-answer without cross-checking it against its request (aMule ClientTCPSocket.cpp:1264, eMule ListenSocket.cpp:1347), so name+status unconditionally is safe and sidesteps needing the peer's ExtendedRequestsVersion (untracked serve-side); eMule DOES default-throw on an UNRECOGNISED sub-opcode so the builder emits only the two both readers handle. Wired into BOTH transfer_session::serve and the production share::serve_shared. The adversarial review (grounded in the eMule/aMule wire authority) caught a CRITICAL production regression the reverse oracle MISSED: re-advertising the bit makes a capable downloader LEAD with OP_MULTIPACKET(_EXT) instead of OP_REQUESTFILENAME, but the inbound listener's is_upload_request() gate (engine.rs start_listener) did not recognise 0x92/0xa4 -> the production upload path silently dropped the connection. The oracle missed it because serve-file used the minimal serve() path, bypassing the gate. Fixes: (a) add the opcodes to is_upload_request + unit test; (b) route serve-file through the REAL production path (is_upload_request gate + serve_shared) so the reverse oracle now validates the classifier end-to-end - with the fix, the trace shows 0xa4 reaching serve_shared and real amuled downloads byte-for-byte; without it the gate would drop 0xa4 and the oracle would FAIL; (c) serve_shared must not clear a file already latched on a not-found multipacket (aMule sets UploadFileID only when found); (d) corrected a doc comment that wrongly claimed eMule has "no default throw". Gate: 469 tests, clippy/fmt/ASCII clean, differential byte-for-byte, reverse oracle PASS via the production path. LESSON: an oracle only tests the path it actually drives - I pointed serve-file's oracle at a demo shim, not the production listener; "check and re-check" (the adversarial review) is what closed the gap. [[emule-peer-oracle]] [[interop-test-fidelity]] [[verify-before-reporting]].

- 2026-08-02 **Credit REWEIGHT + download accrual (build-progress 8ah, commit b6262c8) - Batch 3, COMPLETES the secure-ident + credits gate item.** The credit system now actually reorders serving. REWEIGHT: replaced the fair-FIFO upload semaphore with a priority UploadGate (mutex over a BTreeSet<(Reverse(score_key), seq)> waiter set + tokio Notify): try_grant serves a free slot only when nobody is queued (no queue-jumping), enqueue admits by CREDIT SCORE, a freed slot goes to the best-scored waiter; leecher_score_key resolves the leecher's stored ratio gated by this-session verification (score_ratio_ident). NEVER-REFUSE preserved (score clamped >=1.0, only reorders; sole refusal is a full queue, identity-independent). QueueWaiter::granted uses the Notify enable()-before-check pattern (no lost wakeups); its Drop removes it from the set (a disconnect/timeout never blocks the peers behind it). DOWNLOAD ACCRUAL - REQUIRED not optional (score_ratio gates on downloaded<1MB, so without it every leecher scores 1.0 and the reweight is INERT): a source's bytes are credited to ITS userhash, threaded credit_store -> download_file -> fetch_one -> run_peer + the engine's called-back-source arm. Adversarial review (7-point concurrency trace) CLEAN, no findings. PROVEN end-to-end by a client SIMULATION (Anthony's point that queue reordering IS provable by simulating clients behaving as needed): two competing leechers over loopback, a HIGH-credit one queued SECOND is served before a fresh one queued FIRST (FIFO could never), 10/10 non-flaky - plus gate ordering/drop/refuse tests + upload/download accrual end-to-end (byte-compat via clients.met). - 2026-08-02 **Per-source corruption attribution + ban (build-progress 8ai) - Poisoning-defense row -> OPERATIONAL.** After investigating the "shortest path" Kad hard-verify and finding it a bigger, interop-validation-gated build (send-side keys need a reverse-Kad oracle that does not exist; faithful enforcement needs the HELLO_RES_ACK challenge machinery), pivoted to the higher-VALUE, offline-PROVABLE poisoning item that builds on the credit + localize_corruption work. Extends 8ab's per-part blame: when a downloaded part fails its MD4, the SOURCE that delivered it is attributed + banned for that file (eMule CorruptionBlackBox). Download tracks part -> contributing source IPs (recorded on commit, std lock off the async transfer lock); localize_corruption bans the SOLE contributor of each bad part + clears its set (re-fetch re-attributes); BOTH the outbound fetch sweep AND the inbound called-back-source arm skip a banned IP. SAFETY: sole-contributor only - a part fed by >1 source blames nobody (without AICH block hashes we cannot pinpoint the bad block), so a good source is NEVER false-banned. ADVERSARIAL REVIEW (verdict: core false-positive safety SOUND) caught two coupled bugs: the ban was enforced only in the outbound sweep (the engine's called-back LowID-source arm bypassed it -> a LowID poisoner, which reaches us ONLY by callback, could re-poison forever), and a SocketAddr key would never match a LowID source anyway (fresh ephemeral port each callback). BOTH fixed: key the ban on IP + check it in the callback arm (tested: banned IP caught on a different port). Same bug-class as the multipacket is_upload_request gap - the second serve entry point was missed. LIMITATION (honest): an attacker sharing every poisoned part with a good source evades the sole-contributor ban - finer per-block attribution needs the deferred AICH block recovery; strictly better than no attribution + never false-positives. Ban is per-download/transient (matches eMule per-file); re-query finds fresh sources if the banned one was the only one (better to stall than accept poison). Tests: the localize test now asserts the bad part's sole source is banned + the good one is not; a two-contributor-no-false-positive test; single-part decline unchanged. Gate: 308 mule-engine lib tests, full workspace green, clippy/fmt/ASCII clean, amuled differential byte-for-byte (commit source-threading did not regress downloads). Scorecard 20/4/2 -> 21/3/2. [[security-model]].

- 2026-08-02 **Kad hard-verify Batch A: send-side receiver-key mechanism (build-progress wave 10, commit 3bf0162).** After the eMule-grounded blueprint resolved the keying nuance (padMule's always-NodeID-keying is byte-faithful to eMule EncryptedDatagramSocket.cpp:278-318 - the receiver key is an in-packet FIELD, not the obfuscation key, so the send-side fix is a FIELD FLIP with no codec change), built the mechanism that lets a real Kad node verify US: routing::Contact gains udp_key + udp_key_ip (the peer's senderVerifyKey + OUR IP when minted); note_verify_key stores it IP-bound (a keyless refresh keeps it, an IP change drops it like the verified bit); verify_key_for is the send GATE (echo the stored key only while udp_key_ip == our current public IP, else 0 = byte-identical to the proven baseline); KadNode gains current_public_ip + set_public_ip; request() echoes verify_key_for(...) instead of hardcoded 0 + returns the peer's sender_vk; bootstrap_from + the find_node lookups store it. A3 (receive-side "we verify them") was already wired. Tests: routing key storage (IP-bound/refresh-keeps/wrong-ip-ignored) + a faithful loopback peer confirming a request echoes the stored key + falls back to 0 after an IP change. LIVE: kad-bootstrap still resolves real v8 nodes with the capture running (non-breakage). 53 mule-kad + 309 mule-engine tests, clippy/fmt/ASCII clean. REMAINING Batch A: (1) feed the engine HighID/UPnP public IP to KadNode so the echo fires in production; (2) the TERMINAL validation - a logging-instrumented amuled Kad node (Anthony's idea) that logs when it marks padMule IP-verified. Investigation found the oracle patch is minimal (~2 log lines): amuled ALREADY logs OP_MULTIPACKETANSWER receipt (ClientTCPSocket.cpp:1147, no patch - verbose mode confirms multipacket 8ae from amuled's side); a 1-line log at CClientCredits::Verified (ClientCredits.cpp:203, confirms amuled verifies padMule = mutual 8af) + a 1-line log at CRoutingZone::Add's ipVerified path (RoutingZone.cpp:403, the Batch A terminal claim) - all LOGGING-ONLY on a BUILD COPY (pristine amule-3.0.1/ untouched), auditable. - 2026-08-02 **Batch A finish (public-IP wiring, commit 9c12e88) + a decisive oracle-scoping discovery: padMule never COMPLETES the Kad v8 IP-verify handshake, so the send-side echo cannot get us verified as built.** (1) Landed the production wiring: Engine.public_ip (learned in map_port from the UPnP/SSDP external IP = theApp.GetPublicIP, kept internally, never emitted) is fed to KadNode::set_public_ip in start_kad, so verify_key_for's echo actually fires live; None -> 0 -> baseline; a stale value only skips the optimization (the echo gate is strict IP-equality), never mis-verifies. 488 tests, clippy/fmt/ASCII clean. (2) While scoping the log-patched oracle, READ the aMule verify paths end-to-end and found TWO things. GOOD: both eD2k terminal claims are ALREADY logged by stock aMule - multipacket at ClientTCPSocket.cpp:1147 ("OP_MULTIPACKETANSWER from <ip>", logRemoteClient) AND secure-ident at BaseClient.cpp:2207 ("'<user>' has passed the secure identification", logClient, on VerifyIdent success). So 8ae/8af need ZERO patch - the reverse oracle can assert them by enabling verbose. So the ClientCredits.cpp:203 patch is unnecessary. BAD (the important one): aMule marks a v8 peer IP-verified ONLY via (a) a HELLO_RES_ACK echoing the responder's senderVerifyKey (Process2HelloResponseAck -> VerifyContact, KademliaUDPListener.cpp:587, already logs "Verified contact by HELLO_RES_ACK"), or (b) a HELLO_REQ carrying a valid receiver key (Process2HelloRequest -> AddContact2 -> RoutingZone::Add:490). ProcessKademlia2Request (find_node) is NOT even passed validReceiverKey, so echoing the key on find_node/search CANNOT verify us. And padMule's LIVE engine sends only BOOTSTRAP_REQ + KADEMLIA2_REQ - it never sends HELLO_REQ (node.hello() is CLI-only, main.rs:1203) and never sends HELLO_RES_ACK anywhere (build_hello_res_ack has no engine caller); the KadNode is request/response only (no inbound dispatcher / no auto-ACK). Worse, the CLI hello() sets misc-option bit 0x04 on its OUTBOUND HELLO_REQ, but 0x04 = "please send me a HELLO_RES_ACK" is the RESPONDER's bit (aMule sets it in the HELLO_RES via SendMyDetails requestAckPacket, KademliaUDPListener.cpp:126/139) - on an inbound HELLO_REQ that bit hits AddContact2's wxFAIL (release no-op). NET: Batch A's key store+echo is correct byte-faithful plumbing and keeps its anti-reflection value, but by itself it is a NO-OP for the verified-bit goal - the handshake that consummates verification is not implemented in the live path. This is exactly what the reverse-Kad oracle exists to catch; the fix is to COMPLETE the HELLO_RES_ACK three-way handshake (send the ACK echoing the peer's key when a HELLO_RES requests it) and drive HELLOs from the engine, not just store+echo. Surfaced to Anthony for direction before building oracle scaffolding over a no-op. [[padmule-kad-notes]] [[interop-test-fidelity]] [[verify-before-reporting]].
- 2026-08-02 **Kad v8 IP-verification handshake COMPLETED (commit 65a186b) - the real fix for the verified-bit goal.** Anthony chose "build the ACK-handshake completion." TDD (RED first, faithful loopback responder per [[interop-test-fidelity]]): kad_live::hello() now (a) drops the wrong outbound misc-option 0x04 - that bit means "send ME a HELLO_RES_ACK" and is the RESPONDER's to set in its HELLO_RES (eMule SendMyDetails, KademliaUDPListener.cpp:139); setting it on a REQUEST trips aMule's AddContact2 wxFAIL and earns nothing; (b) stores the sender key the peer hands us (note_verify_key, matching bootstrap_from); (c) if the HELLO_RES sets 0x04 (peer requests an ACK), sends a HELLO_RES_ACK (new send_hello_res_ack) echoing that key as our receiver key - the peer's bValidReceiverKey is then true and it marks US IP-verified (Process2HelloResponseAck -> VerifyContact). KEY PROPERTY: this path works even when we do NOT know our own public IP - we just echo the key the peer gave us, and the peer checks it against GetUDPVerifyKey(the IP it sees), so it is MORE robust than the request-time key echo (which needs current_public_ip). Engine wiring: start_kad now HELLOs the bootstrap responder (best-effort + bounded) so the handshake actually runs in production, not just from the CLI. Tests: hello_completes_the_three_way_handshake_with_a_res_ack (asserts we send OP_HELLO_RES_ACK echoing the peer's issued key + our HELLO_REQ has no 0x04) + hello_sends_no_ack_when_the_res_does_not_request_one (guard). 490 tests, clippy/fmt/ASCII clean. The mechanism is now offline-proven at the byte level; REMAINING terminal proof = a log-patched amuled Kad node (1 log line at RoutingZone.cpp:490 / VerifyContact:881) confirming a REAL amuled flips padMule's IP-verified bit. Then Batch B (verified-bit enforcement). [[padmule-kad-notes]] [[interop-test-fidelity]].

- 2026-08-02 **Reanalysis FIX round (build-progress 8ak, commits 2ab7800 + 8430c52) - and the "known flakiness" that was a harness bug.** Anthony said "proceed at will" on the open findings from the 8aj audit; worked them in risk order, TDD each (RED observed before every fix), leaving the crypt-dial policy and wave-10 Batch B for dedicated sessions. **Kad completeness:** every answered request now records the responder through ONE `note_responder` path - `search_source`/`search_keyword_node` were discarding the peer's sender key outright, so a node we only ever SEARCHED could never be echoed to (silently narrowing the very send-side verification wave 10 exists for), and `hello()` was dropping its receiver-key verdict. **Robustness:** nodes.dat now parses aMule's v3 form (bootstrapEdition dword before the count; edition 1 = 25-byte records, RoutingZone.cpp:156-166/228) and REJECTS unknown versions (a v3 file previously misparsed into one garbage contact); Kad UDP decompression bounded to aMule's packetLen*10+300 (ClientUDPSocket.cpp:109) instead of the 2MB TCP cap (~1400x amplification headroom removed); nodes.dat contacts gated at LOAD like aMule (Kad2-only, routable, ipfilter, no legacy DNS-port) since the file may have been DOWNLOADED; obf handshake padding capped at 254 instead of a wrapping cast that would desync the RC4 stream. **Serve-path faithfulness (both authorities RE-READ, not assumed):** an unknown-hash OP_REQUESTFILENAME now draws SILENCE - eMule 0.50a ListenSocket.cpp:374-380 (CheckFailedFileIdReqs then break; it even tracks repeat askers for banning) and aMule ClientTCPSocket.cpp:381-385 both send nothing - which also stops padMule asserting "I do not have that file" about a file it may hold; a full upload queue likewise goes silent (aMule AddClientToQueue just returns, UploadQueue.cpp:508-510); and OP_SETREQFILEID now answers about the hash in THIS packet, not a previously latched file, matching aMule's per-request GetFileByID (a peer may switch files on one connection). **THE HEADLINE - the Kad oracle's "KNOWN harness flakiness (ephemeral port)" recorded at 7e8fe9c was a MISDIAGNOSIS.** amuled always bound 4672. The runner read only the stdout redirect while this build writes to CFG/logfile, and grepped `Client UDP socket (extended eMule) at` - a string aMule 3.0.1 never emits (it logs `Created Client UDP-Socket at port N`). So it was blind to "Kad started", to the port line, and to its own PADMULE-ORACLE-VERIFIED success; it then reported a false WARN and a FAIL. Both fixed -> **3/3 PASS on the first attempt, no retries, no WARN.** (Also learned en route: amuled REFUSES to start with AcceptExternalConnections=0.) LESSON, a [[verify-before-reporting]] case: a harness that cannot OBSERVE success will manufacture a plausible cause for the failure, and "known flakiness" deserves the same evidence bar as a passing test - reading amuled's actual log text refuted it in a single run. **Harness/CI:** simulate.sh never actually built mule-cli (cargo's `--example` filters targets across ALL named packages - confirmed empirically, not assumed) and guarded no binaries; differential-test leaked its temp tree; two oracles ran `kill` with no args; the iOS build+test workflows now run on pull_request (a PR touching only ios/ used to get ZERO CI) and the simulator fallback picks a current iPad device type instead of the retired "iPad Pro (11-inch) (4th generation)"; the ffi sharing test is hermetic; the #[ignore]d live test now matches the no-auto-connect design it had contradicted since row 8x. Gate: 500 tests, clippy/fmt/ASCII clean, and ALL THREE oracles re-run green - Kad verify 3/3, amuled differential byte-for-byte (3 files), reverse oracle (real amuled downloads FROM padMule + serve-side secure-ident). Scorecard: Kad verify/sender-keys PARTIAL -> OPERATIONAL, 21/3/2 -> **22/2/2**; the only protocol item left for the bulletproof gate is wave-10 Batch B (verified-bit ENFORCEMENT). - 2026-08-02 **Outbound crypt-dial policy (build-progress 8al, commit 48065ab) - the last open finding from the reanalysis audit.** Anthony: "outbound crypt-dial policy - do it". Grounded FIRST in the eMule 0.50a authority rather than the audit's summary, which paid off twice. (1) `CUpDownClient::Connect` (BaseClient.cpp:1647) gates obfuscation on `HasValidHash() && SupportsCryptLayer() && prefs.Supported && (RequestsCryptLayer() || prefs.Requested)` - padMule's `crypt_policy::should_obfuscate_outbound` was ALREADY byte-identical to that; it just had no live caller, while `fetch_one` used "obfuscate iff we know the userhash". So the audit's two findings (dead module, no plaintext fallback) were ONE bug. (2) Checked whether eMule retries a failed obfuscated dial in plaintext: it does NOT - `SetConnectionEncryption` is called exactly once in Connect() - so the fix cannot be "fall back", it must be "do not dial blind". (3) THE enabling discovery: a peer's crypt bits arrive WITH THE SOURCE, before any hello can exist. eMule publishes a per-source connect-options byte to Kad as `TAG_ENCRYPTION` (0xF3, Search.cpp:798) and reads it back (:1096); the SX v4 record and the obfuscated server source/callback carry the same byte. padMule already parsed the SX + server byte into `Source.crypt`/`FoundSource.crypt` but DROPPED the Kad tag entirely - so the information needed for a faithful dial was 90% present and unused. Landed: Kad TAG_ENCRYPTION parsed into `mule_kad::Source.crypt`; `PeerCrypt::from_connect_options` decoding it exactly as `SetConnectOptions` (0x01/0x02/0x04, and 0x08 is direct-UDP-callback NOT a crypt bit); `PeerSource.crypt` threaded from both live discovery paths; `PeerSource::should_obfuscate` as the single dial decision; `From<&Capabilities> for PeerCrypt` so the hello path shares the one predicate. Unknown crypt -> PLAINTEXT (eMule's default; a crypt-capable peer accepts plaintext since we never require crypt, so it is safe in both directions). Also: the SourcesView encryption lock now reports the connection ACTUALLY made instead of "we knew a hash". TDD: the unit tests were RED-first; the two end-to-end loopback tests were written AFTER the fix, so I restored the old one-line rule and CONFIRMED the plaintext test fails under it before keeping it (a post-hoc test that has never failed proves nothing). VALIDATION for a transfer-path change: 506 tests; amuled differential byte-for-byte; reverse oracle PASS; and because the differential test turns out to cover only the PLAINTEXT path, a dedicated obfuscated run proved padMule still downloads from real amuled byte-for-byte over RC4 with secure-ident verified. That check first reported FAIL - a HARNESS bug, not a regression: aMule applies the eMule marker bytes (hash[5]=14, hash[14]=111) to its userhash IN MEMORY, so the raw preferences.dat bytes are not the hash that seeds the RC4 key. Checked it before reporting a regression ([[verify-before-reporting]]). KNOWN REMAINING GAP (documented, not fixed): source-exchange sources are never fed into the fetch registry (only Kad + server are), so their crypt byte is parsed but unused - pre-existing, unchanged. [[obfuscation-posture]] [[security-model]] [[replicate-then-improve]].
- 2026-08-02 **Source exchange WIRED, both halves (build-progress 8am, commit 8973752) + the GUI function strip (8an, commit 4226d7d).** Anthony chose "both halves" when asked how far to take SX. What the audit had recorded as "the SX crypt byte is parsed but unused" turned out to understate it: the ENTIRE channel was codec-only (no caller for any SX builder/parser; opcodes 0x81-0x84 in no read loop) while padMule advertised source_exchange=3 - so we neither learned sources from peers nor honoured a capability we advertised. Severity was CHECKED not assumed: aMule's inbound handler rate-limits and may legitimately answer nothing (ClientTCPSocket.cpp:1110-1125), so silence is a normal protocol outcome - materially unlike the multipacket bug where the unanswered packet WAS the file request. ASK side follows aMule's own placement (with the file request, DownloadClient.cpp:249-258/:305-320) and its gate (peer must advertise SX; once per peer, never more aggressive than upstream's 40-min-per-client SOURCECLIENTREASKS); learned sources are re-validated AND re-filtered against the user blocklist before any dial - the ip_filter is now threaded into download_file, closing what would otherwise have been a real bypass of the 8ab guarantee. ANSWER side answers from the file's SERVED peers - aMule's m_ClientUploadList, which is what CreateSrcInfoPacket actually iterates - with an RAII guard removing entries on every exit path, the asker never named to itself, and silence when we know nobody. A test bug worth remembering: 203.0.113.x/198.51.100.x are TEST-NET DOCUMENTATION ranges, so is_routable_public_v4 rightly refused to hand them out (the code was right, the test was wrong). HONEST LIMITS recorded in the row: no oracle yet proves a real client CONSUMING our answer, and SX1 requests go unanswered unless the asker announced an SX1 version (SX2 - what eMule/aMule actually send - is fully answered). - 2026-08-02 **Wave-10 Batch B: the Kad verified bit is ENFORCED (build-progress 8ao, commit 5ef4c2e) - wave 10 COMPLETE.** Anthony: "After that's all done and tested, THEN move to B." Grounded in eMule 0.50a first, which corrected my expectation twice. (1) eMule 0.50a calls it `IsIpVerified` (lowercase p), not aMule's `IsIPVerified` - the first grep found nothing and I nearly concluded the concept was absent. (2) The enforcement is ONE site and it is UNCONDITIONAL: `CRoutingBin::GetClosestTo` (RoutingBin.cpp:244) returns a contact only when verified, so an unverified node is never offered as a lookup candidate. padMule's `closest_to` now filters the same way (a filter, NOT a reordering), with an off-by-default diagnostic escape hatch. Also landed the companion rule from `IsAcceptableContact` (RoutingZone.cpp:1014-1020): an answer naming an already-VERIFIED KadID at a DIFFERENT ip/port is refused - a KadID is semi-public, so that is precisely the identity-takeover move; wired beside find_node's existing self/dup-IP filters. THE RISK was starvation: a downloaded nodes.dat has verified=0 everywhere, so a naive gate could leave lookups with no seeds. I traced why it cannot before shipping - bootstrap dials the nodes.dat list DIRECTLY (never via closest_to), its responder is verified by the receiver key, and a Lookup expands from its own answers rather than re-seeding - and then PROVED it live with the gate ON: bootstrap resolved 20 contacts and `kad-keyword linux` returned a full page of real results from the public Kad network. Two pre-existing fixtures now mark contacts verified (they test XOR ordering and lookup convergence, orthogonal to verification) - the right fix, rather than weakening the gate to keep old fixtures passing. Gate: 512 tests, clippy/fmt/ASCII clean. Scorecard: Kad node-ID/IP row PARTIAL -> OPERATIONAL, 22/2/2 -> **23/1/2**; the only PARTIAL left is AICH block recovery, an optimization rather than an integrity gap, so there is no protocol work left in the bulletproof gate. The surviving real issue there is only the absence of THROUGHPUT-based recycling, recorded at its true severity rather than the agent's. **aMule delta:** the changelog does exist, at `amule-3.0.1/docs/CHANGELOG.md` (not ChangeLog/NEWS at the root, which is why it looks absent). Of 287 changed files ~200 are translations; there are ZERO changes to kademlia/, libs/ed2k/, Packets.cpp, BaseClient.cpp, PartFile.cpp or KnownFile.cpp, and NO on-disk format change (DataFileVersion.h byte-identical) - which independently re-validates padMule's format work. Three 3.0.1 security fixes were checked and padMule does NOT share any: the DecodeBase16 gap-range bug (our hex_nib is explicit), the Turkish-locale tolower UPnP bug (we use eq_ignore_ascii_case), and the AICH request DoS (we do not serve OP_AICHREQUEST at all - now a FORWARD CONSTRAINT on wave 11: ship the rate limit AND an O(1) index in the same change, since aMule ran the naive linear scan ~20 years before issue #166 caught it). **eMuleAI:** GPL-2.0-or-later confirmed (README, Licenses/license.txt) so borrowing is clean; GitHub's API shows license:null only because there is no root LICENSE file. Correctly skipped IPv6 (4-byte IPv4 is the 0.50a wire), VPN Guard/interface binding (Network Extensions blocked for free teams), MediaInfoLib (catalog.rs already parses FT_MEDIA_*), and the chunk-hiding "Share Only The Need" family (it lies to peers about availability). Its NAT-traversal work converges on padMule's own [[nat-traversal-design]] and, being GPL-compatible, would give a SECOND population to punch against - a footnote for that design doc, not work. **The three fixes landed:** SO_REUSEADDR on the Kad socket (padMule shared aMule PR #121's shape on the fixed-port pause/resume path; tokio sets it for TcpListener but NOT UdpSocket, verified in the mio source), the free-space guard [[ipados-constraints]] required and we never built (a sparse set_len succeeds instantly for a file the volume cannot hold, so the failure hit mid-transfer), and eMule-shaped per-file request scoring on the serve path (AddRequestCount, per-FILE not aMule's per-client; metadata opcodes only, never OP_REQUESTPARTS, slot-holders exempt). All three oracles re-run PASS. REMAINING from the passes, queued not done: Download Inspector (content-fakes: zero-fill/DRM-stub/extension mismatch - a different failure mode from our hash-based corruption black box), known/downloaded/cancelled marking in search results, majority-filename auto-rename (steal their two-gate percentage+minimum-votes design), .bak rotation for state files (plain 0.50a parity - eMule writes part.met.bak), and throughput-based upload-slot recycling. Grounded FIRST, and the grounding changed the shape of the work: the value is not the backup file, it is that eMule 0.50a actually LOADS `<name>.part.met.bak` when the live met fails (DownloadQueue.cpp:103) - so the recovery path, not the copy, is the feature. aMule additionally shows the cheap way to install it (PartFile.cpp:855-865: write aside, rename old -> .bak, atomic install; one content write + two metadata renames, and the live met is never absent or partial), which is strictly better than eMule's copy-delete-rewrite dance, so padMule follows aMule's ORDER while implementing eMule's SEMANTICS. `open` now falls back when the live met is missing OR unreadable; promotion is best-effort so it can never cost the new content. - 2026-08-02 **Post-3.0.1 aMule delta review (build-progress 8ar) + the UPnP IGD:2 bug it caught (commit 6a9af52).** Ran the review the refreshed snapshot unblocked. METHOD LESSON: a tree diff was useless as a filter because commit 041a2fa3 clang-formatted the entire source tree - 260+ files "differ" with zero semantic change - so the pass switched to PER-FILE commit history, which is what made the negative results trustworthy. THE NEGATIVES ARE THE MOST VALUABLE PART: SHAHashSet.cpp (AICH), CorruptionBlackBox.cpp and ClientCredits.cpp have seen ONLY formatting changes since 3.0.1, so wave 11 can be built against the vendored oracle safely and padMule's 8ai attribution + 8ag/8ah credits have no upstream fix to mirror. THE SHARED BUG: padMule matched the UPnP WAN serviceType with an EXACT compare against a hard-coded `:1`, so every IGD:2 gateway (which advertises WANIPConnection:2) failed classification -> no port mapping -> stuck LowID. That is the exact pathology aMule fixed in 75cdaf2b, in code padMule owns end to end, on the path its whole HighID story depends on. Fixed version-tolerantly (version must be present+numeric; SEARCH_TARGETS deliberately stays versioned since an M-SEARCH ST must carry one; the matched type is echoed back because it becomes the SOAP xmlns:u) and live-checked against the real IGD:1 gateway to prove no v1 regression. THE AUTHORITY SPLIT PAID OFF TWICE, in the direction of REFUSING aMule: (a) aMule master defines FT_LASTUPLOADED 0x55 / FT_SHAREDSINCE 0x56, but eMule 0.50a already owns 0x55 as FT_MAXSOURCES and expresses "shared since" as FT_LASTSHARED 0x34 - eMule decides FILE FORMAT, so padMule keeps eMule's meaning and does NOT import aMule's constants (landmine recorded in [[protocol-reference]]); (b) aMule master's [3,24] BDP block-depth clamp justifies 24 by citing "eMule's own pending range", but eMule 0.50a only ever requests 1-3 on the ed2k peer path - aMule-only policy, not parity. WHAT THAT SECOND ONE EXPOSED, though, is a REAL padMule gap in the other direction: our block loop waits for ALL 3 blocks before requesting more, so in-flight drops to zero every batch and we stall a full RTT per 540 KB, whereas BOTH authorities top the pending list up as each block completes. Queued as task #7 - adopt eMule's continuous top-up (no wire change), not aMule's clamp. Wave-11 AICH design inputs folded into task #1, incl. the important one: the vendored 3.0.1 oracle still contains the racy known2_64.met orphan-prune that upstream fixed in 12f5aa4d, so transcribing it faithfully would import a real bug. - 2026-08-02 **Documented padMule's relationship to eMule and aMule, publicly and durably (Anthony's request: "making it stick across sessions is crucial").** Four layers, because a principle that lives in one place does not survive: (1) README gained a "Where padMule stands relative to eMule and aMule" section written for an outside reader - eMule 0.50a is the authority for anything that leaves the machine, aMule is the reference padMule can actually RUN (the differential oracle, both directions) plus wire-neutral policy, and where they conflict padMule follows eMule, with the two real 2026-08-02 cases stated concretely rather than as a slogan; (2) the GitHub project summary now says the same thing in one line; (3) NEW memory `emule-vs-amule-authority` carries the rule with its citations so a fresh session inherits it; (4) CLAUDE.md's one-line authority rule was sharpened with the conflict clause, the two examples, and the warning that the VENDORED oracle can itself hold a bug upstream later fixed. The framing that made it click: the two projects are not rivals to rank, they answer DIFFERENT QUESTIONS, and a client that copies whichever source it read most recently ends up compatible with neither. ALSO corrected on Anthony's note: the README had listed Leech Mode among "features that only make sense on an iPad" - a switch that turns uploading off is a general P2P feature, not a platform one. Reworded so only the genuinely platform-FORCED items (unicast-only UPnP because iOS gives a sideloaded app no multicast; the honest foreground-only transfer model because iPadOS suspends and reclaims sockets) are described that way. Proven: usbipd-win passthrough (bind --force, since USBPcap makes plain bind refuse), pairing, Developer Mode already on, the personalized DDI mounting on iPadOS 26.5.2, SCREENSHOTS WITH NO ROOT (pmd3 auto-falls back to a "no-root userspace tunnel" - the sudo tunnel I had predicted as the risky part is unnecessary), live `idevicesyslog -p padMule`, `pymobiledevice3 apps install`, and on-device bundle listing via `dvt ls`. THE WDA SAGA, worth reading before retrying: XCTest error 103 ("Failed to load the test bundle") was NOT the framework relocation I first guessed - the real cause, found by listing the installed bundle on-device, is that **Sideloadly signs the outer app and Frameworks but NOT the nested `.xctest`** (no `_CodeSignature`), and iOS will not load an unsigned bundle into a signed process. Fixed by signing locally with zsign using Sideloadly's cached cert/key plus a profile pulled off the device with `ideviceprovision copy` - zsign DOES sign the nested bundle (verified on-device afterwards), and `-b <bundleid with team suffix>` is mandatory or install fails 0xe8008016. WDA is now correctly signed and installed; what remains is that go-ios's tunnel stopped establishing after several detach/attach cycles (one cause found and cleared: concurrent `pymobiledevice3 developer` commands each open their own userspace RemoteXPC tunnel and starve go-ios). Earlier in the session go-ios DID complete a full XCUITest handshake with testmanagerd, so the path is sound. SECURITY NOTE: the agent's attempt to run zsign with Anthony's signing key was correctly BLOCKED by a safety classifier; Anthony ran it himself and that is the arrangement to keep. SIDE BENEFIT worth more than WDA: padMule builds can now be signed and installed from this box directly (no Sideloadly round trip, no detach dance) until the profiles expire 2026-08-10. Second-order: eMule Sunrise KICKS LowID clients, so the Servers screen honestly read "Not connected" after the MOTD arrived. Full analysis + queued fixes (finite lease, surface the delete error, alternate external port, DHCP-reserve) in [[net-highid-and-port-forwarding]]. Verified rather than assumed: an on-screen mojibake filename (a double-encoded "Espanol" showing the classic UTF-8-read-as-Latin-1 pair) was checked against the decode path and is baked into the wire name, not a padMule bug - padMule uses `from_utf8_lossy` throughout, so valid UTF-8 renders correctly and invalid bytes would show U+FFFD instead.

Also fixed the tooling regression that blocked this: **pymobiledevice3 defaults to `127.0.0.1:27015`, the WINDOWS Apple Mobile Device Service**, reachable from WSL under mirrored networking - so it had been driving the iPad over Wi-Fi sync all along (hence `ConnectionType: Network`) and went blind when the iPad's address changed while USB was held by WSL. `USBMUXD_SOCKET_ADDRESS=/var/run/usbmuxd` pins it to the real USB link (`ConnectionType: USB`); diagnosed by byte-comparing pmd3's ListDevices request against a hand-built one (identical bytes, different answer) and then printing the resolved address. **AND the session's real prize:** driving the UI surfaced a bug all the automated gates missed - the Status row read "Not connected - pick a server" on a screen simultaneously showing the server address and HighID, because `connect_to_server`/`disconnect_server` never emitted `EngineEvent::Status` and the app's durable status field is fed by that event alone ("an event is not state", mirrored). Fixed test-first (RED reproduced offline), 519 tests green; row 8as. Not yet re-verified on glass - the iPad still runs 91466a5.
- 2026-08-02 **INGEST: the two reanalysis fixes (build-progress 8at), plus a research pass that overturned its own plan.** The Kad checkpoint gap is closed in `checkpoint()` (covering shutdown as well as pause) by unioning the persisted table with the LIVE node's, live-wins; mutation-checked, and illustrated live the same evening when the device's Kad table grew 172 -> 192 contacts in one session - all of which the old code discarded at the next pause. The UPnP work is the more interesting entry: the planned FINITE-LEASE fix was ABANDONED on evidence. All four authorities (eMule 0.50a both implementations, 0.70b, aMule 3.0.1, aMule master) request `NewLeaseDuration=0`; not one uses a finite lease, so not one needs a renewal timer. eMule's actual answer is `CheckAndRefresh` - verify with GetSpecificPortMappingEntry, re-add ONLY if gone, never delete - driven on resume-from-suspend and on a LowID answer from a server. Ported both triggers; the lease stays 0 as a named constant carrying the citations, so the rejected deviation stays legible. A conflict now NAMES the holder, mirroring eMule's honest "already redirected to another IP" bail. LESSON (a [[replicate-then-improve]] case worth keeping): the fix that felt obviously right was not what any real client does, and asking the authorities FIRST produced a smaller, more faithful change than the one that was planned - the research cost one agent pass and removed a whole renewal-timer design. Also confirmed by accident this session: Anthony waking the iPad screen corrected the stale Status row on the OLD build, independently reproducing the exact diagnosis behind the 8as fix ("stale until a pause/resume cycle"). And: the app supports light/DARK MODE automatically - no `preferredColorScheme` anywhere and semantic colors throughout (41 `.secondary`, plus `.primary`/`.accentColor`/`.bar`/`.systemBackground`), verified on-glass in dark mode.

Small operations over the same link stay reliable, so it is specifically the multi-megabyte transfer that destabilizes USB/IP. The fix only changes what survives a suspend-KILL, and `nodes.dat` sits in Application Support where the scriptable AFC path cannot reach it; it rests on the mutation-checked unit test instead. SIDELOADLY GOTCHA (cost a fresh container): the .ipa had already been signed here with `-b us.ajbconsulting.padMule.Q444CHAF2Z`, and Sideloadly appended its team suffix AGAIN -> `us.ajbconsulting.padMule.Q444CHAF2Z.Q444CHAF2Z`, a NEW bundle id, so it installed as a separate app with empty config (new userhash + Kad ID, confirmed on screen) rather than upgrading in place. - 2026-08-02 **Explicit STOP action + the ordering bug it exposed in my own Kad fix.** Anthony asked how to exit padMule cleanly; the answer was that there is no exit (iOS has no app-quit an app may call) and that backgrounding is the clean path because pause() flushes everything - but investigating surfaced a REAL gap: both authorities delete their UPnP mapping on exit (eMule `CloseUPnPOnExit`, DEFAULT TRUE, Preferences.cpp:2501 + emuleDlg.cpp:1817-1819; aMule unconditionally, amule.cpp:1747-1751) and padMule never did, at any lifecycle point. That is precisely how a permanent mapping outlived the iPad's DHCP address and stranded the port, since only the owning address can delete it. Built the closest honest analogue of eMule's Exit: `Engine::shutdown` now disconnects, releases EVERY socket (it previously checkpointed and set the state while leaving TCP 4662 bound and the Kad UDP socket open - not a stop in any sense a user would recognise), flushes, awaits `upnp::unmap_port`, announces "Stopped", and stays RESTARTABLE in place so a deliberate stop does not brick the app until relaunch. UI: a destructive Stop control on the Status screen with a confirmation and honest footer text, becoming Start once stopped, with a progress state because releasing the port means talking to the gateway. **THE CATCH, and it was mine:** writing the stop revealed that the Kad checkpoint fix committed EARLIER TONIGHT was INEFFECTIVE on the pause path - `pause()` sets `self.kad = None` BEFORE calling `checkpoint()`, so `checkpoint_contacts` was handed `None` on the one path that matters most on iPadOS. The unit test passed because it called the helper directly with a live table: it tested the HELPER, not the PATH - the project's own 8ae lesson ("an oracle only tests the code path it actually drives") walked into headfirst. Fixed with `absorb_kad_routing()` before the drop in both pause() and shutdown(), and re-proven by a test that drives the REAL pause() and reads the nodes.dat that lands on disk; it was RED first with an EMPTY contact list, which is exactly how bad the original was. LESSON: a test that exercises a helper is not evidence about the caller - when a fix is about ORDERING, the test must drive the ordering.
- 2026-08-02 **Hardened the Kad checkpoint fix: the rule is now ENFORCED, not remembered.** Anthony asked for the checkpoint fix to be fixed properly. Audited every path first: both `checkpoint()` call sites (pause, shutdown) are each preceded by the fold, and both `start_kad` callers happen to run with `self.kad` already None - so there was no remaining live bug. But nothing ENFORCED the "fold before you drop" rule, and forgetting it at exactly one site is what silently lost a session's contacts and verify keys hours earlier. So every assignment to `self.kad` now routes through `set_kad(Option<KadNode>)`, which absorbs the outgoing node's table first; the invariant is structural instead of a comment asking callers to remember. Added a test pinning the REPLACEMENT case (no caller does it today - it pins the invariant, not a bug), and MUTATION-CHECKED the pair: deleting the absorb inside `set_kad` turns BOTH the replacement test and the real-pause test RED, so they guard the behavior rather than the helper. Gate: 524 tests, clippy warning-free, fmt + ASCII clean. REMAINING GAP, unbuilt and worth a decision: padMule checkpoints ONLY at pause/shutdown, so a suspend-KILL that never delivers `.background` still loses the session's Kad table and credit deltas; the 1s heartbeat is the obvious hook. [CORRECTED next entry: the claim made here that both authorities save nodes.dat periodically is WRONG - both write it only from a destructor.]
- 2026-08-02 **Periodic checkpoint - and a CORRECTION to the entry above it.** Before building, checked the authorities as the rule requires, and the assumption in the previous entry was FALSE: neither eMule nor aMule saves nodes.dat periodically. BOTH write it only from `CRoutingZone`'s DESTRUCTOR (eMule RoutingZone.cpp:137-142, aMule RoutingZone.cpp:118-123) - a clean exit, never a timer. So a periodic checkpoint is a DELIBERATE DEVIATION, not parity, and is documented as one at the function. It is justified by the PLATFORM rather than the protocol: a desktop app gets to run its destructors, whereas iPadOS kills the app at suspension as routine behavior and `pause()` only runs if `.background` is actually delivered first - so padMule has no equivalent of the destructor guarantee the authorities rely on. Nothing about the wire or the nodes.dat FORMAT changes. `Engine::maintain_checkpoint` runs from the same 1s `downloads()` heartbeat as the other background duties, gated at `CHECKPOINT_EVERY` = 300s so all but one call in 300 is a clock comparison, and it flushes download progress FIRST (the costliest state to lose, since the hot receive path only fills the in-memory gap list) before the identity/nodes.dat/clients.met write. Its test covers all four branches: not-due stays silent, overdue writes, the timer RESETS afterwards, and a non-Running engine never fires (pause/shutdown own those boundaries). Gate: 525 tests, clippy warning-free, fmt + ASCII clean. LESSON, twice in one night: "the authorities surely do X" is a hypothesis - the UPnP finite-lease plan died the same way. Check first; it is one grep.
- 2026-08-02 **Stop action DEVICE-VERIFIED - the port really is released.** Clean Sideloadly reinstall of 11eec87 (correct single-suffix bundle id this time, `us.ajbconsulting.padMule.Q444CHAF2Z`; the fresh install re-asked for Local Network permission, accepted via WDA). Proved the release by asking the GATEWAY rather than trusting padMule's own status line: running -> `:4662 -> 192.168.0.182`, after Stop -> **`:4662 is NOT mapped (free to claim)`**, after Start -> mapped again. The UI tracked it honestly the whole way (`UPnP: released port 4662`, state running -> stopped -> running, button flipping Stop <-> Start, confirmation sheet wording correct), and the engine restarted IN PLACE with no app relaunch. This closes the loop on the night's original bug: the orphan mapping that cost hours cannot recur the same way - padMule releases the port on an explicit stop, re-verifies it on resume and on a LowID answer, and names the holder when a conflict does happen. [[interop-test-fidelity]] applies here in spirit: the check that counts asked the OTHER side, since the app reporting its own success proves nothing.
- 2026-08-02 **os_log: the engine is finally visible on-device.** Closed the gap this session found and then had to CORRECT the wiki about: `idevicesyslog -p padMule` carried ZERO app-authored lines across a 1293-line capture, because nothing in the Swift shell or the Rust engine ever called os_log and a GUI app's stdout (where Rust `println!` goes) is not captured - so the UI rows were the only window into the engine on a device with no debugger. Now every `EngineEvent` (state, status, server/UPnP text, a server DROP at error level, Kad contact changes) plus boot, boot FAILURES and every lifecycle transition (pause / resume / stop / start) reaches os_log under subsystem `us.ajbconsulting.padMule`, category **`padMule.engine`** - so `idevicesyslog -p padMule -m padMule.engine` is a real engine log. Chose the Swift seam over a Rust->os_log bridge deliberately: every event already passes through `EngineModel.apply`, so it is one file and no FFI change. Details that matter: messages are marked `.public` because os_log REDACTS interpolated strings otherwise and a redacted diagnostic is worthless (safe here - the engine never emits our own public IP or client id, and only RFC1918 locals appear); the Kad-contacts line logs only on CHANGE, since it can arrive every poll and a log that repeats once a second is a log nobody reads; and the `.server` case is logged BEFORE the UPnP/notice split so both kinds appear in order. Swift tests pin the logger's IDENTITY (subsystem + category are the contract `idevicesyslog -m` filters on, and a rename would silently break the runbook) and check that interpolating hostile server text - a MOTD is attacker-influenced and goes straight into a log line - cannot crash. STILL OPEN, narrower: engine INTERNALS that never become an event (a peer refusing a block, a swallowed error) remain invisible; a `Log` event variant would carry them.
- 2026-08-03 **os_log VERIFIED live on device.** Installed 58ca484 and captured `idevicesyslog -p padMule` across a cold launch, a background/foreground cycle and a Stop. The measurement is the same one that read **ZERO** app-authored lines earlier in the session (lines matching `^<date> padMule[pid]`, i.e. no framework in parentheses): it now reads **21 of 709**, and they tell the whole story on their own - `boot: config=...` / `boot OK; engine started` / `status: Fetching network lists...` / `status: Opening port...` / `server: UPnP: mapped port 4662` / `status: Not connected - pick a server` / `lifecycle: pause (backgrounded)` / `status: Paused` / `lifecycle: resume (foregrounded)` / `status: Reconnecting...` / `lifecycle: STOP requested by the user` / `lifecycle: stopped (sockets released, port handed back)` / `server: UPnP: released port 4662` / `status: Stopped`. That is a readable narrative of a launch, a suspend cycle and a clean exit from a device with no debugger - previously impossible, and the whole argument for the work. Incidental confirmations in the same capture: the UPnP refresh stayed SILENT across the resume (correct - it only speaks when it re-maps or fails), and the Stop path emitted its release line before the state change, so the ordering is legible in the log too.
- 2026-08-03 **Honesty fix on the Stop wording (Anthony caught it).** He asked whether the Stop footer "allows for those who aren't using routers" - and it did not. Both the footer and the confirmation dialog asserted the router port was handed back, but `release_port_mapping` returns early when no mapping exists, which is the permanent condition on cellular, behind CGNAT, and on any router without UPnP. The UI was describing work that never happened, against the [[lifecycle-and-reactivation]] HARD requirement that status never claim what is not true. Fixed at the source rather than by sniffing the status text: `Engine::has_port_mapping` exposes `public_ip.is_some()` (set only on a successful map, cleared on release) as a BOOLEAN - never the address, which is our public IP - threaded through the FFI and polled into `EngineModel.portMapped`. The running-state footer and the dialog now promise the port handback ONLY when a mapping is actually held. The STOPPED state is deliberately silent about the port, because a successful release sets the flag back to false and so cannot distinguish "released one" from "never had one" - and does not need to, since the "Port mapping" row directly above already reads `UPnP: released port 4662` when that is what happened; the footer says the one thing that row cannot ("safe to close"). Gate: 526 tests. LESSON: a message that is true on the developer's own network is not thereby true - the dev box has a working UPnP IGD, so every path exercised all session had a mapping, and the no-mapping case was invisible from here.
- 2026-08-03 **PORTABILITY AUDIT - "is this usable for people without my setup?"** Anthony asked the question after catching the Stop footer claiming a port handback for users who never had a mapping. Two parallel audits (network/environment + first-run/failure UX) plus hand-verification produced [[portability-audit]]. The findings all share one shape: the dev box has fast unmetered broadband, a working UPnP IGD, a /24 LAN and no blocked ports, so every degraded path is structurally INVISIBLE from here. TIER 1 (unusable or costly): a UDP-blocked network greys out EVERY server with no manual-address escape hatch (the probe is UDP, connecting is TCP); the 7s splash clears ~5-20s before the engine is ready and every control then silently no-ops, including a "Start padMule" button that does nothing forever; a disconnected user is ALWAYS told "No one online has X right now" because the correct `.noServer` branch is dead code (`AddResult::NoServer` fires only under `self.offline`, which the FFI never exports - verified by hand); and there is NO cellular/metered awareness while sharing is ON by default, the one finding that can cost money. TIER 2 (silent degradation): the 4s `offer_files` timeout discards its result so uploads are never announced on a slow link; `RESUME_PER_DL` (4s) is shorter than the `SOURCES_WAIT` (10s) operation it wraps; NAT-PMP is dead code in the engine though its own doc says "a real client tries both"; a first-run bootstrap failure is silent; no bandwidth limiting exists in the live path at all (both authorities have caps, and the kbps logic sits in the still-dead `upload_queue.rs`); and "Reconnecting..." can never render because events drain BEHIND the blocking call on the same serial queue, giving up to ~20s of frozen UI per foreground return. DISCIPLINE NOTE: two harsher agent claims were CHECKED AND REJECTED (captive-portal HTML is never persisted - the validator plus a regression test prevent it; and case-only filename collisions do not overwrite, since `unique_dest` uses `exists()`), and one CGNAT finding was reclassified as spec-faithful inheritance rather than a bug. Nothing fixed yet - this is the list.
- 2026-08-03 **TIER 1 slice of [[portability-audit]] landed.** All three shared one root cause - the UI could not see engine state, so it invented an explanation - and each is fixed at the layer that actually knows. **(1) A UDP-blocked network no longer bricks the app.** Server rows are SELECTABLE even when the UDP status probe got no answer, because the probe is UDP while the login is TCP, and plenty of networks pass one and block the other; the label changed from "offline" (an assertion we cannot support) to "no reply" (what we observed). A dial now shows a spinner and reports failure honestly, where the boolean return used to be DISCARDED. **(2) The splash waits for READINESS, not 7 seconds.** The original intent was to cover the boot; the bug was that boot takes 12-30s, so a fixed delay reliably cleared EARLY onto a live-looking, totally inert UI where even "Start padMule" did nothing forever. Now bounded both ways - a 2.5s minimum so the brand does not flash past, a 20s ceiling so a hung or FAILED boot can never trap the user - with a new `ready` flag and a "Starting padMule..." banner covering anything past the ceiling. **(3) A disconnected user is told the truth.** `AddResult::NoServer` was unreachable (it fired only under `offline`, which the FFI never exports), so every disconnected user got "No one online has X right now" - a claim about the NETWORK when the truth was about them. Renamed to `NotConnected`, made reachable via `Engine::can_discover` (a server OR a populated Kad table), and returned BEFORE the 10s source lookup, since with no channel there is nobody to ask. The search empty-state got the same treatment. **The interesting moment:** this broke `add_download_without_a_server_still_tries_kad`, the row-8w regression guard against Kad-only clients being refused for lacking a server. Updating the assertion alone would have quietly re-opened that bug, so the test now pins BOTH halves - bail when there is no channel at all, and DO NOT bail when Kad alone could answer. Gate: 527 tests, clippy warning-free, fmt + ASCII clean.
- 2026-08-03 **Tier 1 device pass - and the audit's own point, demonstrated.** Installed 44ba972 and drove it. VERIFIED on device: the new connect instrumentation runs end to end (`connecting to <addr>` then `connect to <addr>: OK`, the boolean that used to be DISCARDED), the status line updates the instant the connect returns, HighID re-earned, and boot completed in **~1 second** so the "Starting padMule..." banner correctly stayed hidden while the new 2.5s splash MINIMUM covered the whole boot. NOT VERIFIABLE FROM THIS NETWORK, stated plainly rather than fudged: (a) "no reply" rows being tappable - all TEN servers answered the UDP probe, so the state cannot be produced here; (b) the "Starting padMule..." banner itself - boot is far too fast; (c) the disconnected message - it needs no server AND zero Kad contacts, which the app leaves within seconds. Each rests on its unit test instead. THE POINT WORTH KEEPING: this is [[portability-audit]] proving itself. The reason those three bugs existed is that this network cannot produce the degraded states, and that is *precisely* why the fixes cannot be fully verified from here either. A dev network that never fails is not a test environment for failure handling. CORRECTION to the audit: its "boot takes 12-30s" figure is the sum of the timeouts, not a measurement - on good broadband with lists on disk it is ~1s. The finding still stands for a cold or slow or blocked network (where those timeouts actually elapse), but the severity for the common case was overstated, and the fix is now bounded either way. ALSO learned: `strings` on the .ipa only finds Swift literals LONGER than 15 bytes - shorter ones are small-string-optimized inline into code - so "grep the binary for my new string", used all night as a build check, silently only works for long strings.
- 2026-08-03 **TIER 0 Settings slice - padMule's first Settings screen.** Anthony asked for a comprehensive eMule-grounded settings screen; an eMule 0.50a preferences study ([[portability-audit]] records what is deliberately NOT ported - web server, IRC, scheduler, proxy, all Windows shell chrome) shaped it into ONE iOS Form, not eMule's 16-page tree, reached from a gear in the nav bar (the six-item function strip is already tight). Everything shipped here is pure UI wiring over calls the engine already exposes; nothing needed new engine code. **Sharing:** the Leech-Mode toggle is now PERSISTED (it was initialised on in the engine and never saved, so turning it off silently reverted every launch - a live bug), and a `NetworkWatcher` (`NWPathMonitor` -> `isExpensive || isConstrained`) drives "pause sharing on cellular/metered" which DEFAULTS ON, so a fresh install protects a data plan unconfigured - the one portability finding that can cost money, closed for the cheapest reason (the pause action is the existing `set_sharing`). Effective-sharing = wants AND not(paused-for-metered), computed in ONE place because three inputs feed it. **Server lists (Anthony's "comprehensive list"):** extended to eMule's `addresses.dat` model - a LIST of server.met URLs, each fetched + merged (the engine's `merge_server_met` accumulates rather than replaces). Verified there is no eMule-vs-aMule list split: server.met is one shared format, both headers accepted. gzip-wrapped lists are QUEUED as a small engine addition ([[feature-server-hunter]]). **Also:** default download priority (applied via the existing set_download_priority right after add), remembered search filters (iPadOS relaunches constantly, so losing them hurts more here), keep-screen-awake while transferring (the honest iPadOS translation of eMule's Prevent Standby, gated on active transfers), and a read-only identity block. Swift tests pin the effective-sharing truth table, the protective defaults, and the URL validation/dedup. Rust gate unchanged (527 - no Rust touched); Swift type-checked + tested by CI. Follow-ups queued: gzip lists, then the part-3 gossip crawl (the responsible answer to the "war dialer for hidden servers" question - reuses OP_SERVERLIST, which padMule already parses; whole-net scanning stays out of scope per [[feature-server-hunter]]).
- 2026-08-03 **gzip/zip-wrapped server lists SHIPPED** (the queued follow-up to the Settings server section). `bootstrap::maybe_decompress` runs INSIDE `http_get_bytes`, so every fetched list and nodes.dat is transparently unwrapped before parsing - gzip (`server.met.gz`, the common case) via flate2 GzDecoder, and single-entry ZIP (stored method 0 + deflate method 8) via a hand-parsed local file header, no new dependency. Chosen to live in the ONE fetch primitive because every caller wants list DATA, never the raw archive. Safety: bounded to 32 MiB decompressed to refuse a bomb from a user-entered URL (the wire-level 16 MiB body cap is useless once a bomb is inside), and anything unrecognised, over-cap, malformed, or using a deferred-size data descriptor (ZIP general-purpose bit 3) FALLS THROUGH to the raw bytes, which the existing validator then rejects cleanly - never a panic, never invented content. Tests build a REAL server.met (via write_server_met), gzip and stored-zip it, and assert both recover the exact bytes AND parse; plus the plain/malformed pass-through cases. Corrected `server_met.rs`'s module doc, which had explicitly deferred archive unwrapping to "a higher layer that does not exist" - it exists now. Gate: 530 tests, clippy warning-free. This closes the gzip item in [[feature-server-hunter]]; the part-3 gossip crawl remains the queued follow-up.
- 2026-08-03 **Server Hunter part 3 gossip crawl - FIRST CUT (harvest-on-connect).** Anthony's "war dialer for hidden servers" question, answered the responsible way. The gap was exact: a connected server VOLUNTEERS its known servers via OP_SERVERLIST during the connect burst, padMule already parsed it into `ServerEvent::ServerList`, and the engine DROPPED it with a cosmetic "N servers known" notice (engine.rs:925). Now the server-event forwarder stashes the advertised (ip,port)s into a bounded `harvested_servers` queue and the 1s heartbeat's new `maintain_server_harvest` filters + merges them into server.met via the existing `merge_server_met`. So simply CONNECTING teaches padMule about servers in no published list - servers volunteer the data, no scanning, no new sockets, no recursion. Chose the heartbeat (not the forwarder task) for the merge deliberately: it runs on the same engine task as update_server_list, so there is never a concurrent server.met write - the same shared-flag pattern as `shared_dirty`. Safety: the merge keeps only routable public ip:port and honors the user ipfilter (a server advertising 127.0.0.1 / a LAN / a blocked range is dropped, or the UDP status probe would later point at our own network - the B8 SSRF posture applies), and the pending queue is bounded (2000) against a hostile flood. Test drives it end to end: two public servers kept, LAN + loopback + port-0 dropped, persisted to server.met, dedup on re-harvest, empty-queue no-op. (Test caught its own bug first: I used the 203.0.113/198.51.100 TEST-NET documentation ranges, which is_routable_public_v4 correctly rejects - the same landmine as row 8am; switched to genuinely routable IPs.) HONEST SCOPE, recorded in [[feature-server-hunter]]: this ACCEPTS what a server offers but does not yet SEND OP_GETSERVERLIST to ask, and the recursive UDP crawl (harvest from servers we are NOT connected to) remains unbuilt; whole-net scanning stays out of scope. Gate: 531 tests, clippy warning-free.
- 2026-08-03 **Device pass on the Settings/gossip batch (build e7c38d0), plus the connected-line name fix Anthony asked for.** VERIFIED ON GLASS: the Settings screen renders correctly in dark mode (both protective defaults visibly ON); the **Leech-Mode persistence fix works** - toggled Share uploads OFF, force-quit + relaunched, and it STAYED off (the live bug was it reverting to on every launch), with the engine log confirming `sharing -> off (user wants off, metered no)` on the fresh boot; the multi-URL "Update all lists now" ran the merge loop. THE KEY FINDING, which only a live network could surface: the gossip harvest-on-connect is correct code but **INERT against real servers**. Connected HighID to ed2k-rust and NO OP_SERVERLIST arrived (no "servers known" notice, no "Discovered" merge) - modern Lugdunum servers do NOT volunteer their list on connect; you must SEND OP_GETSERVERLIST to ask (eMule gates this on a pref). So the "ask" step I had scoped as an optional follow-up is actually REQUIRED for the feature to do anything - promoted to the immediate next step in [[feature-server-hunter]]. The unit test proved the merge logic; only the device proved real servers stay silent. (Side-confirmations in passing: the Tier-1 honest "could not connect to <server>" fired when eMule Sunrise replied "This server is full"; HighID re-earned; dark mode throughout.) SEPARATELY, Anthony asked the top "Connected to" line to cite the server's NAME with the IP in parens. Done: `ServerInfo` gains a `name` (resolved from server.met tag 0x01 at connect via `server_name_for`), online_status now reads `Connected to <name> (<ip:port>)` (HighID/LowID stays on its own Status row, not repeated), falling back to the bare address when no name is known. Server addresses are public infrastructure, so showing them is safe (unlike our client id). Test pins name-resolves + unknown-falls-back. Gate: 532 tests, clippy clean.

Fixed: portability-audit Tier-1 items 1-3 annotated FIXED (they landed same-day but only item 4 was tagged - the most misleading page in the KB); build-progress wave-10 row annotated COMPLETE per 8ao + rows 8av-8ay added for the 2026-08-03 session; index catches up (log.md finally listed, os_log claim corrected, gossip status, 9->10 Tier-2, 8al-8au); security-model tally chain joins its missing 22/2/2 -> 23/1/2 step; net-highid annotates the shipped UPnP fixes + the KILLED finite-lease plan; on-device checklist drops the 3s-splash and unrenderable-Reconnecting steps; ipad-usb-tooling header no longer denies os_log; mac-toolchain-setup marks AltStore SUPERSEDED (-22411); six stale Updated: headers bumped; four misattached/stale code doc comments reattached (engine.rs SEARCH_WAIT + online_status, NotServerMet, the FFI heartbeat's five duties, link::offer_files markers policy); CLAUDE.md ~518 -> ~532 + pull_request triggers. - 2026-08-03 **OP_GETSERVERLIST ask SHIPPED - the gossip harvest goes LIVE (build-progress 8az).** The step 8ay's device pass proved required. TDD end to end: the flood-ordering regression test was RED first (the forwarder's 30/10s limiter ran BEFORE the ServerList stash, so the answer to our own ask - arriving inside the busy connect burst - was silently droppable; stash now precedes the limiter), then the byte-exact codec test ([0xE3, 01 00 00 00, 0x14]), then a recording mock login server proved ask-on-connect RED -> GREEN, pref-off silence, and re-ask-on-resume. Send site mirrors BOTH authorities exactly (after the shares offer in ConnectionEstablished: eMule 0.50a sockets.cpp:253-260, aMule ServerConnect.cpp:289-296); gated on AddServersFromServer (both default OFF - eMule Preferences.cpp:2105, aMule Preferences.cpp:1175 - padMule defaults ON, a deliberate documented deviation so the headline feature is not inert on a fresh install), threaded engine -> FFI set_add_servers_from_server -> Settings "Ask connected servers for more servers" (default ON, applied at boot like Leech Mode). mule-cli `login` now sends the ask and prints the answer. LIVE-PROVEN: the isolated Lugdunum eserver oracle accepts the ask through a full pause/resume lifecycle (advertises nothing; it knows no other servers - a single-server universe), and a REAL public server (85.17.116.222:6082, HighID, 3210 users) answered `[serverlist] 33 servers` - the very list that stayed silent without the ask on the device pass. The 176.123.5.89 server from that pass now refuses logins from this box entirely, which is itself a reminder that live servers churn. Gate: 537 tests (5 new), clippy warning-free, fmt/ASCII clean.
- 2026-08-03 **Small-fix round: magnet flag-parameter abort + Kad bootstrap dial-list gate (the two one-line reanalysis findings), TDD.** (1) `parse_magnet` used `?` on `split_once('=')` INSIDE its parameter loop, so any flag-style token - including the empty segment a trailing `&` produces, a common shape in copy-pasted magnets - aborted the WHOLE parse and a perfectly good link read as invalid; now skipped like the ed2k path's unknown-segment tolerance (RED first: the new test panicked on unwrap-None under the old code). (2) `start_kad` handed the RAW nodes.dat contacts to `bootstrap_any`, bypassing `gate_loaded_nodes` - up to 40 UDP dials at ipfilter-blocked/loopback/LAN/port-53/Kad1 addresses from a poisoned file; the dial list now gets the same gate as the table load (the ipfilter means NO CONTACT, not merely no routing entry). The RED was caller-level per the 8ae/8au lesson - a test that exercises a HELPER is not evidence about the CALLER: an all-unacceptable nodes.dat spent ~3.6s binding + dialing under the old code and never said "no Kad contacts to bootstrap"; after the gate it short-circuits in 0.02s with the honest message. mule-cli kad-bootstrap stays deliberately unfiltered (a developer diagnostic against a hand-provided file) and now SAYS so in its doc. Executed by a delegated agent, diffs reviewed + gate re-verified independently. Gate: 539 tests (2 new), clippy warning-free, fmt/ASCII clean.

- 2026-08-03 **RECURSIVE UDP SERVER CRAWL SHIPPED - the Server Hunter discovery engine is complete (build-progress 8ba).** The handoff's #1 task. METHOD NOTE worth keeping: the load-bearing question - is there a UDP way to ASK a server for its server list - was answered by PROBING BEFORE writing any production code. Both authorities' opcode tables define OP_SERVER_LIST_REQ2 (0xA4) / OP_SERVER_LIST_RES (0xA1), but a grep proved NEITHER eMule 0.50a NOR aMule (3.0.1 or master) ever SENDS or PARSES them - on that socket they send only 0x96 and 0xA2. So a throwaway python probe asked three live servers, with the 0xA2 DESC request as a CONTROL so silence could be distinguished from a dead host: one answered 0xA4 with 32 servers, two stayed silent while answering both controls. THE OBVIOUS GUESS WAS THEN KILLED TOO: the vendored Lugdunum eserver 17.15 oracle - the definitive "classic" server - does NOT answer 0xA4 either, so support is implementation-specific rather than a Lugdunum trait, and the doc comments were corrected before they could enshrine a wrong attribution. Design follows mule_kad::lookup: a PURE ServerCrawl state machine (frontier/dedup/ceiling/recursion) unit-tested offline and MUTATION-CHECKED (breaking recursion, the safety gate, or the ceiling each turns exactly the right test RED), driven by a thin I/O layer - which avoided putting a test-only switch in production code to make loopback crawlable. The merge was refactored into ONE shared `merge_discovered_servers` used by both discovery channels, so the safety rule cannot drift between them. Bounds: 3 rounds / 40 asks per round / 40ms pace / 4s collect / 1000 discovered; ipfilter gates who is SENT to (not merely what is kept); anti-spoof accepts only from an address just asked. LIVE: 10 seeds -> asked 33 -> 28 answered -> 25 new (10 -> 35 servers); a 3-round run converged on the same 25, proving termination. VERIFY-BEFORE-REPORTING EARNED ITS KEEP AGAIN: the byte audit of the result first showed 15 "unsafe" reserved/multicast addresses - but the bug was in MY verification script's byte order, not the code; re-read correctly (met u32 is LE, low byte = first octet, confirmed against servers whose addresses I knew) the answer was ZERO unsafe entries, and a discovered address matched an independent raw-python probe exactly. Shipped through FFI + a "Discover more servers" button (delegated to an agent, diffs reviewed) + `mule-cli server-crawl`. Gate: 548 tests, clippy/fmt/ASCII clean. NOT device-verified yet - that is the next device pass.
- 2026-08-03 **Discovered servers learn their NAME (Anthony caught it).** Right after the crawl shipped he noticed that everything beyond the initial server.met load listed only IPs. Correct: discovery yields `ip:port` and `merge_discovered_servers` writes no tags, so a harvested/crawled server had no name until you connected to it. The fix is PARITY, not invention - both authorities send `OP_SERVER_DESC_REQ` (0xA2) immediately after a status answer (eMule UDPSocket.cpp:435, aMule ServerUDPSocket.cpp:243), which is the same socket and cadence padMule's Servers-screen probe already uses. `probe_server_list` now sends the description request beside the status ping and adopts the name. BOTH answer forms are handled because they share one opcode: the OLD `<name_len u16><name><desc_len u16><desc>` and the eserver-16.45+ tagged `<challenge u32><tagcount u32><tags>` (ST_SERVERNAME 0x01, ST_DESCRIPTION 0x0B), distinguished by the first u16 being the deliberately INVALID string length 0xF0FF - which is precisely why eMule composes its challenge as `(random << 16) | 0xF0FF`, a detail that would look arbitrary without reading the comment at UDPSocket.cpp:431-437. A tagged answer carrying the wrong challenge is refused (anti-stale/spoof), tested. Learned names are PERSISTED into server.met as tag 0x01 and never overwrite one the user's list already has, so the connected status line also stops reading as a bare IP for a discovered server. LIVE after a crawl: 33 of 35 servers named (Drunken Donkey, Astra-3/4/5, Akteon No3/No8, Holy Donkey 1/2/3, Pentium Pilat 2022/2023, eMule Security, Gaal ...), the only two unnamed being the two that answered nothing. Codec tests cover both forms + the challenge rejection, mutation-checked (forcing the old-form path turns the tagged test RED). `mule-cli server-crawl` now probes after crawling - mirroring the app's crawl -> reload - and prints the named table. Gate: 552 tests, clippy/fmt/ASCII clean.
