The fresh install re-raised the Local Network permission prompt, which was accepted before proceeding. VERIFIED: "Discover more servers" renders on the Servers screen beside Refresh/Prune; tapping it produced the notice **"Crawl asked 33 server(s), 30 answered - 25 new"** and the table grew **Servers (10) -> Servers (35)** - matching the dev-box run almost exactly (33 asked / 28-30 answered / 25 new across three runs, so the reachable graph from that seed is stable). Scrolling the whole list showed **32 of 34 distinct rows NAMED** (Drunken Donkey, Astra-3/4/5, Akteon Server/No3/No8, Holy Donkey 1/2/3, Pentium Pilat 2022/2023, Gaal, Don Pedro, eDonkey Server 1/2/3, eMule Angel/Cosmic/Security, aMule Server, MO-Server, three MO-ad-free ...), the only two unnamed being the two that answered nothing at all - so Anthony's "discovered servers have no names" finding is closed ON THE DEVICE, not just in a CLI run. NOTE: the iOS accessibility tree exposes only the VISIBLE cells of a lazy list, so the first read showed 4 rows and the full picture needed nine swipes with dedup - worth remembering for any future on-glass list assertion. Also observed: a server name may contain non-ASCII (a lightning glyph in three "MO-ad-free" entries) - untrusted server DATA rendering correctly, which the repo's ASCII-only rule does not govern. The ~10s crawl block was not perceptibly worse than a search, though it is the same serial-queue freeze tracked as portability Tier 2.
- 2026-08-03 **USAGE-FEEDBACK ROUND (build-progress 8bb): Anthony's first extended session produced 18 items and SEVEN confirmed bugs.** METHOD: four parallel investigations reproduced every claim in code before anything changed, which mattered - two of his items were questions whose honest answer was "no, and here is why". **The headline finding is one no test could have produced: resume was broken ONLY when Kad was HEALTHY.** `find_sources` joins its server and Kad arms (so it returns in max(), as its own comment says) and the Kad arm carries a 15s budget; `resume_fetches` wrapped the call in a 4s timeout that discarded EVERYTHING on expiry, including server sources that had already arrived. A populated Kad table therefore guaranteed the discard while an empty one let resume work - and `add_download` calls the same function with no outer timeout, which is precisely why adding a file worked and resuming the identical file did not. That asymmetry was the smoking gun. Fixed by making the budget a PARAMETER that bounds each arm, so partial results always survive; the mutation test measures the old bug exactly (10.001s against a 600ms budget). Two siblings fixed with it: only 2 downloads were ever attempted per foreground return (8s pass budget), and there was NO retry anywhere in the engine - `spawn_fetch` was reachable only from start/resume/add_download, so a download that missed its window or ran out of rounds was frozen until relaunch. **Second serious bug, an interop one: phantom shared files.** The library is verified against disk only at `start()`, and the serve path looked up purely in memory - so a file deleted in the Files app was still announced via OP_OFFERFILES, answered "COMPLETE" to a requesting peer, granted an upload slot, and then dropped the connection when the read failed. Fixed at both ends (serve-path verify -> honest FNF; a 60s prune of library + known.met). The mutation proof is literal: without the check the peer receives opcode 0x50 where 0x48 belongs. **A LESSON WORTH KEEPING: my first two resume tests PASSED under mutation and were worthless.** One asserted a return value that was false for other reasons; the other never exercised the Kad arm because the test had no Kad node. Rewritten to assert the interval STAMP (proving the early return actually happened) and to drive a real silent server - both then failed under mutation, one of them by measuring the 10s bug. Same family as "a test that exercises a HELPER is not evidence about the CALLER", but new: a test can exercise the right CALLER and still not exercise the MECHANISM. Also of note: adding the serve-path disk check correctly broke nine tests that used `/does/not/matter` as a shared-file path - they now write REAL files, which makes them faithful to a live serve rather than merely green. Oracles re-run after the serve change: amuled differential byte-for-byte on all three files, reverse oracle PASSES (real amuled downloads FROM padMule, secure-ident verified) - which is what proves the new check does not refuse a legitimate upload. Also shipped from the same list: per-op spinners (Discover more servers had zero feedback for ~12s), results count, entry numbers + back-to-top, connected-server highlighting (it was rendering DIMMER, being a disabled row), the add-category label, percentages + Transfers sorting, the Status/Server collapse he designed (with the server NAME finally threaded over the FFI), a two-network health panel, and search-result provenance - which needed engine work, because origin was destroyed when three channels were flattened into one untagged vec before dedupe; it is now an OR-accumulated bitset, since a popular file legitimately arrives from BOTH. Gate: 559 tests, clippy/fmt/ASCII clean.
- 2026-08-03 **UI half of the feedback round + the CI lesson it taught (commits e744bbf, 47974b9).** Every remaining item from Anthony's list shipped across four parallel Swift batches: search rows deriving status LIVE (a completed file now shows Have, and a file just tapped no longer reverts to a tappable "Get"), pause() taking a beginBackgroundTask assertion plus a refresh in-flight guard so the 1s poll cannot stack jobs ahead of it on the serial queue, keep-awake gated on bytes actually moving, a Downloaded tab listing finished files from DISK with ShareLink (there was no open/share affordance anywhere in the app), results count, entry numbers + back-to-top, the connected server bolded and tinted (it had been rendering DIMMER, being a disabled row), the add-category label, download percentages + Transfers sorting, the Status/Server collapse Anthony designed, a two-network health panel, and the provenance badge. ONE JUDGEMENT CALL WORTH RECORDING: the keep-awake fix originally gated on the single most recent rate sample, which would have let the display sleep during the normal quiet between block batches (padMule is stop-and-wait per batch) - and sleeping there suspends the app and kills the very transfer the setting protects. Widened to a 5-sample window; the fix for the root cause is the queued continuous block-request top-up. **CI LESSON:** the iOS app BUILT green while the TEST BUNDLE failed to compile - `SearchHit` gained an `origin` field after the test helpers were written, and Swift memberwise inits require every field in declaration order. Swift is type-checked ONLY in CI on this box, so the local grep-and-read pass verified that every symbol EXISTS but could not catch a changed initializer SIGNATURE. Rule: wait for the iOS TEST workflow, not just the build, before calling a Swift change good. Fixed in 47974b9; all three workflows green.
- 2026-08-03 **Sideloadly could not see the iPad - and the runbook was WRONG about the fix.** Anthony reported Sideloadly not acknowledging the device. The obvious suspect (WSL is holding it) was checked FIRST and cleared: `lsusb` and `idevice_id -l` both showed nothing, so WSL did not have it - which makes it look like Windows should. `usbipd list` found the real state: the iPad sits at `Shared (forced)`. Because USBPcap is installed on this host, the original bind had to use `--force` (documented as gotcha 1 since the runbook was written), and a FORCED bind hands the device to the USB/IP stub driver at the WINDOWS level - so the Apple Mobile Device Service, and therefore Sideloadly and iTunes, cannot see it EVEN WHEN IT IS NOT ATTACHED TO WSL. The runbook's standing advice ("run `usbipd detach` before a Sideloadly install") is therefore INCOMPLETE on this box and would have sent anyone in circles, since detach was already effectively true. The release is `usbipd unbind --busid <id>` from an ADMINISTRATOR PowerShell; the agent's own attempt returned "Access denied; this operation requires administrator privileges", so this one is genuinely Anthony's to run. Two further traps recorded in [[ipad-usb-tooling]]: the bind PERSISTS ACROSS REBOOTS, so this recurs every time the box does WSL device work and then a Sideloadly install; and the BUSID is not stable (2-4 when the runbook was written, 7-12 now) so it must be read from `usbipd list` each time. Build 47974b9 staged to Downloads meanwhile, verified arm64 + unsigned + carrying the new strings ("Discover more servers", "server + kad", the Downloaded tab).
- 2026-08-03 **UPnP retry fix + a VPN question that turned out to be a padMule question (build-progress 8bc, commits 238267d + 3ff394c).** Closed the last open UPnP finding from the reanalysis: both triggers that exist to RECOVER a missing port mapping - a foreground resume, and a server answering LowID - early-returned when `public_ip` was None, so a `start()` map that simply FAILED (dropped SSDP answer, momentarily busy gateway) left the session LowID for its whole life with no retry short of a Stop/Start. The two paths designed to fix a missing mapping were precisely the two that refused to run when one was missing. Now a pure `port_mapping_action(running, offline, have_mapping)` decides (Refresh / Map / None), so the rule is testable without a gateway, and `public_ip` became an Arc<Mutex<..>> so the SPAWNED retry can record what it creates. Repeated failures stay silent by design. Mutation-checked. PROCESS SLIP, recorded rather than quietly fixed: the first commit went in while clippy reported ONE warning (an unused `mut` the refactor orphaned) - the count was glanced at, not read; fixed immediately in 3ff394c. **VPN:** Anthony asked to be reminded which VPN is best for padMule; there was NO prior recommendation anywhere in the KB, and saying so beat inventing one (the only prior hits are eMuleAI's VPN Guard, deliberately skipped, and an eD2k server's MOTD ad for "VPN with port forwarding (for High ID)" that scrolled past during a live login test). The real content is a padMule fact and is now recorded in [[net-highid-and-port-forwarding]]: a VPN REPLACES the UPnP mechanism, so everything hinges on provider port forwarding (Mullvad and PIA have removed it; Proton is desktop-only; AirVPN/TorGuard/Windscribe were the iPad-usable options as of the 2026-01 cutoff - verify, providers drop this quietly) - AND padMule's listening port is HARDCODED to 4662, so an arbitrary provider-assigned port cannot earn HighID until the backlogged "port override" setting ships. That makes port override a PREREQUISITE for the VPN story rather than a nicety.
- 2026-08-03 **Lint: the KB stop-check caught index drift the session had accumulated.** Two code commits (238267d/3ff394c) had landed without a wiki update, which is what the check exists for - but chasing it surfaced worse: [[index]] still described the recursive UDP crawl as "future work" (it shipped as 8ba the same day, and was device-verified), and its [[build-progress]] summary stopped at the gossip-crawl FIRST CUT, omitting 8az-8bc entirely (the OP_GETSERVERLIST ask, the recursive crawl + server names, the seven-bug usage-feedback round, and the UPnP retry). Both corrected. LESSON: the entry that gets edited when a feature lands is the feature's OWN page - the INDEX summary of it is the one that silently rots, because nothing forces you back to it. Worth a dedicated look during any lint pass rather than trusting that "I updated the docs".
- 2026-08-03 **VPN readiness (build-progress 8bd): AirVPN researched, then the two features it actually requires.** Anthony clarified he had discussed VPNs with me in another venue (his phone), which is why nothing was in this KB - a useful reminder that "no record here" means exactly that and nothing more. RESEARCHED rather than recalled, since VPN feature sets rot fast: AirVPN lets the user CHOOSE ports >= 2048 (so eD2k's own 4662 is requestable if free), forwards TCP and/or UDP per port, configures forwarding SERVER-SIDE in Client Area -> Ports so it is client-agnostic and therefore iPad-usable (official WireGuard app + Config Generator via QR/.conf), and - the design-determining fact - supports REMOTE-TO-LOCAL mapping, where remote port n can reach a DIFFERENT local port x. That forced the advertised-vs-listen split: a VPN replaces the router-forwarding mechanism padMule's whole HighID story runs on, so peers must be told the EXTERNAL port while padMule binds the local one. Landed set_ports(listen, advertised, kad) + set_upnp_enabled(on) through the engine, FFI and Settings; UPnP now no-ops when off instead of emitting a failure line the tunnel makes meaningless. **THE LESSON OF THE ROUND:** a mutation making the login advertise the BIND port instead of the ADVERTISED one passed the ENTIRE 561-test suite. Nothing local can see that error - everything still binds and connects on this box - while every real peer would dial a dead port. Fixed by asserting the value ON THE WIRE, reading it back from the login request's fixed offset against a recording mock server; it now fails loudly (left 4662, right 51234). Same family as the earlier resume-test failure this session: a green suite measures what you thought to check, and the checks that matter for interop are the ones that read the bytes a peer would actually receive. Also caught + fixed pre-commit this time (unlike 238267d): a non-snake-case test name clippy flagged - the gate is warning-free and a count must be READ, not glanced at.
- 2026-08-03 **The VPN port override shipped INERT, and the catch came from an agent's footnote (commit 65261ff).** The Settings agent flagged an iOS quirk in passing: `.keyboardType(.numberPad)` has no Return key, so `.onSubmit` may never fire from on-screen typing. Chasing whether the value would still be honored - it would, since the binding writes UserDefaults on every keystroke - surfaced the ACTUAL defect one layer down: `boot()` calls `e.start()` BEFORE `applyLaunchSettings()`. Ports take effect when the listener BINDS, and every launch constructs a fresh engine holding the 4662 defaults, so the user's port would not have been applied one launch late - it would NEVER have been applied. The whole feature was decoration. Fixed by pushing ports + the UPnP toggle inside boot's work block before `start()`, with a stored 0 falling back to the eD2k default rather than binding port 0. LESSON: a subagent's "flagging this, not something I embellished" aside is worth pulling on - the quirk it reported was cosmetic, the question it raised was not. And: ordering bugs in initialization are invisible to unit tests and to CI (this compiled and the suite stayed green); the only tell was reading the call sequence.
- 2026-08-03 **VPN-drop guard (build-progress 8be): a footnote question turned into a real privacy defence.** Anthony sent a photo of AirVPN's iOS download page and asked (a) which client to use and (b) whether the footnote should concern him. WireGuard is the answer to (a) - the OpenVPN tiles are legacy iOS-version targets, and WireGuard is faster, lighter on battery and survives network handoffs; the one caveat is that it is UDP-only, so a UDP-blocked network needs an OpenVPN-over-TCP fallback (the same class of network [[portability-audit]] Tier 1 exists for). For (b), the footnote makes two claims of very different weight: the GPL/App-Store half explains why AirVPN ships no iOS app and is a non-issue for a user (the official WireGuard app is GPLv2 and on the store) - amusingly the same friction that makes padMule sideload-only - while the "Apple processes can bypass any VPN tunnel" half is TRUE but does not affect padMule's own traffic, which is ordinary app-level TCP/UDP, not an Apple system service. **The consequence that actually matters, and that neither of us had named, is the missing kill switch:** stock iOS has none, so a dropped tunnel silently reverts to the real interface and padMule would keep seeding from the real address with the advertised port now wrong - privacy exposure and LowID at once, invisible. Anthony chose auto-pause AND a loud warning. Built on a fact already in hand: a HighID client id IS the public address, so comparing it across logins detects the drop with no new network work. Privacy by construction - compared internally, never emitted, the event carries NO payload, and a test asserts no address-like string reaches any user-visible text. LowID logins are exempt (they carry no public address; without the exemption every LowID server would false-pause - mutation-checked). Wired at connect AND resume, and mutation-checked AT THE CALLER with a mock server that hands out a different HighID on the second connect. Honest limit written down: switching VPN exit servers or Wi-Fi-to-cellular trips it too, which is a false positive but in the safe direction.
- 2026-08-03 **AirVPN: port 4662 is NOT available, which validates the port-override work.** Anthony imported the WireGuard config and tried to reserve 4662; AirVPN answered "The requested port is not available" - another subscriber holds the most famous eD2k port. So the hopeful path ("4662 is >= 2048 and therefore requestable, in which case padMule needs no change") is DEAD in practice, and the port override shipped in 8bd turns out to have been a hard prerequisite rather than a nicety: without it padMule simply could not have used AirVPN. Recorded in [[net-highid-and-port-forwarding]] along with the clean setup that follows from it - reserve ONE port with BOTH TCP and UDP, leave "Local" empty for same-port forwarding, and set padMule's listen = advertised = kad = that port. Using one number for TCP and UDP is protocol-legal (separate namespaces; the 4662/4672 split is convention, not a requirement), so a single reservation covers peers AND Kad and leaves four of the five spare. Also verified while answering: padMule's transient UDP sockets (status probe, global search, the crawl) bind EPHEMERAL ports, so moving the TCP port shifts nothing else - there is no local "TCP+3" socket to keep in step, despite the eD2k convention noted in [[protocol-reference]]. Config-generator guidance given: keep WireGuard UDP 1637 (the alternates matter only if a network blocks it), keep MTU 1320 (fragmented UDP is invisible packet loss for Kad), and DO NOT set Keepalive to 0 - persistent keepalive is what holds the tunnel's NAT mapping open, and an open mapping is precisely what HighID depends on.
- 2026-08-03 **AirVPN port 4662 is NOT available - which turns the port override from a nicety into the thing that made AirVPN usable at all.** Anthony imported the WireGuard config and tried to reserve 4662; AirVPN answered "The requested port is not available", i.e. another subscriber already holds the best-known eD2k port. So the hopeful branch recorded earlier that day ("4662 is >= 2048 and therefore requestable, in which case padMule needs no change") is dead in practice, and the override shipped in 8bd was a hard prerequisite. Guidance recorded in [[net-highid-and-port-forwarding]]: reserve ONE port with BOTH TCP and UDP, leave "Local" empty for same-port forwarding, and set padMule's listen = advertised = kad = that port - one number for TCP and UDP is protocol-legal (separate namespaces; the 4662/4672 split is convention, not a requirement), so a single reservation covers peers AND Kad and leaves four of five spare. Verified while answering, because it would have been an easy wrong assumption: padMule's transient UDP sockets (status probe, global search, crawl) bind EPHEMERAL ports, so moving the TCP port shifts nothing else - there is no local "TCP+3" socket to keep in step despite the eD2k convention in [[protocol-reference]]. Config-generator answers given from the screenshots: keep WireGuard UDP 1637 (the alternate ports matter only if a network blocks it; OpenVPN TCP 443 is the fallback for a UDP-blocked network); keep MTU 1320 (a fragmented UDP datagram dropped in transit is invisible packet loss, and Kad is UDP); and DO NOT set Keepalive to 0 - persistent keepalive holds the tunnel's NAT mapping open, and an open mapping is precisely what HighID depends on, so disabling it would look like a random slide from HighID into unreachability.
- 2026-08-03 **The port fields were unusable, and the bug is a general lesson about text input. (Anthony found it immediately on device.)** Symptom: typing 5999, or clearing the field first, made the old value reassert itself. Cause: the field's Binding did `get` = read UserDefaults and `set` = write UserDefaults ON EVERY KEYSTROKE. So clearing the field produced an empty string, which the setter treated as "restore the default" and wrote 4662 straight back, which the getter then re-rendered - the user was typing against a value that kept restoring itself. Fixed by the pattern every other field in the app already used: edit a LOCAL @State buffer, seeded on appear, validated exactly once at COMMIT. Commit is driven by focus LEAVING the field plus onDisappear, and a keyboard-toolbar Done button - because `.numberPad` has NO Return key, so `.onSubmit` never fires (an agent had flagged that quirk in passing two rounds earlier; it was the same root cause surfacing a second time and I had only half-heard it). A rejected entry now snaps to the value actually in force rather than silently lying. AUDITED THE WHOLE APP for the same shape, as Anthony asked: every other TextField (server-list URL, search query, results filter, new-category name, rating comment) already binds a plain @State or @Published string, and the size filters are preset MENUS with no free text. The custom get/set Bindings that do exist are all TOGGLES, which are safe with write-through because a toggle yields a complete value with no intermediate state to fight. So the port fields were the only instance. LESSON: write-through-on-keystroke is fine for a control whose every intermediate value is valid (a toggle, a picker) and WRONG for free text, where "partially typed" and "temporarily empty" are states the user must be allowed to occupy.
- 2026-08-03 **Function-strip reorder + a REAL "Open" for finished downloads (commit 7374bd8).** Two asks from Anthony while the VPN setup was in progress. (1) The strip is now Servers, Status, Search, Downloaded, Transfers, Shared, Stats - his order, and it reads left-to-right as the sequence the app is actually used in: pick a server, confirm you are on it, search, see what arrived, watch what is moving, then the two reference screens. Only the enum DECLARATION order matters (CaseIterable drives the strip); the title/shortTitle/icon/body switches are keyed by case and needed no change - verified all seven cases still covered, with shortTitle exhaustive via `default`. The app still LANDS on Search; changing the landing screen was not asked for and was left alone deliberately. (2) He asked whether opening a finished file in the appropriate app was handled - the honest answer was "partly": the Downloaded rows carried only a ShareLink, which reaches "open in" through the share sheet but is an extra hop behind a share icon. Tapping a row now opens the file directly in QuickLook. THE CONSTRAINT WORTH REMEMBERING: iOS refuses to let an app hand a `file://` URL from its own container to another app via `UIApplication.open`, so for a downloaded file there are exactly TWO routes - the share sheet, or QuickLook - and there is no "launch app X with this file" to reach for. QuickLook is the one that matches what a user means by "open" (renders video, audio, PDF, images, text and documents natively) and its own toolbar carries the share button for handing off, so ShareLink stays alongside it as the distinct "send it elsewhere" intent. QLPreviewController is UIKit, so `QuickLookView.swift` wraps it in a UIViewControllerRepresentable - there is no SwiftUI equivalent at the iOS 16 deployment target - and XcodeGen picks the new file up from padMule/Sources with no project change. CI green on all three workflows.
- 2026-08-03 **Toolbar Stop/Start, a How-to-use screen, and the title bar (commits 8437bc8 + a6e1a93).** Stop/Start is now in the toolbar beside Settings and reachable from EVERY tab, keeping the Status control as it was - same red stop.circle / blue play.circle and the same confirmation, so the two read as one feature in two places. Anthony's reasoning is the right one: Stop is the thing to do before closing padMule, and having to find the correct tab first is exactly when it gets skipped. **Moving it exposed a bug in the making:** `confirmStop` drove a `.confirmationDialog` attached to `statusScreen`, and a confirmationDialog only presents while its HOST view is on screen - so the new toolbar button would have silently done nothing from any other tab. Moved to the NavigationStack root (where the kick and VPN alerts already live) and the wording now says you can start again "from the toolbar or the Status screen". Worth generalising: a sheet/alert/dialog bound to state that a GLOBAL control mutates must be attached at the root, or it is dead from everywhere else - the same shape as the earlier note that the kick alert may not surface while a sheet is open. New `HelpView.swift` behind a questionmark.circle: what padMule is; THE ONE RULE (it only runs while open, because iPadOS suspends backgrounded apps); a five-step walkthrough from picking a server to opening a finished file; Stop/Start and WHY it matters (iPadOS gives an app no way to quit itself, so closing from the app switcher can leave a stale router forward - Stop releases it cleanly, and progress is saved either way); HighID vs LowID and what each costs; sharing, including the two cases padMule pauses it on its own (metered link, public-address change); the two networks; a line per remaining screen; and where files actually live. Written to be read rather than skimmed, and every claim is behaviour the app really has - a help screen that lies is worse than none. Title bar now reads `\__ p a d M u l e __/` (the leading backslash escaped in the Swift literal; verified by DECODING the literal rather than eyeballing it). Build a6e1a93 staged to Downloads, verified arm64 + unsigned + carrying the session's work. NB two verification strings read as absent under `strings` - "Kad port (UDP)" and "Stop and Start", both 14 chars - which is the documented Swift small-string trap (literals of 15 bytes or fewer are stored inline); confirmed in source instead, as [[ipad-usb-tooling]] warns.
- 2026-08-03 **Two on-glass fixes: the title bar ate its backslash, and the Activity row was a verbatim duplicate.** (1) `.navigationTitle("...")` with a string LITERAL takes a `LocalizedStringKey`, which performs escape processing - so the leading backslash of `\__ p a d M u l e __/` was swallowed and the device showed `__ p a d M u l e __/`. My pre-commit check had decoded the SWIFT literal correctly (`\\` -> `\`) and concluded it was fine, which was true about the string and false about what SwiftUI would do with it. Fixed with `Text(verbatim:)`, which bypasses localisation entirely. RULE: any user-facing string containing a backslash, `%`, or other escape-significant character must go through `Text(verbatim:)` or a String variable, never a bare literal, or SwiftUI will reinterpret it. (2) The Status screen's "Activity" row rendered a verbatim duplicate of "Server". It was added to preserve the engine's transient text ("Fetching network lists...", "Opening port..."), guarded by `status != connectionSummary` - but the guard compares the engine's FULL sentence ("Connected to eMule Sunrise (176.123.5.89:4725)") against the pure verdict ("Connected"), so once connected it ALWAYS differed and always showed. Removed at Anthony's call; nothing is lost, because Paused/Stopped/Reconnecting are already the Status verdict and the boot-time messages happen behind the splash and the "Starting padMule..." banner. LESSON worth keeping: a "show it only when it differs" guard has to compare against the thing the user will actually SEE next to it, not against a different representation of the same fact.
- 2026-08-03 **First on-glass confirmation of the whole feedback+VPN batch (build a6e1a93/89d8a2c).** Anthony's photos of the running app show the reordered function strip in his order (Servers, Status, Search, Downloads, Transfers, Shared, Stats), the Status screen reading **Connected / HighID / 146 Kad contacts** against a real Lugdunum server (eMule Sunrise 176.123.5.89:4725), and the new two-network health panel green on BOTH networks with the server MOTD rendering in the notice banner. That is the first device proof that the seven-bug usage-feedback round (8bb), the UI batch (8bg) and the VPN-readiness work (8bd/8be) all run correctly on the device - none of it had been on glass before. NOT yet exercised on device: the resume fix (needs a background/foreground cycle with an active download), the VPN path itself (needs the tunnel up with port 5999), and the Downloaded tab's QuickLook Open. Two defects the same photos caught are fixed in 89d8a2c and recorded as 8bh.
- 2026-08-03 **Four on-glass corrections: a stale UPnP row, default ports, banner placement/styling, and the landing tab.** (1) **The "Port mapping" row still read "could not map port 4662" after everything was set to 5999** - and the cause is instructive: `map_port` returned SILENTLY when the UPnP toggle was off, so the DURABLE row kept whatever the last attempt had said, naming the OLD port, which looked exactly like the new settings had not taken. Now it emits "UPnP: off - port forwarding is handled outside padMule", so the row states the truth instead of a fossil. GENERAL SHAPE, seen twice today: a durable UI field fed only on success/failure goes stale when the code path that feeds it starts early-returning - the silent path must still SAY something. (2) App-level default ports are now 5999 (Settings defaults, boot fallbacks, field placeholders) at Anthony's request for convenience while on AirVPN; the engine keeps eD2k's 4662/4672 as its own constants for anything not driven by the Settings screen. (3) **The blue server banner was pinned above EVERY tab.** A server MOTD is often a dozen lines and it persists, so it pushed real content down on screens it had nothing to do with. Split the two kinds: engine `.server` NEWS (MOTD, discovery results) now goes to `serverNotice` and renders only on the Servers tab, dismissable; feedback for an action the user just took stays in `notice` and stays global, because that has to appear where they are. (4) Anthony liked the blue banner's look, so ALL banners now use that solid treatment (tinted gradient, white text) instead of the washed 15%-opacity fill - which was only ever legible in the blue case. The COLOUR still carries meaning (blue informational, orange needs attention, red broken); only the weight changed, deliberately, since making an error banner blue would trade legibility for prettiness. (5) The app now lands on **Servers** rather than Search: padMule does not auto-connect, so picking a server IS step one, and landing on Search offered a box that could not answer yet.
- 2026-08-03 (trivial) **CI caught a Swift type error the local pass structurally cannot see (commit d7d555a).** `.listRowBackground(Color.blue.gradient)` fails to compile: `Color.blue.gradient` is an `AnyGradient`, which is a ShapeStyle - `.background()` accepts one, `.listRowBackground()` requires a VIEW. Wrapped in `Rectangle().fill(...)`. Recorded because it is the third distinct instance today of the same standing limitation: Swift is type-checked ONLY in CI on this box, so grep-and-read verifies that symbols EXIST but never that they TYPE-CHECK (the earlier two were a changed initializer signature after SearchHit gained a field, and a string literal being reinterpreted as a LocalizedStringKey). Rule stands: for any Swift change, wait for the iOS BUILD and TEST workflows, not just the build.
- 2026-08-04 **Fresh handoff written for a new session - and the sweep found UNMERGED WAVE-11 WORK.** Anthony asked for a new handoff and for any OLD ones to be deleted. In the main tree there is exactly ONE (`docs/wiki/handoff-next-session.md`, a living doc replaced wholesale by convention), so nothing to delete there. But searching the repo turned up a SECOND copy inside `.claude/worktrees/wave11-aich` - and that worktree is not leftover clutter: branch `worktree-wave11-aich` (LOCKED) carries **6 unmerged commits implementing AICH block recovery**, the last PARTIAL on the security scorecard, ~3,400 lines across a materialised AICH hash tree, a new `known2_met.rs` codec, the 0x9B-0x9E packet codecs, serve-side root/recovery answers, download-side recovery with per-BLOCK corruption attribution, and an end-to-end wire-loop repair proof - plus 4 files with UNCOMMITTED edits, and it sits 12 commits behind main. NOT deleted, obviously. The lesson is about the ask, not the code: "delete the old ones" was a reasonable instruction that would have destroyed a substantial branch had it been executed literally, because a git worktree LOOKS like a stale duplicate directory. Always check `git worktree list` + `status` + `log main..HEAD` before removing anything that resembles a copy of the repo. The new handoff now LEADS with that worktree, since the previous one listed wave 11 as effectively unstarted, which was simply wrong.
- 2026-08-03 **WAVE 11 SHIPPED: AICH block recovery, and with it the last PARTIAL on the security scorecard (24/0/2).** The queued handoff task, picked up because the iPad was not attached (the higher-ranked device-verify items were unrunnable). Three research passes first - the eMule 0.50a wire authority, the aMule delta, and a padMule integration map - and the shape of the work came straight out of them: the tree itself already existed and was byte-validated (row 8u), so the wave was the EXCHANGE around it. What the research bought, concretely: aMule 3.0.1 has a known2_64.met orphan-prune that RACES its own catalog registration and destroys freshly written hashsets (master fixed it by gating the prune to startup, ThreadTasks.h:136-144), so padMule took master's discipline structurally - prune_orphans runs once, from start(), after the catalog is loaded, and every store mutation goes through one index lock, so the race class cannot exist rather than merely not happening. And eMule 0.50a has NO serve-side throttle on the AICH path at all, while aMule added one - so padMule ships the rate limit WITH the O(1) root->offset index, which is what makes one 38-byte request cheap instead of a full store scan. **THE TERMINAL PROOFS are what make this more than "the tests pass":** padMule's known2_64.met ENTRY bytes are byte-IDENTICAL to what real amuled 3.0.1 writes for the same 10MB file (a straight cmp, upgrading the golden from root-only to the full store layout), and `mule-cli aich-probe` drives the real exchange - 0x9E/0x9D then 0x9B/0x9C - against the running amuled and verifies the recovery payload with the engine's own code. The download side is thus proven against a faithful other side, per [[interop-test-fidelity]], not against a mock that could be wrong the same way. **THE ADVERSARIAL REVIEW (three lenses) FOUND NINE, and the two that matter most were invisible to every test I had written:** (1) a FALSE BAN of the source REPAIRING the file - once a part is re-gapped the sweep starts re-filling it, so a recovery pass reading bytes mid-write calls the rescuer's block bad and bans it, which is precisely the false positive the sole-contributor rule exists to prevent; blame now comes from a contributor snapshot FROZEN at blame time and skips any block the re-fetch has touched (aMule added a mutex for the same interleave, issue #586). (2) a poisoned trusted root could LIVELOCK a download forever - fill, whole-file MD4 fails, re-blame, fill - because nothing re-checked the part's MD4 after recovery "completed" it; eMule does exactly that check and drops a disproved root into a terminal AICH_ERROR, and now so does padMule. Also fixed: the request-counter exemption was a MIS-TRANSCRIPTION (upstream exempts a peer WE are downloading FROM, GetDownloadState() != DS_DOWNLOADING, which a hostile peer cannot elect - padMule exempted a peer holding OUR upload slot, which anyone can elect by asking, then hammer the most expensive answer we serve for free); the counter was separately dodgeable by rotating the wire hash, with an unbounded map behind it; recovery claims had no owner, so a stale claim's late answer could apply over the new claimant's; and the multipacket sub-op walker assumed ExtendedRequestsVersion 2 when the shape is the SENDER's version. **THE LESSON, again and sharper:** my first false-ban test PASSED under mutation and was worthless - the block I had the rescuer "re-fetch" was clean on disk, so it was never judged at all. A test can name the right scenario, sit in the right file, and still exercise nothing. Only after corrupting the byte INSIDE that block did the mutation turn it red. **PROCESS NOTE:** four wiki edits landed in the MAIN checkout by mistake (a cd into the repo root instead of the worktree) while a PARALLEL session was landing rows 8bf-8bi there; caught by noticing the handoff cite a row range I had just claimed, reverted cleanly, and the branch rebased onto the advanced main - the row here is 8bj, not the 8bf I first wrote. [[security-model]] [[padmule-amuled-oracle]] [[build-progress]] row 8bj.

- 2026-08-04 **Independent sweep of the finished wave-11 AICH branch (reanalysis + fix round).** Reviewed the branch as an outsider rather than its author, rebased it onto main (clean; only one main commit since the merge-base touched `crates/` at all), re-ran the gate and the oracles, and read the AICH work against eMule 0.50a directly. The transcription itself held up under checking - MINUNIQUEIPS_TOTRUST=10, MINPERCENTAGE_TOTRUST=92 and the `dwIP & 0x00F0FFFF` /20 vote mask are exact (SHAHashSet.cpp:42-43,:523-532,:955-1031). **Two real defects survived the branch's own review anyway.** The serve-side answer was UNREACHABLE on the path real clients use: `is_upload_request` omitted 0x9B/0x9E, so `classify_inbound` returned `Other` and the listener dropped the connection - while eMule's `SendAICHRequest` goes through `SafeConnectAndSendPacket` (queue + `TryToConnect`, BaseClient.cpp:2402-2414) and `RequestAICHRecovery` picks a RANDOM source from `srclist` with NO connection filter (PartFile.cpp:6087-6106), making the dial-in case the common one. And `localize_corruption`'s `pending.entry(p).or_insert(..)` dropped the fresh per-block contributor map on a SECOND corruption round, leaving the stale one to be blamed - a false ban of an innocent earlier source, the exact invariant the attribution design rests on. **THE LESSON, third occurrence:** both the 8ad multipacket bug, the 8ae classifier bug and this one are the same shape - a test that hands the serve function its packet directly can never discover that the connection dies before reaching that function. An oracle or test aimed at a helper is not coverage of the path production takes to get there. **PROCESS NOTE:** a PARALLEL session was finishing the identical task in the same worktree while this sweep started (it committed under this session between two of its own reads); detected from the worktree transcripts, stood down to read-only until that session landed row 8bj, then took over. Two agents in one worktree is a data-loss shape - check `git worktree list` against running sessions before writing. [[build-progress]] row 8bk, [[security-model]], [[interop-test-fidelity]].

- 2026-08-04 **Device-verification session (row 8bl).** Merged wave 11 to main (PR #1, rebase - main is 377+ commits with ZERO merge commits, so linear was the convention to keep), installed f3bc022 on the iPad, and drove the app over WebDriverAgent. THE RESUME FIX IS PROVEN ON GLASS at last, and non-vacuously: Kad was healthy (190-204 contacts), which is the only condition under which the old bug bit. **TOOLING CORRECTION, worth keeping:** `usbipd bind` is NOT needed to reach the iPad from WSL - `pymobiledevice3` talks to Windows' Apple Mobile Device Service directly, so the device is reachable with the host still owning it. Only the older libimobiledevice CLIs (`idevice_id`, `idevicesyslog`) need the local usbmuxd socket and fail. Anthony caught this; [[ipad-usb-tooling]] should not imply the bind is a prerequisite. Also: the iPad is now on **iPadOS 26.6**, not the 26.5.2 the wiki records. UPnP now defaults OFF (88b0983). VPN HighID still unresolved, but narrowed to the AirVPN side by testing TWO servers. [[build-progress]] row 8bl.

- 2026-08-04 **Researching a feature found a shipped bug (row 8bm).** Asked to build continuous block-request top-up, the first step was pinning what eMule actually does. It tops a 3-block window back up on EVERY completed block and re-states the whole window - which is only viable because the uploader dedups (AddReqBlock, UploadClient.cpp:665-680). padMule's serve loop did not, so it was sending each block to a real eMule up to THREE times. **The lesson is about the harness, not the bug:** both oracles passed the whole time, because duplicate bytes still verify byte-for-byte - a pass/fail transfer harness cannot see a BANDWIDTH defect at all. Worth remembering next to the wrong-role and wrong-path failures already in [[interop-test-fidelity]]. Also this session: UPnP now defaults OFF (PR #2), and the VPN LowID was diagnosed to the AirVPN rule - `Connection refused` rather than a timeout proves the tunnel delivers to the iPad, and the rule targets local 4662 while padMule listens on 5999. Top-up itself and the source-origin indicator are scoped but NOT built. [[build-progress]] row 8bm.

- 2026-08-04 **The AirVPN 'Local port' field cannot be blank - and that is what held padMule at LowID.** [[net-highid-and-port-forwarding]] told the reader to leave it EMPTY for same-port forwarding. The form REFUSES to save a blank value and silently keeps the previous one, so the rule kept an old 4662 while appearing blank, and AirVPN delivered remote 5999 to local 4662 while padMule listened on 5999. Corrected in both places it was stated. **The diagnostic worth keeping:** `Connection refused (111)` from AirVPN's port checker, rather than a TIMEOUT, is proof the packet traversed the tunnel and reached the device, which actively answered 'nothing listening here' - so the tunnel, the keepalive and the routing were never suspects and only the port number was. A timeout would have meant the opposite and sent the search somewhere else entirely. Two servers both reporting LowID had already ruled out a per-server callback quirk.

- 2026-08-04 **Top-up + origin badge (row 8bn).** Closed open item 7. The interesting part is the DEVIATION: eMule re-states its whole 3-block window on every completed block and relies on the uploader to dedup, but padMule asks only for the NEW blocks - uploaders append requests rather than replacing them, so it pipelines the same while halving the chatter and removing any double-count risk. Faithful to the BEHAVIOUR (window always full, depth 3) without copying a wire habit that only works because of a check the other side happens to have. Proven against a real amuled uploader, 15MB multipart included. **Test technique worth reusing:** the mock withholds work until a second request arrives, so the OLD behaviour deadlocks rather than merely producing a worse number - a timing/efficiency change made into a hard pass/fail, with a timeout converting the deadlock into a readable failure. That is the answer to 'how do you test a latency optimisation'. [[build-progress]] row 8bn.

That directory was untracked and NOT ignored, so a `git add -A` would have committed 28MB into a PUBLIC repo - including `eDonkey-ed2k_study.pdf` (SANS, no-repost) and `edonkey-2000-gui-1.4.6.exe` (proprietary MetaMachine), the two things the 2026-07-20 archaeology ingest EXPLICITLY decided not to vendor for copyright reasons. So the ignore rule enforces a decision this log already recorded, rather than being mere hygiene. `CLAUDE.md` and [[arch-upstream-amule]] both still pointed at the old `/mnt/c/Users/ajbuf/Downloads/` path; corrected. ALSO RETIRED: `padmule-firewall.ps1` is dead - it opened inbound TCP 4662 / UDP 4672 on the WINDOWS host for the dev box at 10.0.0.33, a topology gone since the BE9700 replaced the manual chain (2026-07-17); padMule now runs on the iPad, on port 5999, reachable via AirVPN's forward. The two rules it created are still INSTALLED and are now pointless inbound holes - remove with `Get-NetFirewallRule -DisplayName 'padMule*' | Remove-NetFirewallRule` in an elevated PowerShell.

The serial-queue item is HALF done and the row says so: splitting the event drain onto its own queue fixes the symptom the handoff named (`Reconnecting...` cannot render) because `drainEvents` never touches the engine mutex - but `Engine::search` still holds `&mut self` across a 20s join, so the numbers still freeze. **Resisting the temptation to call that item closed is the point:** the remaining work is making `server` and `kad` independently shareable, which is a genuine architectural change to the most concurrency-sensitive code in the tree, and this project's own history (row 8ae, the moved-guard lesson) says a hot-path reshape earns a dedicated pass with an adversarial review. - 2026-08-04 **Device-verified rows 8bn/8bo on the 27ed681 build.** HighID on FIRST connect (eMule Sunrise, 49,923 users) - the AirVPN Local-port fix holds. The SOURCE-ORIGIN BADGE renders live: a 47.9MB download showed `Sources: 2 ed2k / 3 kad`, and a 787.6MB row with no connected sources showed NO badge rather than `0 ed2k`, which is the omit-empty-channels design working. Two small files (11.3MB + 3.4MB) finished in under 50s. **Two things that LOOKED like bugs and were not:** `No transfers` while a Downloading banner showed - the files had simply completed and moved to Downloaded; and a 2.36GB ISO refusing to start with `No one online has ... right now` - the search row's `7 srcs` is the SERVER INDEX count, stale and aggregate, while padMule reports what it can actually REACH. Both are the honest behaviour the Tier-1 portability slice built. **Answered from source, worth recording:** enabling `Search all servers (global)` does NOT disable Kad - `Engine::search` joins all three arms concurrently and the global flag gates only the third, so global ADDS a channel on top of the connected server and Kad.

- 2026-08-04 **The origin badge exposed a pre-existing hole: called-back sources were never recorded.** Anthony spotted it on glass immediately - a transfer at 676 KB of 787.6 MB with NO source indicator. My first explanation (no sources connected, badge omitted by design) was WRONG and he was right to push: bytes were arriving, so something was feeding it. Cause: `note_source` had exactly ONE live caller, `fetch::fetch_one` on the OUTBOUND sweep. A peer that reaches us by CALLBACK goes through the listener's `InboundKind::Source` arm straight into `download_from_peer_at`, so it was recorded nowhere - missing from the origin badge AND from the per-source sheet, since 8i. Fixed by recording it there, with origin Server, which is not a guess: `lowids` is built from the server's OP_FOUNDSOURCES list and the poke goes over the server link, and padMule implements no Kad-mediated callback. **THE POINT:** removing that call again compiles with ZERO errors - nothing structurally catches a missing side-effect at a call site, which is exactly why it shipped and why only a user watching a real transfer found it. The new unit test pins the aggregation INCLUDING the callback case, but it cannot prove the listener calls it; that gap is honest and stated. Another entry for [[interop-test-fidelity]] - and another for 'user testing finds what tests cannot'.

- 2026-08-04 **UI round 8bp, and three platform facts worth keeping.** (1) **iOS scroll-to-top is the STATUS BAR TAP** - that is the actual convention, there is no scroll-to-top button pattern in Apple's HIG at all, and it was verified working in padMule on the device (scrolled to row 54, tapped, landed on row 1). The floating button exists only because the gesture is undiscoverable, so it is an ADDITION to the convention, not a replacement. (2) **iOS has no default-app-per-file-type** and `UIApplication.open` refuses a `file://` URL, so 'open this in the right app' is necessarily the Open In chooser (`UIDocumentInteractionController`) - and the app that takes over backgrounds padMule, pausing transfers. (3) **A `strings` check on the .ipa can FALSE-NEGATIVE:** Swift stores strings of 15 bytes or fewer INLINE in the String struct, so they never appear as literals. Two markers read 0 in the verification pass (`shareddocuments`, `Preview vs Open`) purely because both are exactly 15 characters; longer strings from the same code were all present. This project verifies every build with `strings`, so pick markers over 15 characters or the check quietly lies.

- 2026-08-04 **"So many dead servers" was the PROBE, not the servers - and my first answer was wrong.** I explained the greyed-out rows as the eD2k server network's long decline. Anthony pushed back: these had been connecting. He was right, and the evidence was already in this session - the SAME two servers read `no reply` and then 3,651 / 47,008 users minutes apart. Nothing dead does that. Root cause in `probe_server_list`: `alive` starts false and is only set by a reply inside ONE GLOBAL 3-second deadline shared by the entire fan-out, after sending two UDP packets per server sequentially. Through a VPN at ~200ms base RTT, across dozens of servers, that budget is marginal - and a single lost datagram (routine for UDP) therefore read as DEATH. **That is 'an event is not state' for the THIRD time in this codebase** (after the ID-type poll and the status line): a missed datagram is an event; death is a state, and needs more than one observation. Fixed by remembering the last good answer per server and only calling it dead after 3 consecutive misses, with the budget raised to 6s and a transient socket error no longer abandoning every reply still in flight. Also worth noting the probe is UDP while LOGIN is TCP, so `no reply` never meant unconnectable anyway - the Tier-1 portability fix had already stopped it disabling the row.

- 2026-08-04 **Serial-queue engine half done (row 8bq) - the handoff's #4 open item, closed.** The lesson is about SCOPING a scary refactor: the obvious plan was making `server` and `kad` independently shareable, a genuine architectural change to the most concurrency-sensitive code here. Reading the struct first showed that was not needed for the actual symptom - five of the ten polls read `Arc`s that never needed the engine at all, so handing out clones fixed the freeze with no ownership change and no new deadlock surface. **Checking that those `Arc`s are never REASSIGNED was the load-bearing step**; if any were, a handle would silently go stale and the UI would read a dead copy forever. The other half of the care went to `downloads()` having been the heartbeat: splitting reading from maintenance turned an unmissable side effect into a caller obligation whose failure mode is seven duties dying silently, so it is called first and documented loudly. Honest remainder: Status scalars still lag during a long search. [[build-progress]] row 8bq.

- 2026-08-04 **DECISION: wave 9 seedbox mode DROPPED.** Anthony cut it rather than deferring it a third time. Recorded across [[build-progress]] (wave 9 row), [[decisions-and-lessons]] (new dated entry, with the 2026-07-12 'foreground-only v1, seedbox toggle in v1.1' decision annotated in place as superseded IN PART - the foreground-only half stands), [[index]] and [[lifecycle-and-reactivation]]. **The consequence is the point, and is what the docs now say:** foreground-only stops being a limitation awaiting a fix and becomes the permanent design, so the honest pause/resume, the readiness-gated splash, keep-screen-awake, the 'padMule only transfers while it is open' copy and Stop releasing the forwarded port are all the FINAL answer rather than stopgaps. No code changes - nothing was ever built for seedbox mode. The lifecycle research is kept in place rather than deleted, because the analysis stays true of iPadOS and is precisely why dropping the feature is safe: a user who wants always-on can still set Auto-Lock to Never and leave padMule open, which is what keep-screen-awake already assists. What is dropped is padMule shipping a MODE around it.

CLAUDE.md's two counts were both wrong: ~601 -> **608** tests and 26 -> **29** mule-cli subcommands (wave 11 added `aich-hash`/`aich-probe`, and `server-crawl` had gone uncounted since 8ba). - 2026-08-04 **Split the four oversized entries into `*-history` archives**, the treatment [[build-history]] got on 2026-08-01. VERBATIM moves, nothing trimmed: [[ipad-usb-tooling]] 402 -> 228 (the usbipd bind/attach route now SUPERSEDED, the first-run gotchas, the USB/IP install dead end, the dated verification narrative); [[net-highid-and-port-forwarding]] 381 -> 170 (the pre-VPN Xfinity chain, its validation, the first iPad HighID, the stale-mapping dead end, the VPN vendor survey); [[mac-toolchain-setup]] 227 -> 150 (the decided-against paths, the de-risk checklist, the first-device-run narrative); [[feature-server-hunter]] 225 -> 56 (the shipped per-part narrative and the pre-build design). **What deliberately did NOT move:** the Server Hunter's scope boundary (why whole-net scanning stays out) stayed live, because it is a STANDING decision rather than a completed narrative and is what someone proposing a scanner needs to read. Two process notes: a splitter that cuts at the next `## ` CORRUPTED a two-line heading in net-highid (archiving one line, orphaning the other in the live file) - caught by re-reading the output, and redone with explicit line ranges; and every split was checked by comparing total line counts before and after, which must go UP (headers and pointers added) and never down. [[ipad-usb-tooling]] remains 228 rather than ~150 - what is left is all live runbook, and cutting further would separate commands from the caveats that explain them.

- 2026-08-04 **A night of real use found a starvation bug the whole gate missed (row 8br).** Anthony ran padMule overnight and reported downloads stopping or crawling. Root cause: the idle-retry sweep picked ONE download per 45s via a STABLE sort on priority plus `.first()`, so at uniform Normal priority it returned the same download every sweep and never touched the others. **The lesson is about the shape of the bug, not the fix:** it is invisible with ONE download (the only case every test and every manual check used), and only emerges at N>1 with equal priority - which is the normal way anyone actually uses a P2P client. Ask of any selection code: what does this do when the sort key TIES? Rust's stable sort makes 'first' deterministic, which is exactly what turns a fair-looking pick into permanent starvation. Also shipped the amber receiving-bytes row Anthony asked for, deliberately on a TIME WINDOW rather than a rate. Two items from the same report remain open and are recorded as such: the cross-tab sources/availability disparity, and the dozens-of-downloads stress run (deferred on purpose - the starvation would have dominated any measurement). [[build-progress]] row 8br.

- 2026-08-04 **Stress harness built; three fixes measured; the real root cause found (row 8bs).** New `crates/mule-ffi/examples/stress.rs` drives 25 live downloads through the SAME FFI the app uses, which is what turned this from guesswork into measurement. **Three lessons worth more than the fixes.** (1) **A budget that is always fully consumed is not a budget, it is a cost.** `find_sources` joined two arms and waited for both, so the 'ceiling' was a floor; making it return early cut retries 30x and dissolved a duty-cycle cap I had added an hour before to work around the symptom. Ask of any timeout: does the fast path actually finish early, or does something in the join always burn it? (2) **I overcorrected once and caught it only from the traces** - an 8s budget with a 9s gap gave ~89% engine-lock occupancy, which would starve pause() on iPadOS and risk losing the checkpoint. The instrument caught what reasoning had not. (3) **A zero-result test is not a failing test until you run the control.** The video run moved zero bytes; re-running the scenario that previously moved 372MB proved the build fine and the CONTENT absent - four of seven Blender-open-movie keywords returned zero hits, because that content lives on blender.org, not eD2k. **AND THE ROOT CAUSE:** with retries cheap and fair, sources are plentiful (15/17 retries found 2-7) yet only 5 of 20 downloads ever got a byte - because padMule BAILS the instant a peer queues it, on a network that is fundamentally queue-based. Left unfixed deliberately; it is a hot-path policy change entangled with foreground-only.

- 2026-08-04 **HYPOTHESIS REFUTED BY MEASUREMENT: making the fetch WAIT in peer queues is much worse, and was reverted.** Row 8bs identified `bail_on_queue` as the root cause of stalled downloads - eD2k rations upload slots, so a client that never queues seemed unable to be served. The reasoning was clean and WRONG. A/B on the stress harness, identical scenario, queue policy the only variable: BAILING gave everReceived=5/20 and 53.7MB in 243s; WAITING gave everReceived=1/21 and **0.0MB**. Cause: the manager runs ~4 concurrent peers per download, and waiting parks all of them in hopeless queues instead of cycling to find the peer that actually has a FREE slot. Fast-bail is not the bug - it is doing real work, exactly as the three-file milestone claimed. **The lesson is the one this project keeps relearning, aimed at myself this time:** a mechanism that LOOKS like the cause, with a tidy story about why, is a hypothesis and nothing more until measured - and I had already written it into [[build-progress]] row 8bs and the handoff as 'the root cause' before testing it. Both are corrected. The refined candidate that remains untested: bail while UNTRIED sources remain, and wait only once every known source has queued us - which keeps the cycling that works while not giving up when there is nothing else to try.

- 2026-08-04 **Close-of-session KB pass: handoff rewritten, harness documented, lint.** The handoff was replaced WHOLESALE per its own convention and now leads with THE OPEN PROBLEM rather than the wins, because the honest headline is that downloads still stall and the cause is not known - retries are cheap and fair, 15 of 17 lookups find 2-7 sources, and only ~5 of 20 downloads ever receive a byte. The queue-bail explanation is recorded as REFUTED with its A/B table, not deleted, and the next step is stated as a MEASUREMENT (instrument connect/handshake/slot outcome per peer) rather than another theory. New `## Tools` section in [[index]] and a Key-commands entry in CLAUDE.md for `examples/stress.rs`, which has become a first-class instrument. Memory: [[verify-before-reporting]] gained the root-cause corollary (a tidy causal story is a hypothesis; a wrong DIAGNOSIS is costlier than a wrong fix because it gets written down and believed; when reverting a deliberate decision, first find the measurement behind it), and the harness was recorded in the live-downloads memory with the content-selection caveat that SAFE and SEEDED barely overlap for video on eD2k. Lint: CLAUDE.md test count 608 -> 610, no orphans, ASCII clean; `ipad-usb-tooling` is 228 lines, still over the ~150 guidance and still all live runbook.

- 2026-08-04 **REANALYSIS + THE FETCH FUNNEL: the stall gap finally MEASURED, and the two bugs it found.** A full reacquaintance pass (every crate, the iOS app, CI, scripts, all 33 wiki files) plus the work the handoff asked for: instrument the fetch path. **THE INSTRUMENT:** cumulative per-stage counters in `mule_engine::stats`, bumped one line at a time through `run_peer`/`fetch_one` (dial -> connect -> filestatus -> hashset -> slot -> blocks -> bytes), a 256-entry lock-free tally of every opcode read OUT OF TURN, and a dial-duration histogram split by whether the handshake succeeded. Cumulative stages need no per-attempt state, and the DROP between adjacent stages is the loss at that stage - crucially including a loss to the per-peer TIMEOUT, which no error value can report, which is exactly why this gap had stayed invisible through three rounds of theorising. Exposed as `mule_ffi::fetch_report()`, deliberately NOT a UniFFI export. **THE MEASUREMENT** (480s, 25 downloads, HighID, 41k-user server): 1583 dials -> 74 handshakes -> 65 filestatus -> 65 slot asks -> 7 slots granted -> 6 of those had no block to take -> **ONE session moved a byte**. 23 of 25 downloads reached 0/0/0 connected sources against a Search claim of 8-22; Kad contributed ZERO sources to any download. **BUG 1 (fixed): OP_OUTOFPARTREQS (0x57) had no handler** - the ordinary end of every upload slot (eMule 0.50a UploadClient.cpp:722-725/:767-782, aMule master UploadClient.cpp:463-466), fired at 10MB or 1h (UploadQueue.cpp:609-616, the same 10MB padMule encodes as SESSION_MAX_BYTES). `BlockReceiver::accept` yields no writes for a non-data opcode, so the block loop waited out the caller's 45s timeout holding one of only four workers; the one moving download froze at exactly 18.6MB. Fixed per aMule's own receive side (ClientTCPSocket.cpp:727-736): with bail_on_queue it returns Ok(delivered) - the BYTES, so the manager scores a proven deliverer - otherwise it releases its blocks and waits out the next turn via a new `await_slot`. **BUG 2 (fixed): padMule asked for a slot from peers holding nothing it needed** - eMule sets DS_NONEEDEDPARTS and swaps away without asking (DownloadClient.cpp:634-641). Fast-bail SELECTS for such peers, because a client that just started downloading has a free slot precisely because it has nothing to give. **PROCESS, and the point worth keeping:** the funnel refuted my OWN fresh hypothesis on the spot - the 0x57 gap was traced to real upstream lines and was genuinely missing, yet the funnel showed the block loop nearly unreachable, so it is real but SECONDARY. Citing the upstream line proves the code is wrong, not that it is what is hurting you. Both fixes written test-first with RED observed, then MUTATION-CHECKED (red at 5.02s/5.19s Elapsed, green at 0.03s/0.23s). **REPRODUCED ON THE ACTUAL DEVICE, agent-driven over WebDriverAgent, and it is the clearest capture of the bug yet.** On the iPad on the VPN with the real config and the STALE 8068a71 build: connected to eMule Sunrise HighID, searched (375 hits), queued four. One download ran 20MB -> 35.3 -> 64.8 -> 65 MB / 67.5 MB (96%) and then FROZE for over ten minutes while its source count CLIMBED 20 -> 30 -> 33 -> 42 ed2k / 2 kad. More and more sources found, not one byte from any of them - the handoff's "sources are found, data does not follow" witnessed directly instead of inferred from a harness. A sibling download progressed normally throughout (2MB -> 41.2MB), so the engine was NOT wedged: the stall is per-download and struck at the TAIL, exactly where the two defects compound (~2.5MB left, near the 2.11MB four workers can hold reserved, and a worker hung on the missing 0x57 handler keeps its reservations for the full 45s). CONSISTENT WITH both fixes, PROOF of neither - the device build predates the funnel, so nothing can be seen inside it; the decisive test is the same scenario on a build carrying them, and installing that needs Anthony because CI's .ipa is UNSIGNED (verified: no _CodeSignature, no embedded.mobileprovision) and Sideloadly must re-sign it. Two side observations: the Servers screen read "No server list on disk" / Servers (0) on a device that had been downloading overnight, restored instantly by one Refresh tap; and Kad DID contribute sources on-device where the dev-box stress runs got zero, so the zero-Kad finding may be dev-box-specific. **THE LIVE A/B IS INCONCLUSIVE, and recording that is the point.** A second 480s run with both fixes gave 1676 dialed -> 33 connected -> 24 slot asks -> 17 ACCEPTED -> 12 no-block-to-take -> 5 DELIVERED, 2 of 25 receiving, 22.6MB (baseline 1 of 25, 18.6MB). Every headline moved the right way and none of it is attributable: connected fell 74 -> 33 on a similar dial count, so the runs sampled very different source populations, and an 11% -> 71% slot-accept swing is far too large for a gate that removed six asks. Both fixes are established OFFLINE instead, by deterministic loopback tests with mutation checks. Note `slot REVOKED (0x57) = 0` for the entire second run - no source fed padMule 10MB in one session, so the revocation fix was never exercised live; it is proven by test, not by that run, and no doc should imply otherwise. **A third finding, half proven and half REFUTED:** `accepted, no block to take` did not fall, it rose to 12 of 17. The arithmetic (4 workers x 3 blocks x 184320 = 2.11MB reservable at once) suggests a small file is entirely spoken for by its own workers, but the test written to prove it FAILED first - below ENDGAME_LIMIT (737KB) take_blocks enters endgame and races the reservations rather than returning empty. The real band is 737KB < still-missing < 2.11MB plus any peer holding only already-reserved parts: real, kept as a regression-documenting test, and too narrow to account for 12 of 17, which stays OPEN. **NOT explained, and deliberately not written up as a cause:** 95% of dials never handshake. Two known contributors, unseparated - nothing ever evicts a proven-dead source from `download_file`'s pool (PeerScoreboard only re-ORDERS, so one dead peer costs 8 dials per sweep and again per retry), and the connect shares the 45s per-peer budget where eMule's equivalent 40s costs it nothing because it multiplexes hundreds of sockets. The histogram exists to settle that with data before either number is touched. **Also found:** `should_kick()` and `build_out_of_part_reqs()` are BOTH dead code - padMule's serve side never rotates an upload slot, the mirror image of bug 1, and not covered by the UploadGate's documented foreground-only scoping since rotation happens on the held connection. Doc/lint: the previous handoff said three commits were unpushed when there were four (a handoff cannot list its own commit - use `git log origin/main..HEAD`); CLAUDE.md test count 610 -> 614 and its Key-commands block now documents the funnel; new [[index]] Tools entry; [[verify-before-reporting]] gained the instrument corollary. Gate: 614 tests, clippy -D warnings, fmt + ASCII clean. [[build-progress]] row 8bt.

- 2026-08-04 **The empty Servers tab was a BOOTSTRAP BUG; strip reorder; IPA delivery convention.** Anthony asked for servers to auto-load on app start (after the device session found "No server list on disk" / Servers (0)), and for Transfers to sit before Downloads. The first turned out to be a real defect rather than a missing feature: `bootstrap::ensure` DOES run on every non-offline `start()`, but its already-present guard was `path.exists() && len > 0`. **Length is not usability** - a `server.met` that is non-empty yet parses to ZERO servers, or does not parse at all, satisfied that guard forever, so the fetch never re-ran and the screen stayed empty until the user found the Refresh button. Reachable in ordinary use, not only from a corrupt write: prune the last dead server and the remaining file is exactly that shape. The `validate` predicate was already in the signature, used only to gate what we WRITE; applying it to what we already HAVE is the whole fix, and it covers `nodes.dat` identically. `start()` now also says which happened, since an auto-load and an already-present list are otherwise indistinguishable to the user. Deliberate boundary kept: this auto-loads the LIST, never auto-CONNECTS. TDD with RED observed (`AlreadyPresent` where `Failed` was required). Strip is now Servers, Status, Search, **Transfers, Downloads**, Shared, Stats - a queued file is watched far more often than a finished one is opened; only the enum declaration order matters and nothing persists the raw values, so it is display-only, and row 8bf's order was annotated SUPERSEDED in place rather than rewritten. **Standing convention recorded:** built IPAs are delivered to the Windows Downloads folder (`/mnt/c/Users/ajbuf/Downloads`) as `padMule-INSTALL-THIS-unsigned-<sha>.ipa`, matching the name already there from a prior session - CI's artifact is UNSIGNED and Sideloadly re-signs it at install, which is the one step an agent cannot do. Gate: 615 tests, clippy -D warnings, fmt + ASCII clean. [[build-progress]] row 8bu.

- 2026-08-04 **STALL RE-TEST on the fixed build: fixes EXONERATED by control, device stall NOT cleared, and the dial question ANSWERED.** Installed build 2b86f02 on the iPad (verified by the reordered strip reading Servers/Status/Search/**Transfers/Downloads**/Shared/Stats). Server auto-load CONFIRMED working - "Server list downloaded" then Servers (10); the first three reads showed Servers (0) only because `start()` still held the engine lock doing the bootstrap fetch, so the view had not been populated. Sampling too early is not a failed fix. **DEVICE RE-TEST: the stall REPRODUCED.** Two 50MB+ files sat at Zero KB for 9 minutes, then moved to 33.4MB/67.5MB (49%) and 14.6MB/50MB (29%) - both past the 10MB kick threshold - and then FROZE for another 9 minutes with **ZERO sources**, while three 2-2.4GB Ubuntu ISOs (6-7 full srcs claimed) never registered as transfers at all. Network was healthy throughout: Connected, HighID, Kad 138 contacts. **CONTROL (the discipline that mattered): a dev-box stress run on the SAME server and keyword at the SAME time, with the same fixed binary,** gave 7 of 12 downloads receiving, one at 147MB, 60 of 70 slot asks ACCEPTED and 42 DELIVERING sessions - against the pre-fix baseline's 1 delivering session in 480s. Decisively, `(holds nothing we need) = 0` across 70 file-status reads and `accepted, no block to take = 0` (was 6 of 7): **the new needed-parts gate rejected nothing and is not the cause.** So the device zero-bytes is NOT a regression from either fix; the dev box and the device diverge sharply on the same server at the same moment, and the difference between them is the VPN path. That is the next thing to MEASURE, not to theorise about. **THE DIAL HISTOGRAM ANSWERED THE OPEN QUESTION:** of 76 successful handshakes, 75 landed in under 1s and ONE between 1-2s; **not a single dial connected after 2s**, while 57 dials burned the FULL 45s and every one failed. A short connect deadline (~5s) would therefore lose zero real sources and reclaim ~43 minutes of worker time from a 5-minute run - the "widen the pool instead" branch is refuted, and the timeout branch is now evidence-backed. **STILL NOT EXERCISED LIVE: `slot REVOKED (0x57) = 0` in all three runs** - no single source fed padMule 10MB in one session, so that fix remains test-proven only, and hunting it needs large well-seeded content. **GAP THIS EXPOSED:** the funnel is in the device build (same engine) but is not surfaced through the Swift binding, so an on-device stall cannot be diagnosed at all - exposing it (Stats screen or os_log) is now the blocker for any further device work.

- 2026-08-04 **The funnel is now ON-DEVICE (Stats screen), which was the blocker for any further iPad work.** The engine already carried the funnel, but nothing surfaced it through the Swift binding, so the one problem the app cannot explain from its own screens - a row showing 20 sources and 0 bytes - had nowhere to be diagnosed. Two new UniFFI methods on `MuleEngine`: `fetch_report()` and `reset_fetch_stats()`, both **deliberately LOCK-FREE** (process-global atomics, never the engine lock) for the same reason the status polls are - a stall is exactly when the engine is busy, and a diagnostic that queued behind `search` or a source hunt would be unreadable precisely when wanted. Stats gains a "Fetch diagnostics" section: the report in monospace with `.textSelection`, a **Copy report** button (UIPasteboard - the report has to be able to LEAVE the device, or Anthony is retyping numbers from a photograph), and **Reset counters**. Reset exists because the counters are cumulative since LAUNCH, so by the time something stalls they are dominated by the healthy minutes before it; reset -> reproduce -> read is what makes them describe the thing under investigation. Session byte totals are deliberately NOT reset - those are the user's stats, not a diagnostic. The panel needs no refresh button: the 1s stats poll republishes `rateHistory`, which re-renders the body. TEST: rather than assert a counter reads zero (racy - these are process-global and the suite bumps them in parallel), the test asserts the RACE-FREE invariant that `FUNNEL_REFS` covers every stage `fetch_funnel()` reports AND that every stage appears in the printed report - so a stage added later can neither escape the reset nor be counted invisibly. Swift type-checks only in CI here, so the binding was verified BEFORE pushing by running `uniffi-bindgen` against the compiled cdylib and confirming `fetchReport() -> String` and `resetFetchStats()` are generated with the shapes the call sites use. Gate: 616 tests, clippy -D warnings, fmt + ASCII clean.

- 2026-08-04 **The funnel's OWN bug, caught by its first real device reading; and the connect deadline set from data.** Anthony's on-glass report (HighID, Kad 138, 217.9MB downloaded at 106 KB/s, three of five transfers moving - the healthiest device run yet) contained an IMPOSSIBLE line: `got filestatus 396` against `connected 315`. A session cannot read a file status without connecting first. **Cause: two entry paths conflated.** A called-back source DIALS US (`InboundKind::Source`), entering `download_from_peer_at` directly and never `fetch_one`, so it bumps every stage EXCEPT the two dial stages - and one inbound connection is offered EVERY unfinished download in turn, so a single callback makes several sessions. With a HighID user and LowID peers calling back that was ~160 phantom sessions inside an outbound funnel. Fixed by counting inbound sessions explicitly (`note_inbound` at the callback site) and printing THREE labelled groups - what we dialed, what dialed us, then the per-session stages both paths share. Also fixed: `(peer said NOFILE)` was indented as a subset of `got filestatus` when the NOFILE arm returns BEFORE that counter - they are disjoint, and the indentation was lying about the totals. The stage counts were never wrong; only the story the layout told about them. **CONNECT DEADLINE, and the number CHANGED when the device data arrived.** On this box, 75 of 76 successful handshakes landed under 1s and none after 2s, so "a 5s cap is free" looked safe. On the iPad over a VPN the tail is REAL: 1 connection at 5-10s and TWO at 20-45s out of 315. A 5s cap would have silently discarded real sources on the very path padMule ships on. Settled at **10s**, keeping 313 of 315 (99.4%) while still killing the 63 dials that each burned the full 45s - about 37 minutes of worker time in one run. It matters far more here than upstream: both authorities use CONNECTION_TIMEOUT = 40s, but eMule multiplexes hundreds of sockets so a stalled one is free, whereas padMule gives a download only FOUR workers, making one black-holed address a quarter of that file's dialing capacity. Implemented as a pure `connect_deadline(per_peer)` = `min(10s, per_peer)` so it is unit-testable and so a caller that deliberately chose a SHORTER budget (CLI, tests) never has its dial silently extended. **Also confirmed from the same reading:** `slot REVOKED (0x57)` is still 0 and now EXPLAINED rather than suspicious - 93.8MB spread over 40 sources is ~2.3MB each, so nobody reaches the 10MB kick; it will only fire on a file with few, fast sources. Gate: 617 tests, clippy -D warnings, fmt + ASCII clean, differential oracle re-run GREEN (3 files byte-for-byte incl. the 15MB multipart). **LESSON: the first thing a new instrument measures is itself.** The funnel's arithmetic was internally consistent from `got filestatus` down (accepted 194 = requested 110 + no-block 84) - it was the boundary between the two entry paths that lied, and only real traffic on a HighID device could expose it.

- 2026-08-04 **On-glass UI round (six requests), and TWO Swift failures only CI could see.** Anthony driving the app produced six changes, ingested as [[build-progress]] row 8bw: an explicit dark `Color.bannerBlue`; EVERY banner closeable (the three STATE-driven ones re-arm when their condition next changes, so dismissing means "I have read this" rather than silencing a future warning); the server list loaded on APP open rather than tab open (which also explains the "No server list on disk" seen right after launch - the tab's onAppear fired while `start()` still held the engine lock); a finish BEEP in Settings > Device; the rate chart widened to a fixed 60-point span pinned newest-to-the-right (it had been plotting against ABSOLUTE sample index, so Charts scaled the domain to whatever few points were buffered and squeezed the trace into a sliver); Stop moved to the FIRST toolbar icon; and the Servers tab now names a connected server "Name (ip:port)" like Status, via a shared `serverLabel()`. **The beep needed real plumbing:** completion existed only as the prose line "Saved '..'", so engine + FFI gained a typed `Finished { name }` - matching on prose would have broken silently the first time someone reworded it. **HONESTY NOTE on the blue:** the tree has used `Color.blue.gradient` since 57a75f5 and there is no earlier dark-blue literal, so this is a deliberate value, NOT a revert; `.gradient` lightening the top is the likely reason it read as too bright. **THE LESSON:** two failures were invisible locally because Swift type-checks ONLY in CI here - the five conditional banners with trailing closures blew the type-checker's budget (`unable to type-check this expression in reasonable time`, cured by splitting into a `@ViewBuilder`), and `bootError` is `private(set)` so the dismiss closure could not assign nil (cured with a `clearBootError()` method rather than widening the setter). A third was pre-empted locally by hoisting nested string interpolation out of a closure. Gate: 617 tests, clippy -D warnings, fmt + ASCII clean; differential oracle GREEN; IPA d1f058f built and delivered. **NEW USER REPORT, not yet diagnosed:** "much download activity but no completions". The leading candidate is the tail - the funnel measured `accepted, no block to take` at 84 of 194 granted slots (43%), and 4 workers x 3 blocks x 184320 = 2.11MB can be under reservation at once while ENDGAME_LIMIT only rescues the last 737KB, so a nearly-finished file can have its whole remainder held by a few workers while every other source that wins a slot is turned away. NOT confirmed; the on-device funnel is what will settle it.

- 2026-08-04 **"It was stalled BEFORE you interrupted - did the restart help?" - Anthony's clue, and the instrument it earned.** A file sat at 85% for a long time; the app was force-relaunched (see the mistake below) and it then FINISHED. That observation is worth more than the interruption cost, because it narrows the blocker to **in-memory state that a process restart clears** - and there are exactly TWO such gates on the dial path, both now COUNTED rather than argued about.
