(1) The per-download corruption **ban set** is a plain `StdMutex<HashSet<IpAddr>>`, never persisted, and `is_banned` is consulted BEFORE dialing - so a download whose handful of sources all got banned makes ZERO dials while still listing them, and looks identical to one with no sources at all. (2) The **`fetching` flag**: `maintain_resume_fetches` filters `!dl.is_fetching()`, so a flag that never clears means that download is never re-driven again for the life of the process. New funnel lines `skipped: source BANNED` and `skipped: fetch already running` discriminate them on the next stall. **Also added, from the same session:** per-transfer-row **"N parts nobody has"** - parts still needed that NO source was ever seen holding. `availability` is cumulative and never decremented, which is a FEATURE here: a zero means nobody we ever talked to had that part, so the number never overstates the problem. It is the difference between a slow tail and an impossible one, which are opposite bugs with opposite fixes and look identical on a row reading "90%, 86 sources, not moving". Shown only when non-zero AND at least one source has reported, since before that every part is trivially unavailable. **MY MISTAKE, recorded because it cost real data:** creating a WDA session WITHOUT a bundleId does not attach passively - it backgrounded padMule, which on a foreground-only app pauses everything, the screen then slept, and recovery needed a relaunch. Download progress survived (all byte counts matched) but the accumulated funnel counters did not. RULE: on a live run, `GET /source` and `pymobiledevice3 developer dvt screenshot` are safe and session-free; creating ANY session disturbs the app. **What the live reading did show** (post-relaunch, healthy): 33 outbound dials -> 19 handshaked + 4 inbound -> 16 filestatus -> 11 slots ACCEPTED -> 10 DELIVERED, `accepted, no block to take` = 0, and the `>=45s` dial bucket GONE, replaced by 10-20s failures - the 10s connect cap confirmed live. Gate: 617 tests, clippy -D warnings, fmt + ASCII clean; differential oracle GREEN.

- 2026-08-04 **Get made 75x faster, and the new badge answered a live stall within twenty minutes.** Anthony: "why does it take so long for Get to add a file to downloading?" Because it was a FLAT ~15s: `add_download` passed `stop_when_server_answers=false`, so `join!` waited on the slower arm and the Kad lookup burned its whole budget after the server had already answered in ~200ms - all while holding the engine lock, making four queued files cost a minute. The `false` was DELIBERATE and the rationale was MINE and was reasoning, not measurement ("the user wants the widest net"), contradicted by a measurement already on the books for the retry path (195ms vs 6.001s). Flipped; the serverless/Kad-only case falls through to the identical joined path and keeps its regression guard. **THE PAYOFF FROM THE DIAGNOSTICS SHIPPED AN HOUR EARLIER:** passive live sampling (session-free `GET /source` only - see the rule below) showed ~375MB in 20 minutes, about 320 KB/s and 3x the earlier rate, with the two NON-moving files being exactly the two carrying the new badge: one at Zero KB with **24 parts nobody has**, another that can only reach ~50% because **13 parts (~120MB)** are held by no source seen. That ANSWERS "much activity, no completions" for those files - the content is not fully on the network and padMule is correct. **HONEST LIMITATION recorded at the same time:** the badge counts parts no source WE HAVE SEEN held, so on a 1-2 source file it means "the one peer we found lacks these", not "the swarm lacks these"; it is trustworthy in proportion to source count, and tightening it to a source-count threshold with the wording "no connected source has" is queued rather than quietly left to mislead. **OPERATIONAL RULE, learned by breaking it:** on a LIVE device run, `GET /source` and `pymobiledevice3 developer dvt screenshot` are session-free and safe; creating ANY WebDriverAgent session - even with no bundleId - disturbs the app (it backgrounded padMule, which on a foreground-only app pauses everything, then the screen slept and recovery needed a relaunch). Progress survived; the accumulated funnel counters did not. [[build-progress]] row 8bx.

- 2026-08-04 **Tightened the unavailable-parts badge so it stops overstating.** Same day it shipped, the badge was reporting "13 parts nobody has" from a sample of ONE source - a real measurement inflated into a claim about the whole network, which is the exact failure mode this session kept catching elsewhere. Three changes: `Inner` now carries `status_reports` (how many peer statuses were folded into `availability`), `part_availability` returns it as a third value so the SAMPLE SIZE travels with the gap, and the row names it out loud - **"13 parts missing from all 22 sources"** instead of "nobody has". The badge is also gated at **4 sampled statuses**: below that a unanimous gap is anecdotal (one peer lacking parts is ordinary and says nothing about the swarm). A test pins the semantics end to end - zero sample reports a zero sample, one peer holding only part 0 gives gap=2/seen=1, a second peer shrinks the gap and grows the sample, and a COMPLETE source closes it - because this number makes a claim about the network and an untested claim of that kind is how the first version went wrong. Gate: 618 tests, clippy -D warnings, fmt + ASCII clean; differential oracle GREEN. IPA delivered.

- 2026-08-04 **Close-of-session pass.** Row 8bx annotated IN PLACE with its own same-evening correction (the parts badge shipped overstating its evidence and was fixed hours later - kept as an annotation rather than a rewrite, per ledger convention). Handoff REPLACED wholesale and reorganised around the session's actual product: four instruments, seven shipped fixes, eight open items, and the discipline each one cost to learn. [[index]] Tools section now lists all three on-device diagnostics rather than just the funnel. Memory: [[verify-before-reporting]] gained two corollaries - "an instrument's first duty is to measure itself" (the funnel's arithmetic was impossible and the parts badge overstated, both in code written while auditing the same failure mode elsewhere) and "a threshold tuned on one network is a hypothesis about the others" (5s connect cap: true on the dev box, false over the VPN); [[padmule-live-downloads]] gained a section naming the diagnostics and telling the next session to read the funnel BEFORE writing a theory. Lint: no orphan links, ASCII clean, test counts consistent at 618. Branch `fetch-funnel` sits 16 commits ahead of main and is still UNMERGED - that is the first decision next session.

- 2026-08-04 **Banner colour settled at #000066, and the real cause was a TRANSFORM, not the value.** Third attempt at this shade. Anthony specified the hex directly; the reason the first two picks both missed is that banners were drawn with `.gradient`, which lightens the top of whatever tint it is given - so **the colour named at the call site was never the colour rendered**, and no amount of re-picking could have fixed it. The gradient is removed: banners now take a FLAT fill, which is exactly what `banner`'s own doc comment had always claimed ("solid tint with white text"), so the code and its documentation agree for the first time. Orange and red keep their hue and simply render as themselves. Both banner sites moved together - the global status banners and the server-NEWS row on the Servers tab, the row the treatment was originally picked from and the worst one to leave on the old bright blue. The literal is written as hex components over 255 (`0x00/255.0` ...) rather than pre-divided decimals, so it can be checked against #000066 by eye and cannot drift through a rounding edit. **GENERAL LESSON, worth more than the colour:** when a value is reported wrong repeatedly, check whether anything TRANSFORMS it between declaration and render before picking another value - two rounds were spent tuning a number that was being modified downstream. Gate: 618 tests, fmt + ASCII clean; no Rust behaviour touched. IPA 9e446ea delivered. [[build-progress]] row 8bw.

- 2026-08-05 **Reanalysis pass: the serve side never rotated its slot, and two instruments could not see what they claimed.** A full reacquaintance (gate re-run from scratch - 618 tests / clippy / fmt / ASCII all verified rather than quoted, plus the delivered IPA unpacked and checked) turned up one real defect and three honesty problems, ingested as [[build-progress]] row 8by. **THE DEFECT:** padMule's upload slot was held for the whole connection - `should_kick` and `build_out_of_part_reqs` were both dead outside tests - so the first peer to win a slot kept it and everyone else timed out at QUEUE_WAIT and was closed. Fixed per both authorities (10 MB / 1 hour), gated on somebody actually waiting per eMule's explicit anti-churn rule where aMule has none, and clearing the re-send dedup on rotation because upstream does (`ClearUploadBlockRequests` before 0x57) and because without it padMule's own downloader - which releases and re-requests its blocks on 0x57 - would have hung for its full 45s. RED observed first in bytes, both guards mutation-checked (0.15s vs a 10.15s real hang). Oracles GREEN but explicitly NOT proof: `mule-cli serve-file` passes no gate and the fixture is 300KB with one downloader, so neither oracle drives the new path. **THE INSTRUMENTS:** `skipped: fetch already running` sat behind callers that already filter `!is_fetching()`, so it read 0 in exactly the stuck-flag case it was built for - relabelled as the race it counts, replaced by a never-reset `fetches in flight` GAUGE, which is [[an-event-is-not-state]] for the fifth time. The parts badge said "sources" for a count of SESSIONS (the pool is re-swept and nothing evicts a source), re-committing the very inflation the 8bx correction had just fixed. The dial histogram was documented as CENSORED by the 10s cap its own reading justified. **A HANDOFF CLAIM CORRECTED:** "real connections land at 20-45s" over the VPN reads as though the bulk do, which would make the shipped 10s cap a serious regression - it was chased as one until [[log]] 2026-08-04 refuted it (one connection at 5-10s, TWO at 20-45s, out of 315). The measurement was sound; the compression was not, in the one document the next session reads first. Also swept: CLAUDE.md and the index still said iPadOS 26.5.2 (device is on 26.6), the free `fetch_report` still claimed the Swift binding had no method for it (it has had one since 8bv), `find_sources` said "BOTH callers" of three, and the on-device checklist had none of the 8bv-8by on-glass items - six steps added, since that is the doc for the pass being started. Gate: 622 tests, clippy -D warnings, fmt + ASCII clean.

- 2026-08-05 **The device can name its own build at last.** Every .ipa padMule has ever shipped reported `CFBundleVersion = 1` (XcodeGen's default, never overridden), so nothing on glass could say WHICH build was running and an install was confirmed by spotting a UI change and remembering which round it came from - guesswork aimed at the first question any bug report has to answer, and the handoff had already resorted to "verified by the reordered strip". CI now stamps the git short sha with PlistBuddy on the BUILT app (after xcodebuild can no longer overwrite it, and while it is still unsigned so there is no signature to invalidate), and Settings > This device gains a SELECTABLE "Build" row - the value belongs in a report, and the alternative is transcribing it off a photograph. A hex sha is not a legal App Store CFBundleVersion; irrelevant here, since padMule is sideload-only and never faces that validation. The formatting rule is a pure `buildLabel(short:build:)` so it is testable without a bundle, including the unstamped "(dev)" fallback - a label reading "1.0" for both a CI build and a local one would answer the question WRONGLY rather than not at all, which is worse because it looks like information. Delivered as `padMule-INSTALL-THIS-unsigned-d24e88d.ipa`; the artifact was unpacked and its plist read back (`CFBundleVersion = d24e88d`) rather than trusted from the CI log. Note while verifying: `strings` could not see "peer reports" (13 bytes, so Swift stores it inline) - the known false negative, and the reason the stamped plist is now the better provenance check than any string grep. Both iOS workflows green on the branch: the .ipa build and 21 Swift simulator tests, which are the only Swift type-check available from this box.

- 2026-08-05 **A live session that found two bugs the code read had missed, and the best throughput padMule has ever shown.** Anthony installed d24e88d and drove it while the agent watched over USB with SESSION-FREE screenshots only (the documented rule: a WebDriverAgent session backgrounds padMule and pauses every transfer). Ingested as [[build-progress]] row 8bz. **THE OBSERVATION THAT DID THE WORK was Anthony's, not the agent's:** a 312 MB file sat at Zero KB with a blank source line, the agent wrote it off as "zero sources found, nothing to diagnose", and Anthony said "but it had sources when I selected it from search". He was right and the dismissal was wrong - the SEARCH number is the server's advertised availability (it counts LowID peers that can never be dialed), while the TRANSFERS badge counts completed handshakes, so an empty badge conflated "nothing found" with "plenty found, none reachable". Both numbers to tell those apart already existed at `spawn_fetch`'s call sites and were being thrown away. **THE SECOND BUG FELL OUT OF THE SAME SCREEN:** every source badge across six downloads read `ed2k` or `sx` and not one read `kad`, which turned the code-read suspicion about the Get-latency fast path into live evidence - `find_sources` was skipping Kad on ANY non-empty server answer, including one that yields almost nothing dialable. Both fixed, plus Dark Mode and a provider-agnostic VPN badge with a drop warning that is explicit about not being a safety guarantee. **THROUGHPUT, measured from timestamped screenshots rather than the Stats screen:** ~990 MB moved in 23 minutes across six downloads, **~718 KB/s sustained** - against 106 KB/s on the 2026-08-04 device run and ~320 KB/s at the best earlier passive sample. **AND A REPRODUCTION LEFT LIVE at close:** an ebook stuck at exactly 23.2 MB / 25.9 MB (89%) for 23 minutes with FOUR handshaked sources and no parts-missing badge - i.e. the parts it needs are held by peers it has seen, so the swarm is not the problem. 2.7 MB remained against the 2.11 MB that four workers x three blocks can hold reserved at once, which puts it next to the band row 8bt identified; `accepted, no block to take` is the funnel line to read. **PROCESS NOTE:** the agent killed a leftover WebDriverAgent runner it had left running, misreading Anthony's "you have a session still going" as a cleanup request when it was an offer to REUSE it. Restored, and the runner is now left up between checks - starting one is the disruptive act, not holding one.

- 2026-08-05 **THE FUNNEL'S BIGGEST READING, and it refuted the handoff's own standing hypothesis.** Anthony put the Stats screen on glass and the agent read it session-free (row 8ca). From a HEALTHY session - 2719 dials, ~1235 sessions, six downloads, ~990 MB moved at ~718 KB/s - one line carries the diagnosis: `slot ACCEPTED 959`, `accepted, no block to take 722`. **padMule wins the scarcest resource on eD2k and then has nothing to ask for, 75% of the time.** Every other stage is healthy (42% of dials handshake against 5% in the 8bt baseline; 78% of slot asks accepted; 94% of block requests deliver), so there is now exactly ONE lossy stage and it is on padMule's side of the wire. The arithmetic closes exactly - 722+237=959 - which matters because this same instrument FAILED its own audit on 2026-08-04 and the lesson taken then was that an instrument's first duty is to measure itself. **THE REFUTATION:** `skipped: source BANNED` = 0. Hours earlier the handoff had narrowed the restart-clearable stall to the per-download ban set on a clean code read (never lifted, never persisted, consulted before dialing); the counter says it never fired once. The reasoning was sound and the answer was still no, which is the entire reason the counter was placed there rather than the hypothesis being written up as a finding. Handoff corrected in place. **ALSO SETTLED:** the 10s connect cap is vindicated at an order of magnitude more data than the decision rested on (`10-20s: 0 connected / 119 failed`; 1074 of 1137 successes under 1s, none past 10s); and `fetches in flight` = 3 against SIX queued downloads exposed a SECOND, different stall - downloads with no live fetch task - visible only because that gauge had replaced a counter that structurally could not see it earlier the same day. **A NEW LEAD, honestly sized:** `0x01` read out of turn 140 times, which on a PEER connection is OP_HELLO (not OP_LOGINREQUEST - same number, server protocol; the agent misdecoded it once and corrected). A peer re-HELLOing gets no HELLOANSWER and may hang. Not the bottleneck; unexplained, which every other out-of-turn opcode is not - they run at ~1 per session and are the answers to things padMule asks fire-and-forget.

- 2026-08-05 **`accepted, no block to take` SOLVED, and the cause was a leak nobody had a name for.** Row 8ca measured it at 722 of 959 won upload slots and named two candidate mechanisms without asserting either; the answer was neither. `download_from_peer_at` released its block reservations with a line AFTER `run_peer` returned, and both session paths wrap that function in a `timeout` - **a timeout DROPS the future, and a dropped future never runs trailing code**. So every session ended by the 45s per-peer timeout, the most common failure on this network, stranded its three blocks permanently. `reserved` only ever grew. **It explains every symptom on record:** monotonic worsening within a run ("partially downloads then slows to a crawl"), worst at the TAIL where few blocks remain, and cleared by a RESTART because `reserved` is in-memory per-download - so THIS, not the ban set, is the restart-clearable stall Anthony reported at 85%. A third in-memory gate; the two that had been enumerated and counted both measured 0 on the device, which is precisely what sent the search looking for a third. **The fix is a destructor,** since that is the only cleanup a cancelled future runs - which meant moving `reserved` to a StdMutex so the release can be synchronous (same pattern as the existing `banned`/`block_sources`/`aich`). RED first, mutation-checked, both oracles GREEN. **Deliberately NOT bundled:** the narrower contention band row 8bt proved (ENDGAME_LIMIT is 4 blocks where four workers can hold 12) is real but unmeasured, and widening endgame costs duplicate tail traffic - the funnel line is how to judge whether it still matters. **Why the leak survived so long:** the peer-that-DIES case was tested and always passed, because there `run_peer` returns and the release executes. The working case and the broken case are identical in source and differ only in whether the future was polled to completion. **Also shipped:** the Search tab's eight loose option rows rolled into two collapsed disclosures whose LABELS name what is active - hiding a control is fine, hiding state is not. **PROCESS LESSON:** the first CI dispatch raced the push and type-checked the PREVIOUS commit, reporting success for code it had never seen; only the test COUNT (23 where 24 was due) exposed it. Verify `gh run view --json headSha` against local HEAD before believing a CI green.

- 2026-08-05 **IPA `92a8f45` delivered** (CI run 30984990314) - the first build carrying the block-reservation leak fix (row 8cb), plus the idle-row source accounting, the Kad dialable-threshold, Dark Mode, the VPN badge and the Search roll-up. Commit under build was verified against local HEAD BEFORE waiting on the run, per the lesson from the dispatch that raced a push earlier the same day; the stamped `CFBundleVersion` was then read back out of the delivered artifact rather than trusted from the CI log. **The one thing to do with this build: Stats -> Fetch diagnostics, and read `accepted, no block to take`.** It was 722 of 959 before the fix; that line is how the fix gets judged, and it is the only outstanding question about it.

- 2026-08-05 **A live device session that produced FOUR findings, three of them Anthony's from simply using a fresh install** (row 8cd). (1) Kad had **no periodic maintenance whatsoever** - a grep for bucket refresh, random lookup or contact ping came back empty across the whole workspace, so the routing table was fed only by the bootstrap and by accident. That was BOTH of his Kad complaints in one defect: "138 contacts where we usually have 200+" (the usual 200+ had accumulated across sessions via the checkpoint rather than being built, and the reinstall wiped it) and "very few kad results" (keyword-search quality is a direct function of table breadth). (2) The server probe called EVERY server dead on a cold start, because the three-misses grace only covered servers that had already answered and the health map is per-launch - one silent 6s round became a verdict. Proven on his screen: padMule showed `eMule Sunrise` as "no reply" and logged into it with HighID minutes later. (3) A syslog capture during an Open-in-another-app round trip caught resume RE-SEEDING KAD FROM DISK ONLY, discarding the live table `pause()` had just folded into memory - 138 contacts became 21 in one cycle. **THE CAPTURE ALSO SETTLED A DISAGREEMENT, against me twice.** Anthony reported transfers looked uninterrupted across the round trip; I first said fast pause/resume, then talked myself out of it when he proposed that opening an app from an app might not background padMule, and endorsed his theory with a plausible Slide Over story. The log says padMule DID background, suspend, and fully reconnect - my first answer was right and I abandoned it for a better-sounding one. I then claimed the background-task assertion was missing; it has always been requested, and I had misread a log line timestamped AFTER resume as belonging to the backgrounding moment. **What the capture genuinely shows and cannot explain:** padMule was suspended 465ms after `pause (backgrounded)` fired, and the teardown did not complete for another 30.5s - it ran on the way back IN, 293ms before resume. So the orderly pause the lifecycle doc promises as a HARD requirement is not what happens. Two mechanisms fit (assertion refused, or the work item queued behind something long on the serial queue) and the log cannot separate them, so pause now LOGS which: whether the assertion was granted, and how long the work waited before starting. Instrument, not a third theory. **Also this session:** three then four downloads COMPLETED on the device - the handoff's "no completions" item is answered - and the Kad table was watched recovering 21 -> 223 organically on a build with neither Kad fix, which is the baseline the next build gets compared against.

- 2026-08-06 **REANALYSIS pass (full tree, post-`/clear`), and it found that the instrument the handoff nominated as its next verdict cannot fall** (row 8ce, new entry [[kad-routing-lifecycle]]). Gate re-verified locally FIRST rather than taken from prose: 628 tests, clippy `-D warnings` clean, fmt clean, ASCII clean; `fetch-funnel` is 33 ahead of local `main` (the handoff said 17) and local `main` is 4 ahead of `origin/main`; 447 commits, linear. **THE HEADLINE: "Kad contacts" is two different measurements sharing one `@Published` field.** The 1s poll writes `Engine::kad_contacts()` = `self.routing.len()` (the PERSISTED table), while `EngineEvent::Kad` carries that same persisted count from `start()` but the **LIVE node's** `contacts_known()` from `start_kad` and `maintain_kad` - and `RoutingTable` has no removal path anywhere, so the polled number is monotonic and **cannot fall on any build**. The handoff's "watch it climb, baseline 21 -> 223" therefore could not have judged `maintain_kad` either way. **SECOND: row 8cd's resume fix named the wrong table.** `Engine::routing` was never discarded - `absorb_kad_routing` folds INTO it and `start_kad` only ever `load_nodes`, which merges. What is discarded on every resume is the LIVE table, and it still is: `bind_with_identity` builds an EMPTY `RoutingTable`, `bootstrap_any` returns on the FIRST success, and `bootstrap_from` adds the responder plus the ~20 contacts it names - **which is exactly the "21" in the syslog that motivated the fix.** The union is a genuine improvement to the bootstrap DIAL LIST; it does not seed the table. And since `closest_to` is verified-only and only the responder is verified, the seed set for the first lookup after a bootstrap is ONE contact. **THIRD: `maintain_kad` is the only Kad call with no deadline** (4 rounds x 3 sequential queries x 750ms = up to 9s) **and `heartbeat()` holds the engine lock across it** while the Swift work queue is serial - the same shape row 8bx called a defect at 15s. Plus three smaller gaps: only eMule's `OnBigTimer` half of maintenance exists (no expiry, no liveness ping, nothing evicts anywhere), the nodes.dat truncation to 200 is near-biased where eMule's `TopDepth(4)` is a deliberately SPREAD sample, and a failed bootstrap is unrecoverable within a foreground session. **VERIFIED SOUND, so the next pass need not re-audit:** the 8cb reservation-leak fix (both production session paths timeout-wrapped, so `HeldBlocks::drop` really is the only cleanup that runs), the 8by slot rotation against its citations, `fold_probe_round` genuinely shared with its test, and the funnel's self-audit. **LINT, same pass:** [[build-progress]]'s header was two days stale against its own newest rows; `CLAUDE.md` said ~618 tests against 628; [[handoff-next-session]] named the delivered IPA `2186e48` in one line and quoted the Build row as `d24e88d` in another; [[lifecycle-and-reactivation]] still asserted clean pause/resume as a hard requirement with no annotation for the 2026-08-05 capture that contradicts it. All four fixed; the lifecycle one is annotated in place per ledger convention rather than rewritten. **THE LESSON, and it is a new one:** a fix can be correct while its stated cause is wrong, and that is MORE durable than an ordinary mistake - the cause is what goes into the wiki beside the fix and what the next session reasons from. Corollary written into the handoff: before nominating a reading as the judge of a change, ask what it shows when the change does NOT work; if the answer is "the same thing", it is not an instrument.

- 2026-08-06 **The first three reanalysis findings FIXED the same day** (row 8ce). (a) **`KadNode::seed_routing`** - the live routing table is filled from the same gated contact list the bootstrap dials, from `start_kad` and BEFORE the bootstrap, so a bootstrap that fails outright still leaves a usable table and `maintain_kad` can still run. The verified bit and the stored verify key travel with each seed, because `closest_to` is verified-only and seeds that lost the bit would inflate the count while staying invisible to every lookup - which is worse than not seeding, since the number would then claim the problem was solved. (b) **One field, one measurement:** `Engine::kad_contacts()` reports the LIVE table whenever there is one (persisted only while Kad is down) and `start()`'s emit site goes through the accessor, so the poll and all three `EngineEvent::Kad` sites finally agree. (c) **`maintain_kad` gets a real deadline** (`KAD_MAINTENANCE_BUDGET` = 3s, pinned by test below `refresh_routing`'s 9s structural worst case), with the gain read off the table rather than the return value so a cancelled round still reports what it learned. **RED observed first** - live table 0 against 5 persisted, and the poll/live split printing as 5 vs 0 - then five mutation checks. **TWO OF MY OWN TESTS WERE VACUOUS AND THE MUTATIONS CAUGHT BOTH, in a change whose entire subject is instruments that cannot see what they claim.** The one-measurement test passed with `kad_contacts` reverted, because once seeding works the two tables AGREE and there was nothing left to discriminate; it now forces divergence with three contacts sharing one IP (the persisted path keeps all three, the live layer's anti-sybil cap keeps one). The seed-gate test then passed with `add_contact` bypassed entirely, because all four poisoned contacts were already caught by `gate_loaded_nodes` one layer UP; it now carries the same-IP trio, the only case the live layer alone catches. **A green test is not a test that can fail** - and the way to find out is to break the code, not to read the test. Third slip, recorded so the ledger is not tidier than the work: a `git checkout` inside a mutation-check script reverted every uncommitted `engine.rs` edit and they had to be rewritten; mutation checks now restore from a file copy. Gate: **632 tests**, clippy `-D warnings`, fmt + ASCII clean, differential oracle GREEN - and per this project's own rule, naming the path it drove: the eD2k transfer path, and **not one line of this change**. Findings 4-6 (contact expiry + liveness ping, the near-biased nodes.dat sample, the missing bootstrap retry) deliberately NOT bundled - each is a separate change, and the first two want a device measurement now that the live table is finally populated. Also found and NOT fixed, as its own defect: `KadNode::add_contact` has no Kad-version gate, so a peer that NAMES a v1 contact over the wire gets it into the table where a lookup can spend `KAD_PER_QUERY` on a node the protocol cannot speak to.

- 2026-08-06 **Ordering note, not a gap.** The 2026-08-06 work landed as two commits at Anthony's request - docs (`491a3cd`) then engine (`2269720`) - so the newest CODE commit post-dates the newest DOCS commit and a mechanical "has the KB kept up" check reads as behind. It is not: row 8ce, the [[log]] entry above it, [[kad-routing-lifecycle]] and the memory files were all written BEFORE either commit and already describe `seed_routing`, the `kad_contacts` unification, `KAD_MAINTENANCE_BUDGET`, the five mutation checks, the two vacuous tests, the three findings left open, and the `add_contact` Kad-version gap. Verified by grepping each load-bearing symbol against the entries rather than by assertion. **The one real consequence is for BISECT:** `491a3cd`'s prose describes code that arrives one commit later and quotes the 632-test count it does not yet have, so a reader landing on it alone will find the tree a commit behind its own ledger. Worth knowing rather than worth rewriting - the two are adjacent and neither was ever pushed separately.

- 2026-08-07 **The routing audit missed the FEATURE, and running one search found it.** Anthony asked padMule to Kad-search "Yes Prime Minister"; it returned zero. **`kad_keyword_target` hashed the WHOLE phrase**, so every multi-word Kad search since Wave 6 walked toward a hash nobody publishes to and converged correctly on an empty region. Control before any code changed: `"minister"` alone -> a full page including Yes Prime Minister releases. **We had researched the correct behaviour and not implemented it** - `docs/raw/wave6-kad-research-2026-07-14.md` line 246 specifies target = MD4 of the PRIMARY keyword, tokens split on `INV_KAD_KEYWORD_CHARS` keeping UTF-8 byte-len >= 3, and results kept only if every query word appears in the filename. None of the three existed. That section is headed "KEYWORD search (for completeness)"; flagged peripheral in research, read as optional at implementation. FIXED faithful to `CSearchManager::GetWords`, including two rules easy to miss (a repeat takes its LAST position; a trailing 3-char/3-byte token is dropped as a presumed extension) and one my own test caught in my implementation rather than the expectation: upstream ITERATES on empty tokens between delimiters, leaving uChars/uBytes at 0, so **a trailing delimiter SUPPRESSES the extension drop**. Verified live - `"Minister Prime"` now returns a full page, every hit carrying both words. **`"Yes Prime Minister"` still returns zero and the reason is NOT the fix:** the primary word "yes" is an enormous region sampled unfiltered, and eMule avoids that by sending the boolean search-expression tree (flags `0x8000`) so the STORING node filters before choosing what to return. padMule sends flags 0. That is the third piece, still marked "UNSURE - not byte-decoded" from Wave 6. **ALSO FOUND: padMule publishes NOTHING to Kad** - opcodes 0x43-0x45 undefined, no call site, so its shares are invisible to every other client searching Kad, and nothing recorded that as a deliberate deferral. The payload layouts were decoded from `CSearch::StorePacket` and written into [[kad-routing-lifecycle]] so the research gap is closed, but the feature was deliberately NOT half-built: codecs without a publish driver would be dead code, which is precisely row 8by's `build_out_of_part_reqs` mistake. **THE CORRECTION THAT MATTERED MOST was to my own writing hours earlier:** the 8cd memory said "keyword-search quality is a DIRECT function of routing-table breadth - if it looks weak, check `contacts_known()` before suspecting the search path", which points the next session away from where the bug actually was. Corrected in place. **An audit of the PLUMBING is not an audit of the FEATURE** - the 2026-08-06 pass verified the routing table, contact counts, maintenance timers, nodes.dat selection and the bootstrap, and never ran one keyword search. Gate: 637 tests, clippy -D warnings, fmt + ASCII clean.

- 2026-08-07 **The expression tree: two thirds of a spec is not a fix** (row 8cf). After the keyword TOKENISER landed, `"Yes Prime Minister"` still returned zero - and the reason was the third piece, not the first two. One word goes on the wire, so the storing node returns a bounded sample of everything indexed under it; for a common primary word ("yes") that sample is drawn from an enormous pool and the wanted file is never in it. **Local filtering cannot recover what was never sampled.** eMule sets a restrictive bit and attaches a boolean expression so the node filters BEFORE choosing what to send. Wave 6 had marked `CreateSearchExpressionTree` "UNSURE - not byte-decoded"; decoded here from BOTH sides of upstream (writer `SearchResultsWnd.cpp:895-920`, decoder `KademliaUDPListener.cpp`) and pinned by wire tests: `target 16 | (start_pos | 0x8000) u16 | expression`, PREFIX order, `0x00` boolean + op byte, `0x01` u16-length UTF-8 string, depth limit 24 beyond which the far end discards the WHOLE expression, and the flag riding in the TOP BIT of the start-position word rather than a field of its own. Result: the search returns a full page of exactly that series. **TWO SELF-INFLICTED TESTING LESSONS.** (1) The depth test counted `0x00` bytes to measure chain depth - which also match the high byte of every u16 string length - so it asserted nothing; replaced with a structural decoder that walks the prefix encoding the way upstream does. (2) `clippy --all-targets` then caught a stale test call site that plain `cargo test` had reported as an EMPTY test count rather than a failure - **an empty test count is a compile failure wearing a disguise**, and the summing one-liner used all session would have shown "0 passed" as if it were merely uninteresting. Gate: 640 tests, clippy -D warnings, fmt + ASCII clean. Also answered from source, for Anthony's shared collection: renaming inside eMule DOES re-index in Kad (`SharedFilesCtrl.cpp:1063-1065` calls RemoveKeywords -> SetFileName -> AddKeywords), no deshare/reshare needed; the old keyword entries are not retracted but age out on the 24h clock, and the file hash never changes so the SOURCE publish is untouched. Rename inside eMule, never behind its back, or a rescan may re-hash large files.

- 2026-08-07 **The install path was in the wiki the whole time, and a stale MEMORY line overrode it.** `docs/wiki/ipad-usb-tooling.md` has said since 2026-08-02 that zsign + `pymobiledevice3 apps install` puts a build on the iPad "with no Sideloadly round trip" - and the [[padmule-ipa-delivery]] memory said the opposite: "the install itself is the one step [the agent] cannot" do. The memory won, because it is what loads every session while the wiki entry has to be opened. So Anthony was routed through the Sideloadly GUI, that round re-signed WebDriverAgent, stripped its nested `.xctest` signature, and produced an XCTest-103 dead end that cost most of an hour to diagnose and a full zsign rebuild to clear. **Anthony asked afterwards whether he needed Sideloadly at all; the honest answer was no, and it had been no for five days.** Both documents corrected: the wiki section PROMOTED from a footnote titled "a genuine win" to the entry's stated default with the Sideloadly-breaks-WDA consequence spelled out, and the memory's wrong claim replaced. **THE LESSON IS ABOUT PLACEMENT, NOT KNOWLEDGE:** a capability recorded at the bottom of a long entry under a soft heading reads as trivia, and a memory that contradicts it wins by default. When something changes what the DEFAULT WORKFLOW is, it belongs at the top of the entry AND in memory, or it will not be acted on. Also corrected while there: the 7-day clock is the PROFILE, not the cert (`TimeToLive: 7` read from the profile itself; the developer cert inside runs to 2027-07-16), so renewals are profile refreshes - live expiries are WDA 2026-08-10 and padMule 2026-08-14. And a near-miss recorded in the delivery memory: `gh run download` hit a pre-existing file, errored "file exists", and the plist read off the STALE artifact reported `92a8f45` - a two-day-old build one step from being delivered as current. Extract to a fresh directory; the sha-match check is what caught it. **DEVICE ACCEPTANCE, same session:** padMule 32f1d0e installed by the new path in ~30 seconds with WDA still answering `ready: True`, and a Kad search for "Yes Prime Minister" from the iPad over AirVPN returned **143 results with `server + kad` on every row** - zero on every build before tonight. The Kad work is now device-proven, not just protocol-proven.

- 2026-08-07 **TRACK 1 COMPLETE - the GUI slowness is MEASURED, and it has TWO stacked causes, only one of which I had suspected.** Anthony's complaint was "many operations seem too slow, noticeable in GUI updates to various statuses". Measured on device (build 32f1d0e, WebDriverAgent, over AirVPN): a keyword search takes **10.3s** submit-to-results; a "Refresh server list" with nothing else running takes **9.31s / 7.54s**; and the SAME refresh, tapped 1.9s into a search, takes **20.62s**. So a user action issued during a search waits roughly 12 extra seconds. **THE EARLIER MEASUREMENT COULD NOT SEE THIS AND I SAID SO AT THE TIME:** sampling the accessibility tree during a search showed it staying responsive at ~1s per fetch, but WDA renders the view hierarchy without touching the engine at all, so a frozen app painting stale data is indistinguishable from a healthy one. Firing a SECOND lock-taking operation and timing it is what actually measures the contention. **CAUSE 1 - the Rust engine mutex:** 25 of 42 FFI methods take it, and the 1s UI poll takes it five times (`heartbeat`, `state`, `kad_contacts`, `ip_filter_ranges`, `sharing_paused_for_ip_change`; only `server_info` is free). **CAUSE 2, and this is the one that changes the fix - the SWIFT SIDE IS SERIAL BY CONSTRUCTION:** `EngineModel.swift:376` is `DispatchQueue(label: "...engine")`, a SERIAL queue, and every engine call goes through `work.async`. **That serialises even the LOCK-FREE Rust methods.** So the lock-free work already done (row 8bq, and the fetch funnel's deliberately lock-free `fetch_report`/`reset_fetch_stats`) is partly defeated on the Swift side: a lock-free call still waits behind a 10s search on `work`. **Making more Rust methods lock-free would NOT have fixed this**, which is exactly the fix I would have reached for from the code read alone. **HONEST LIMIT: the two causes cannot be separated from outside the app** - either one alone produces this measurement. What IS provable without further measurement is that the Swift queue is serial by declaration, so it is sufficient on its own. **SECOND FINDING, independent of contention: the individual operations are slow on their own.** 10.3s for a search (`SEARCH_WAIT` caps at 20s and both arms are awaited) and 7.5-9.3s for a probe round (`PROBE_COLLECT_BUDGET` is 6s, held under the lock throughout). So there are two separable problems - operations that are individually slow, and serialisation that makes them compound - and fixing either alone leaves the other.

- 2026-08-07 **The status poll no longer waits behind a search** (row 8cg), and the lesson is that the OBVIOUS fix would have bought nothing. Anthony asked whether padMule does "proper multithreading" and whether the Swift/serial issue could be addressed. Researched it: the standard Swift answer - make the `DispatchQueue` concurrent, or use barriers, or move to actors - does not help here, because **four of the five engine calls the 1s UI poll makes were taking the RUST engine mutex**, so loosening the Swift queue just moves the pile-up one layer down. Checking what those four actually READ is what settled it: `state()` is a Copy enum field, `sharing_paused_for_ip_change()` a bool, `ip_filter_ranges()` an `Arc::len` - **three trivial field reads, each taking a lock that a search holds for up to twenty seconds.** Fixed on both sides: `StatusPub` publishes them as atomics through the existing `EngineHandles` (the lock-free pattern already serving downloads/transfer totals), and on the Swift side they move from `refresh()` - where they sat AFTER `heartbeat()` in one closure on the serial `work` queue - into `refreshFast()` on the lock-free queue. `refresh()` is now heartbeat-only and publishes nothing to the UI. **The test is the durable part:** it HOLDS the engine mutex on another thread for 3s and asserts the readers answer within 500ms, so it fails by TIMING rather than assertion - which is the only way this regression gets caught, since re-adding a lock breaks nothing functionally. Mutation-checked at 3.001s. **NOT fixed and deliberately separable:** the operations are individually slow (`SEARCH_WAIT` 20s with both arms awaited, `PROBE_COLLECT_BUDGET` 6s under the lock), so a search still takes ~10s; it just no longer freezes everything else. Unverified on device at time of writing - the honest check is re-running the refresh-during-search timing that read 20.62s.

- 2026-08-07 **Track 1's fix could not be verified from outside the app, so it got an instrument** (row 8cg annotation, commit f946e02). The obvious re-measurement - a "Refresh server list" tapped into a running search - read **16.37s** against 20.62s before, and that proves nothing: it measures operation-vs-operation contention, which 8cg deliberately did not fix, and the difference is inside the run-to-run variance of search populations and probe rounds. **Saying so BEFORE running it, rather than explaining it afterwards, is the part worth keeping** - the prediction was written down first and the number then matched it. The deeper problem: **every probe available from outside padMule needs the engine lock itself**, so none of them can observe whether the lock-free status poll keeps running while the lock is held. Instrumented instead: a PAIR of counters under Stats -> "UI responsiveness", `uiPollTicks` (bumped where the lock-free `refreshFast` publishes) and `heartbeatTicks` (where the lock-taking `refresh` completes). **The pairing is the design, not decoration:** during a search the first must climb at ~1/s while the second stalls, so the instrument carries its own control and no old build is needed for comparison - the fetch funnel's adjacent-stage-drop shape applied to the UI. Falsifier fixed in advance: if BOTH stall, the Swift move did not achieve what 8cg claims, and the Rust lock test was insufficient evidence (it proves the READERS do not block, not that the publish CYCLE keeps running). NOTE this is the third instrument of the session after two that were VACUOUS - the one-measurement test that passed with its own fix reverted, and the depth test that counted `0x00` bytes and asserted nothing - so it gets checked for its ability to FAIL before its output is believed.

- 2026-08-07 **TRACK 1 CLOSED, and the root cause of the confusion was MY OWN PROBE.** The paired counter (f946e02) was read three times and the first reading REFUTED the fix: both counters flat at 0 for ~10s during a search, then a burst of +11. Every FFI call in that path is lock-free, so the block had to be Swift-side - and it was, but not in padMule. **WDA's `GET /source` walks the entire view hierarchy ON THE MAIN THREAD**, it was costing 1.4-2.4s per call, and polling it once a second starved exactly the main-thread publish blocks the counters increment in. The instrument was fine; reading it caused the freeze I was attributing to the app. **Re-measured with NO reads inside the window** (record, wait, record once): during a real search the lock-free poll runs at **0.94/s** against an idle **1.2/s**, while the lock-taking heartbeat drops to **0.69/s**, losing ~10s of ticks. The two diverge exactly as row 8cg predicts, so the fix is confirmed on device. **A SECOND SELF-INFLICTED ERROR on the way, caught by Anthony not by me:** the WDA search field CONCATENATES - a documented gotcha in [[ipad-usb-tooling]] - so a re-run silently searched `ministerminister`, which returns nothing and completes fast, producing a measurement that looked clean precisely because it was meaningless. The helper now CLEARS and READS THE FIELD BACK before submitting and aborts on mismatch, rather than relying on remembering the rule. **THE GENERAL LESSON, and it is the third observer-effect this project has hit:** the earlier two were about WDA SESSIONS disturbing the app; this one is `/source` itself. A read that costs main-thread time is not a passive observation. When measuring anything main-thread-bound, take the measurement OUT of the window - record, leave, record - rather than sampling through it. Also note the shape: the first reading refuted the fix, and the refutation was wrong. **A failed verification deserves the same scepticism as a successful one.**

- 2026-08-07 **Reanalysis + THE SLOW OPERATIONS (row 8ch).** A full reacquaintance
  pass over every crate, the iOS app and all 33 wiki files, then the fix the
  handoff nominated as "the biggest remaining user-facing win". Gate verified
  first, not quoted: 641 tests, clippy `-D warnings`, fmt clean. **THE CAUSE OF
  THE 10.3s SEARCH, which no prior session had looked at:** `KadNode::
  resolve_keyword` awaited its `ALPHA_QUERY` queries one at a time - alpha is a
  CONCURRENCY parameter (its own doc comment says so) and padMule used it as a
  batch size - making the lookup structurally 12x3x750ms before the keyword phase
  even started, so `KAD_SEARCH_WAIT` was not a bound but the cost. Same in
  `resolve_sources`, and in `refresh_routing` whose 9s worst case under a 3s
  deadline meant Kad maintenance was cut off after ~4 of its 12 queries every
  time. ONE UDP socket is why it was serial (`recv_from` gives a datagram to one
  waiter, and the old loop discarded anything not from its own destination, so
  concurrent requests would eat each other's replies); `request_batch` is one
  receive loop owning the whole batch, matching on the exact address with the old
  IP-only rule as fallback. Wire-identical, and closer to eMule. **THREE MORE
  FOUND BY THE PASS RATHER THAN REPORTED:** `server_info()` was the fifth status
  reader row 8cg claimed to have made lock-free and did not, so `refreshFast()`
  and the event drain sharing its queue still stalled for every search;
  `heartbeat()` held the engine lock across all eight maintainers in one
  acquisition (up to ~10s, and every user action waited behind all of it), now one
  lock per duty; the server probe always spent its full 6s budget with no early
  exit, now stopping when every server has answered or 2s after the last answer.
  **AND ONE INSTRUMENT CORRECTED:** 8cg's `uiPollTicks`/`heartbeatTicks` pair
  cannot answer its own question - the queue is serial, so a blocked poll defers
  its ticks rather than losing them and bursts on release, giving the same
  cumulative total as no stall at all; the device reading drawn from it is
  therefore not evidence. Added Stats -> "Longest poll gap", which a burst cannot
  hide. All five new tests mutation-checked before being believed (re-serialise
  the batch -> 1.201s; drop the exact-address match -> two peers swap answers;
  delete `note_responder` -> both key-capture tests fail; restore
  `inner.lock()` on `server_info` -> 3.001s; restore the probe deadline ->
  6.002s), and the two key-capture tests were REWRITTEN to drive the public
  lookup paths rather than the helpers, since the capture moved to the callers and
  a helper-level test would have passed with the production call site deleted.
  Gate after: 644 tests, clippy `-D warnings`, fmt + ASCII clean. NOT measured on
  device - structural plus tests only; the device timing pass is the top next
  action ([[handoff-next-session]]).

- 2026-08-07 **DEVICE PASS on row 8ch (build 7d1b349).** Committed 7d1b349, pushed,
  dispatched BOTH CI workflows on that sha - `iOS build` and, by
  `workflow_dispatch`, the `Rust unit gate` that this branch normally never gets -
  and both passed. Artifact verified as that commit twice over (run `headSha` ==
  local HEAD, and `CFBundleVersion: 7d1b349` read from the Info.plist inside a
  FRESH extraction), delivered to Windows Downloads, staged for zsign, and the
  stale `padmule-signed.ipa` from f946e02 deleted so it could not be installed by
  accident. Anthony signed; installed with `pymobiledevice3` in ~30s; **WDA
  survived**, as the zsign path promises. Build confirmed the only honest way:
  Settings > This device > Build = `1.0 (7d1b349)`. **RESULTS, on eMule Sunrise
  with HighID: search submit-to-results 4.58-6.38s over nine runs against a 10.3s
  baseline; Refresh server list 2.82/2.84/2.86s against 7.5-9.31s; Longest poll
  gap 1.1s** across all of it. Kad still contributes (`server + kad` rows, and
  "hedda hopper" split 2 kad / 2 server), so the speedup is not an arm being
  dropped. The residual ~6.3s is the Kad arm at round-count x RTT, which is what a
  Kademlia lookup costs - the next lever is fewer rounds or overlapping the
  keyword phase with the lookup, not another constant. **ATTRIBUTION STATED
  UNEVENLY ON PURPOSE:** Refresh is mechanism-matched and the poll gap is measured
  inside the app, so neither depends on the probe; the SEARCH comparison is weaker
  because the baseline's probe cadence is unknown and a cheaper probe biases the
  new number down - the A/B against f946e02 would settle it and was not run.
  **THREE PROBES WERE WRONG BEFORE THEY WERE RIGHT, each with a plausible number
  first:** the results list is not cleared between searches so the probe fired on
  the PREVIOUS run's rows and reported 1.3-1.5s of pure probe latency; a `srcs`
  matcher reported NO RESULTS by 46s while four results sat on screen, because a
  single-source row reads `1 src`; and the probes themselves were timed before
  being trusted (`/source` 1.70s, element query 0.53s, screenshot 2.13s) so the
  cheapest was used. All three are now written into [[handoff-next-session]]'s
  measurement rules, and `scripts/device-timing.sh` encodes them.

- 2026-08-07 **Kad lookup diagnostic, the device pass on 19d06d0, and a claim of
  mine deleted.** Added `stats::kad_report` (rounds run, rounds held open by a
  peer that never answered, requests sent/answered, avg round and value window),
  instrumented at the three batch functions, exported as `MuleEngine::kad_report`
  and shown as Stats -> "Kad lookup"; `mule-cli kad-keyword` prints the same
  block. **It corrected my model on its first reading, twice:** a lookup runs 5-6
  rounds, not 12 - the `0..12` bound is a safety cap the frontier never reaches -
  so the earlier "12 rounds x RTT matches the residual" was agreement between two
  wrong numbers, and it is DELETED from the handoff rather than softened. And a
  15% silence rate poisons ~45% of rounds, because a round is only as fast as its
  slowest member: `1 - 0.85^3 = 38.6%` predicted, 40-50% observed. **On device it
  is worse and that is the reading that decides the CSearch question:** 62% of
  rounds and 87% of value windows held open by a silent peer, a 67% answer rate
  against the dev box's 85%, and an average round burning 633ms of its 750ms cap.
  Nearly every round runs to the deadline, so the barrier IS the remaining cost.
  **The instrument also paid for itself immediately:** searches on 19d06d0 read
  7.27/8.13/7.42s against 4.58-6.38s for 7d1b349 the same day, which looks like a
  regression - `git diff` over the Kad path between those commits is counter
  increments and a timer and nothing else, so it was the swarm answering 67%
  instead of 85%. A day of hunting avoided. Also: the search A/B was settled
  OFF-DEVICE with `mule-cli kad-keyword` (alternating old/new binaries, no probe,
  no UI) at median **-25%**, not the 3x the worst-case table implies - recorded,
  and the worst case relabelled as a worst case. Refresh 1.66/2.77/1.67s, poll
  gap 1.1s again. **VPN badge fixed** - it rendered as ". . .   . . ." because a
  ToolbarItem compresses its content below ideal size; `fixedSize` + `lineLimit`.
  The accessibility label was correct throughout, which is why only Anthony's eye
  caught it. **And a gap nothing had recorded:** padMule ANSWERS nothing on Kad -
  three production socket call sites, the only `recv_from` inside the reply
  collection, no listener and no request opcode handled - so it stores nothing
  (Anthony's eMule on the Acer carries 7,700+ indexed entries), answers no
  ping, and ages out of other clients' routing tables. Its fix needs the same
  restructure as the CSearch design, so the two are now flagged to be designed
  together.

- 2026-08-07 **The VPN badge, and why CI now renders views (row 8ci).** Anthony
  saw the badge reading ". . .   . . ." on glass. Nothing automated could have:
  every UI assertion padMule has runs against the accessibility tree, and the
  tree read "VPN on" correctly the whole time - the label was right while the
  screen was wrong. Cause: a `ToolbarItem` proposes its content a width smaller
  than ideal, and two short `Text`s get squeezed to nothing; `.lineLimit(1)
  .fixedSize()` refuses that. `VpnBadge` extracted from `ContentView` into its
  own `View` taking `on: Bool` - not tidiness, but because as a private property
  reading `model.vpnActive` the OFF state could only be seen with a tunnel
  actually down on a device, which is why it had never been looked at. **The
  lesson kept is why the obvious test would have been vacuous:** `fixedSize()`
  only acts when the parent proposes less than ideal, so a lone snapshot passes
  with the fix DELETED. The test therefore MAKES a proposal
  (`ImageRenderer.proposedSize`, the same mechanism a ToolbarItem uses) and
  asserts the badge still takes its ideal width - reproducing the squeeze rather
  than imitating it. Plus a record with no assertion at all: both states attached
  as PNGs, and one inside a real toolbar, uploaded by `ios-test.yml`. CI green on
  1605c9a (27 tests, 0 failures) and the OFF state was seen for the FIRST time in
  that artifact. Device-verified separately by screenshot on 48b5128.

- 2026-08-07 **Always-on background running REOPENED (Anthony).** The
  foreground-only posture, made "permanent" on 2026-08-04, is back on the table.
  No new research was needed and none was done: [[lifecycle-and-reactivation]]
  already ranks the four mechanisms, and the headline is that there is no
  backdoor to look for - the `audio` `UIBackgroundModes` key is a plain Info.plist
  key a FREE team may set, forbidden only by App Store review 2.5.4, which never
  applies to a build that is never reviewed. Sideloading is what makes the
  ORDINARY mechanism available. Annotated in place rather than rewritten. What
  the entry did NOT have, added now: jetsam TERMINATION rather than suspension is
  the overnight failure mode, so background memory under ~100MB is the real
  target and has never been measured on any build; and the 1s heartbeat is a
  `Timer` on the MAIN RUNLOOP owned by the UI, driving seven duties that fail
  silently if it stops - it would still fire under keepalive, but that clock
  belongs in Rust before any background posture rests on it. Clean pause/resume
  stays required either way, because every keepalive can be revoked or killed.

- 2026-08-07 **SPEC written: one owning Kad read loop** (routing serve +
  event-driven CSearch lookup), at
  `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-07-kad-owning-read-loop-design.md`. Both changes
  have the same root cause - nothing owns the socket - so they are specified
  together and built in two verifiable steps rather than restructuring twice.
  Decisions taken with Anthony: serve scope is ROUTING ANSWERS ONLY (no index, no
  storage: foreground-only makes stateless answering a pure win, while storing
  other clients' keywords and then vanishing on a background is arguably worse
  for the network than not storing); sequencing is serve FIRST, because its
  success test is external and unambiguous - does a real amuled keep padMule in
  its routing table across a ping cycle - leaving the lookup change as pure
  policy on proven plumbing. Three calls approved: answer an EMPTY SEARCH_RES
  rather than staying silent (a deliberate deviation, justified by the 62%-silent
  cost we measured on ourselves, recorded as a deviation regardless);
  `Arc<Mutex<RoutingTable>>` now rather than moving lookups into the actor; and
  the Kad panel is reshaped IN the same change as step 2, because "rounds" stops
  existing and a panel that reads plausibly while meaning nothing is the failure
  this project keeps catching. Self-reviewed: no placeholders, and every code
  citation checked - `Search.cpp:281/336/347` resolve, and the `OnSmallTimer`
  path was WRONG in the first draft (`kademlia/kademlia/` rather than
  `kademlia/routing/`) and is corrected. Not started; awaiting Anthony's read of
  the spec.

- 2026-08-07 **The autonomy loop closed, and the background-memory gate MEASURED.**
  Anthony granted the signing key to the agent explicitly, reversing the
  "Anthony runs zsign himself" rule recorded in [[ipad-usb-tooling]] and
  [[padmule-ipa-delivery]]. `scripts/ship.sh` is the closed loop that follows:

  **THE MEASUREMENT THAT MATTERS:** padMule's `physFootprint` on device is

  Also found: **padMule had been UNINSTALLED from the device** at some point

  FBSOpenApplicationServiceErrorDomain Code=4, and the apps list confirmed the

  on disk. Worth knowing that a launch failure can mean "not installed" rather
  than "broken build", and that the apps list settles it in one call.

- 2026-08-07 **BACKGROUND SEEDING BUILT (row 8cj)**, the same day the decision was
  reopened. Anthony's ask was specific and better-shaped than "always on": keep
  SHARING what has been downloaded, to be a good neighbour and earn standing.
  Measured before building - `physFootprint` 30.8 MB against the ~100MB jetsam
  budget - which is what turned this from risky to obviously worth doing. The
  mechanism is the ordinary `audio` `UIBackgroundModes` key; sideloading is what
  makes it available (review 2.5.4 is the only thing that normally forbids it),
  so there is no backdoor and no private API. `EngineState::Seeding` keeps the
  listener and server login up and drops Kad; the maintainers that should keep
  running have no `Running` guard and continue for free, the ones that should
  stop already gate on it. A separate STATE rather than a flag, because
  "Running" while backgrounded is the dishonest status this entry calls a hard
  requirement to avoid. **The keepalive decides the path, not the setting** - it
  is started first and a failure falls back to a plain pause, because claiming a
  seed we are not performing is the same dishonesty in a different place.
  Mutation-checked, and the first listener test was VACUOUS (offline `start()`
  never binds a listener, so it asserted about one that never existed) and said
  so by failing. Default OFF; footer blunt about jetsam, because the failure mode
  is a user who believes it is a guarantee. Not yet device-verified: longevity
  overnight is the open question.

- 2026-08-07 **Two ships raced and BOTH lost their install.** Running
  `scripts/ship.sh` twice with an overlap - my error - had both runs reach the
  install stage, and iOS answers a second concurrent installation with
  "Coordinator superseded": the first dies, and the second died too. The device
  reported `CFBundleVersion 48b5128` afterwards, i.e. NEITHER new build landed,
  which is exactly the failure a "signed OK" line would have hidden if the loop
  had not read the installed version back. The script now takes an flock and
  refuses to start while another ship holds it - the device is a single resource
  and the lock says so. Also worth remembering: a hanging `apps install` after
  such a collision is not a broken build; `pymobiledevice3 apps list` gives the
  installed CFBundleVersion in one call and settles what is actually on there.

- 2026-08-07 **Background seeding DEVICE-VERIFIED - and the first result was a
  wrong TEST, not a failed feature.** With the toggle on, padMule backgrounded
  stayed ALIVE at 32.2 MB / ~7% CPU, where before the change the process ended.
  But the first four samples read DEAD, and the cause was the method:
  **creating a WDA session for a DIFFERENT bundle TERMINATES the previous app**
  rather than backgrounding it. [[ipad-usb-tooling]] warned that creating a
  session disturbs the app; it did not say that a session for another bundle
  kills the old one. Re-run with `pymobiledevice3 developer dvt launch`, which
  does not touch WDA, and it survives - confirmed by screenshot (Files
  foreground with the `padMule` back-indicator, process alive behind it). That
  is twice in one feature that a wrong instrument nearly wrote a false verdict
  into the record: the other was a Settings footer whose SECOND paragraph never
  rendered, because a Section footer built from two sibling Texts shows only the
  first - source and accessibility tree both disagreed with the screen, so only
  a screenshot could find it. **Open: ~7% CPU while serving nobody** (foreground
  idle is 0.1%), so the keepalive plus the 1s heartbeat is not free and is the
  next battery question; and longevity past a minute, for which a soak is
  running.
